W^{1,1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
and the formula:
2\mu\varrho_{r}=\eta_{r}\varrho(V-U)
, we obtain the
L^{1}([0,T];L^{\infty})
-estimate for
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\varrho^{\gamma-1}U
from (
3.5
), (
3.12
), and the uniform lower bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
. This, along with (
3.11
), leads to the weighted
L^{\infty}
-estimate for
V
in Lemma
6.3
:

(3.13)

|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Near the origin, we first establish some special weighted
L^{p}
-estimates for
U
. Comparing with the calculation in (
3.8
) in the proof of (
3.5
) (the classical
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
U
), we instead multiply
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}|U|^{p-2}U
for
p\geq 2
. Then, utilizing the equation:
2\mu\varrho_{r}=\varrho(V-U)
and the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
\zeta V
, together with the uniform lower bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
, we obtain in Lemma
6.4
that, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}+1\big).

Subsequently, using a similar argument in deriving the
L^{1}([0,T];L^{\infty})
-estimate for
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\varrho^{\gamma-1}U
, we can obtain from the above with suitable fixed
k>3
that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\!\!\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\!|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{k}}\!+\big(1+\!\!\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\!|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{k}}\big)\!\int_{0}^{t}\!\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}\!D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{k}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\varepsilon\!\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty},

which, along with (
3.11
), leads to the
L^{\infty}
-estimate for
\zeta V
in Lemma
6.6
:

(3.14)

|\zeta V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

3.2.4.
Global uniform upper bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
and lower bound of the density inside the fluids

To obtain the global uniform upper bounds for
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
, our main objective is to establish the following two types of estimates for
\log\varrho
: for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(3.15)

|(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{K}\log\varrho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(K,T)\ \ \text{for some large $K>0$},\qquad|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}\log\varrho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T).

Formally, together with (
2.1
), the above estimates imply that
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
do not develop singularities inside the interval
(0,1)
. Since
\eta_{r}|_{r=1}
on
[0,T]
, we can thus derive the uniform upper bounds for
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
near and away from the origin in Lemmas
7.5
and
7.7
, respectively. Clearly, the lower bound of
\varrho
inside the fluids then follows directly from expression (
2.1
) for
\varrho
.

The proofs of
\eqref{39'}_{1}
and
\eqref{39'}_{2}
are essentially the same; we take
\eqref{39'}_{1}
as an example. Generally, establishing
\eqref{39'}_{1}
relies on the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
and estimates of
V
. A direct calculation yields the inequality:

|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{K}\log\varrho|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C(T)+C_{0}\underline{\big|(\zeta^{\sharp})^{4}\rho_{0}^{2K}\eta_{r}\log\varrho(V,U)\big|_{1}}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{\star}}.

Here, handling the factor
\eta_{r}
in
\mathrm{I}_{\star}
is particularly intricate, since we do not have the
a priori
upper bound for
\eta_{r}
. In fact, we find that
\mathrm{I}_{\star}
can be treated effectively by distributing
\eta_{r}
,
i.e.
,

\mathrm{I}_{\star}\leq C_{0}\big|(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}\log\varrho\big|_{2}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}(V,U)\big|_{2},

which involves some unconventional
\sqrt{\eta_{r}}
-weighted estimates for
(\log\varrho,U,V)
.

To this end, we begin by estimating
(U,V)
. Let
(M,N)
denote generic positive constants. Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
and (
4.2
) by
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\eta_{r}\rho_{0}^{2M}\varrho^{-1}U
and
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{2N}\eta_{r}V
, respectively, and integrating over
I
, then we derive estimates of the form:

(3.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{M}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}U\big|_{2}^{2}+\mu\Big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{M}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq C(M,T)\big(1+\big|(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{2M}V_{r}\big|_{q_{*}}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{2M-\beta}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}V\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{M}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}U\big|_{2}^{2}\big),\end{aligned}

(3.17)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{N}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}V\big|_{2}\leq C(N,T)\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{N+\beta}{2}}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}(U,D_{\eta}U)\big|_{2},

where
q_{*}\in(1,2]
is a special parameter defined in (
7.1
). To close these estimates, we further derive the weighted
L^{q_{*}}
-estimate for
V_{r}
from (
4.2
), which gives

(3.18)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{N+\beta}V_{r}|_{q_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C(N,T)\big(\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{N}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}V\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{N+\beta}{2}}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}(U,D_{\eta}U)\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C(N,T)\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{N+\beta}{2}}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{N+\beta}{2}}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Thus, collecting (
3.16
)–(
3.18
) by letting
N=2M-\beta
and choosing a suitable
M
, we arrive at the desired weighted estimates for
(U,V)
:

(3.19)

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{M}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}U(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{2M-\beta}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}V(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(M,T)\qquad\text{for some $M>0$}.

The detailed calculations of (
3.19
) can be found in Lemmas
7.3
–
7.4
.

Finally, multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{1}
by
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{4}\rho_{0}^{2K}\varrho^{-1}\log\varrho
and integrating over
I
, we obtain the
L^{2}
-estimate for
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\rho_{0}^{K}\sqrt{\eta_{r}}\log\varrho
from (
3.19
) with sufficiently large
K
, thereby obtaining
\eqref{39'}_{1}
.

3.2.5.
Global weighted energy estimates for the velocity

For establishing the global weighted energy estimates, we first establish in §
9.1.2
–§
9.1.4
all tangential estimates:

(3.20)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}^{k}U,D_{\eta}\partial_{t}^{k}U,\frac{\partial_{t}^{k}U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for $k=0,1$},

alongside the second- and third-order interior elliptic estimates and
W^{1,\infty}
-estimates for
U
:

(3.21)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}^{2}U,D_{\eta}(\frac{U}{\eta}),D_{\eta}^{3}U,D_{\eta}^{2}(\frac{U}{\eta}),\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}(\frac{U}{\eta})\big)\Big|_{2}+\Big|\big(U,D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big)\leq C(T).

Note that the calculations in (
3.20
)–(
3.21
) rely on the global uniform upper and lower bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
and the estimates on
V
given in (
3.12
)–(
3.14
).

Afterward, in §
9.2
–§
9.4
, we derive the second- and third-order exterior elliptic estimates for
U
and the
L^{1}(0,T)
-estimate and time-weighted estimate for
\mathcal{D}(t,U)
. Here, we take the
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
-estimates for
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{4}U
as an example, and let
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)\in L^{1}(0,T)
, and
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{2}U_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
. From (
3.20
)–(
3.21
) and these assumptions, we can show that the crossing term
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{cross}}:=(D_{\eta}^{3}U)_{r}+(\beta^{-1}+2)\rho_{0}^{-\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}D_{\eta}^{3}U
satisfies a Grönwall-type inequality:

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}\leq C(T)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{4}U\big|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}+1\Big).

Then Proposition
D.1
in Appendix
D
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
give

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{4}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{3}U\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{4}U\big|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)\Big),

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, leads to the desired estimate.

4.
Global-In-Time Uniform Upper Bound of the Density

The purpose of this section is to establish the global-in-time upper bound of density
\varrho
.
For simplicity, we first define a solution class
D(T)
as follows:

Definition 4.1
.

Let
T>0
,
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
, and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. For
IBVP
(
2.2
), a solution
(U,\eta)(t,r)
is said to be in the class
D(T)
if the following conditions hold
:

•

(U,\eta)(t,r)
is a classical solution of
IBVP
(
2.2
) in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
defined by
Definition
2.1
;

•

the boundary conditions in (
2.13
) hold
:

U|_{r=0}=U_{r}|_{r=1}=0\qquad\text{on $(0,T]$};

•

the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
satisfy

u_{0}\in C^{1}(\bar{I}),\qquad(1-r)\sim\rho_{0}^{\beta}\in C^{1}(\bar{I})\cap C^{2}((0,1])\quad\text{for some $\beta\in(0,\gamma-1]$}.

Such local well-posedness in the class
D(T)
has been established in Theorem
3.1
when
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\gamma-1]
and
\gamma\in(\frac{4}{3},\infty)
in two and three spatial dimensions.
Nevertheless, it is important to observe that the key
a priori
estimates developed in
§
4
–§
8
— namely, the uniform upper bound of the density, the uniform lower and upper bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
,
the uniform estimates for the effective velocity,
and the lower bound of the density near the origin — in fact hold for general classical solutions in the class

D(T)
as defined in Definition
4.1
.
In a word, the regularity requirement
\mathcal{E}(0,U)<\infty
imposed in (
2.11
) is not required for the analysis carried out in §
4
–§
8
.

Therefore, throughout §
4
–§
8
, we always assume the following:

•

(\beta,\gamma)
satisfy

\beta\in(0,\gamma-1],\qquad\gamma\in(1,\infty)\ \ \text{if }n=2,\qquad\gamma\in(1,3)\ \ \text{if }n=3;

•

(U,\eta)(t,r)
is a classical solution in the class
D(T)
of
IBVP
(
2.2
) in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
for some
T>0
, as defined in Definition
4.1
;

•

C_{0}\in(1,\infty)
is a generic constant depending only on
(n,\mu,A,\gamma,\beta,\varepsilon_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2})
, and
C(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k})\in(1,\infty)
is a generic constant depending on
C_{0}
and parameters
(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k})
,
which may be different at each occurrence,
i.e.
,

C_{0}=C_{0}(n,\mu,A,\gamma,\beta,\varepsilon_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}),\qquad C(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k})=C(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k},C_{0}).

Notice that, for this solution
(U,\eta)
, density
\varrho
can be given by (
2.1
), and
(\varrho,U,\eta)(t,r)
still solves problem (
1.16
) in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
.

Now we are ready to introduce the so-called effective velocity.

Definition 4.2
.

We say that
V
is the effective velocity if

(4.1)

V=U+2\mu\frac{\eta^{m}\varrho_{r}}{r^{m}\rho_{0}}=U+2\mu D_{\eta}\log\varrho.

Besides, we define the initial value of
V
as
v_{0}=V|_{t=0}=u_{0}+2\mu(\log\rho_{0})_{r}
.

Then we have

Proposition 4.1
.

The effective velocity
V
satisfies the equation
:

(4.2)

V_{t}+A\gamma\varrho^{\gamma-2}D_{\eta}\varrho=V_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\varrho^{\gamma-1}(V-U)=0,

and takes the form
:

(4.3)

\displaystyle V(t,r)

\displaystyle=v_{0}(r)\exp\Big(-\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{t}\varrho^{\gamma-1}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{t}(\varrho^{\gamma-1}U)(\tau,r)\exp\Big(-\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{\tau}^{t}\varrho^{\gamma-1}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Proof.

To derive (
4.2
), it follows from (
1.16
) and Definition
4.2
that

\displaystyle V_{t}

\displaystyle=U_{t}+2\mu(D_{\eta}\log\varrho)_{t}=U_{t}+2\mu D_{\eta}(\log\varrho)_{t}-2\mu(D_{\eta}U)(D_{\eta}\log\varrho)

\displaystyle=U_{t}-2\mu D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}U+\frac{mU}{\eta}\big)-2\mu D_{\eta}U\frac{D_{\eta}\varrho}{\varrho}

\displaystyle=U_{t}-\frac{2\mu}{\varrho}D_{\eta}\Big(\varrho\big(D_{\eta}U+\frac{mU}{\eta}\big)\Big)+\frac{2\mu}{\varrho}\frac{mD_{\eta}\varrho U}{\eta}

\displaystyle=-A\gamma\varrho^{\gamma-2}D_{\eta}\varrho=-\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\varrho^{\gamma-1}(V-U).

Then (
4.3
) can be directly obtained by solving ODE (
4.2
).
∎

4.1.
Some basic estimates

First, we have the fundamental energy estimate:

Lemma 4.1
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}(t)\big|_{1}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
2\eta^{m}\eta_{r}U
, together with
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{1}
and (
2.1
), gives

\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}U^{2}+\frac{2A}{\gamma-1}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}\big)_{t}+4\mu r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\eta}U|^{2}+\frac{mU^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\big)=\big(4\mu r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{UD_{\eta}U}{\eta_{r}}-2A\varrho^{\gamma}\eta^{m}U\big)_{r}.

Then integrating the above over
[0,t]\times I
with
t\in[0,T]
leads to the desired result.
∎

Next, the following
\eta
-weighted estimates hold for
\varrho
:

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.4)

|\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho(t)|_{1}+|(\eta^{m}\varrho)(t)|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\eta^{m}\eta_{r}
, along with
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{1}
, yields

(2\mu(\eta^{m}\varrho)_{r}+r^{m}\rho_{0}U-2\mu m\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho)_{t}=Am\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}-A(\eta^{m}\varrho^{\gamma})_{r}.

Then, for arbitrary
\tilde{r}\in[0,1)
, integrating the above with respect to
r
over
[\tilde{r},1]
, together with
\varrho|_{r=1}=0
and (
2.1
), gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(2\mu(\eta^{m}\varrho)(t,\tilde{r})-\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}U\,\mathrm{d}r+2\mu m\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle=-Am\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}r-A(\eta^{m}\varrho^{\gamma})(t,\tilde{r}),

which, along with the fact that
\eta_{r}\geq 0
, implies that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(2\mu(\eta^{m}\varrho)(t,\tilde{r})+2\mu m\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}U\,\mathrm{d}r.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
, we obtain from Lemma
4.1
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\eta^{m}\varrho(\cdot,\tilde{r})+\int_{\tilde{r}}^{1}\eta^{m-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|r^{m}\rho_{0}U|_{1}+|r^{m-1}\rho_{0}|_{1}+|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U\big|_{2}|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\big)\leq C_{0}.

Since
C_{0}
is independent of the choice of
\tilde{r}
, we derive the desired conclusion.
∎

Based on Lemma
4.2
, we derive the uniform lower bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
away from the origin.

Lemma 4.3
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>1
such that

\frac{\eta(t,r)}{r}\geq\frac{r^{m}}{C(T)},\quad\eta_{r}(t,r)\geq\frac{r^{m}}{C(T)}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

In particular, for any
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(a,T)>1
such that

\frac{\eta(t,r)}{r}\geq C(a,T)^{-1},\quad\eta_{r}(t,r)\geq C(a,T)^{-1}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[a,1]$}.

Proof.

First, it follows from Lemma
4.2
that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|(\eta^{m}\varrho)(t)|_{\infty}=\Big|\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta_{r}}(t)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0},

which implies

(4.5)

\eta_{r}(t,r)\geq\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}(r)}{C_{0}}\qquad\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

We now claim that, for any
T>0
, there exists a constant
C(T)
such that

(4.6)

\eta_{r}(t,r)\geq\frac{r^{m}}{C(T)}\qquad\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

Otherwise, there exist both
T>0
and a sequence of

\{(t_{k},r_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]\times\bar{I}
such that

(4.7)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\eta_{r}(t_{k},r_{k})}{r_{k}^{m}}=0.

This, together with (
4.5
), yields

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\rho_{0}(r_{k})}{C_{0}}\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\eta_{r}(t_{k},r_{k})}{r_{k}^{m}}=0,

which implies that
r_{k}\to 1
due to
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
. Moreover, since
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]
, we can extract a subsequence
\{t_{k_{\ell}}\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]
such that
t_{k_{\ell}}\to t_{0}
for some
t_{0}\in[0,T]
. Then

(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})\to(t_{0},1)\qquad\text{as}\ \ \ell\to\infty.

This, along with (
4.7
), leads to

\eta_{r}(t_{0},1)=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{\eta_{r}(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})}{r_{k_{\ell}}^{m}}=0.

However, this contradicts to the fact that
\eta_{r}|_{r=1}=1
for all
t\in[0,T]
since
U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
. Therefore,
we conclude claim (
4.6
).

Finally, thanks to
\eta|_{r=0}=0
, we obtain from (
4.6
) that

\frac{\eta(t,r)}{r}=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\eta_{r}(t,\tilde{r})\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\geq\frac{1}{C(T)r}\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{r}^{m}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\geq\frac{r^{m}}{C(T)}.

This completes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Interior BD entropy estimates

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(a,T)>0
such that

\big|(\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}V(t)\big|_{2}+\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(a,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$},

where the cut-off function
\zeta_{a}
is defined in
§
3.1.3
.

Proof.

Multiplying (
4.2
) by
\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0}V
and integrating over
I
give that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0}V^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+2\mu A\gamma\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma-2}|D_{\eta}\varrho|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-A\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\gamma})U\,\mathrm{d}r=-A\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}(\varrho^{\gamma})_{r}U\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=A\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\varrho^{\gamma}\eta_{r}D_{\eta}(\eta^{m}U)\,\mathrm{d}r+A\int_{0}^{1}(D_{\eta}\zeta_{a})\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}U\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{G}_{i}.

Then, for
\mathrm{G}_{1}
, it follows from (
2.1
) that

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{1}

\displaystyle=A\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\gamma}}(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\frac{A}{\gamma-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-\frac{A}{\gamma-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}r.

For
\mathrm{G}_{2}
, we obtain from (
2.1
), the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, Lemmas
4.1
and
4.3
, and the Hölder inequality that

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{2}

\displaystyle=A\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta_{a})_{r}\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}U}{\eta^{(\gamma-1)m}\eta_{r}^{\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(a,T)\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}|U|\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(a,T)\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(a,T)\Big(\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}U^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(a,T).

Consequently, substituting (
4.9
)–(
4.10
) into (
4.8
) and integrating the resulting inequality over
[0,t]
yield

(4.11)

\displaystyle\big|(\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}V(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}(t)\big|_{1}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\big|(\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}\big|_{1})+C(a,T)\leq C(a,T).

Here, to obtain the boundedness of the initial data, it suffices to note that

\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
and

|(\zeta_{a}r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log\rho_{0})_{r}|_{2}\leq C(a)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{2}\leq C(a).

Thanks to (
4.1
), we thus obtain from Lemma
4.1
and (
4.11
) that

(4.12)

\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(a,T),

which leads to the desired estimates.
∎

4.3.
Some new global
\eta
-weighted estimates for the density

Lemma 4.5
.

Let parameters
(q_{1},q_{2})
satisfy

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle\in(0,2],\quad q_{2}\in(0,1]

\displaystyle\text{if $n=2$};

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle\in[1,3],\quad\,q_{2}\in[1,2]

\displaystyle\text{if $n=3$}.

Then, for any
a\in(0,1)
and
(q_{1},q_{2})
defined above, there exist positive constants
C(a,q_{1},T)
and
C(a,q_{2},T)
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.13)

|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho(t)|_{1}\leq C(a,q_{1},T),\qquad|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}}\varrho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(a,q_{2},T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Case
n=2
(
m=1
).

First, for the first estimate in (
4.13
), if
q_{1}\in[1,2]
, it follows from Lemma
4.2
, (
2.1
), and the Hölder inequality that, for all
a\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.14)

|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}\leq|\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}\leq|\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{2-q_{1}}|\eta\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{q_{1}-1}=|\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{2-q_{1}}|r\rho_{0}|_{1}^{q_{1}-1}\leq C_{0}.

If
q_{1}\in(0,1)
, we claim that, for all
a\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.15)

|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}\leq|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+C(a,q_{1},T).

Indeed, since
\eta|_{r=0}=\varrho|_{r=1}=0
and
\eta,\eta_{r},\varrho\geq 0
, it follows from integration by parts, (
2.1
), the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, Lemmas
4.3
–
4.4
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}(\eta^{q_{1}})=-\frac{2}{q_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}}\sqrt{\varrho}(\sqrt{\varrho})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{q_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta_{a})_{r}\eta^{q_{1}}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{q_{1}}\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}\big|_{2}|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(a,q_{1})\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}\frac{r\rho_{0}}{\eta^{1-q_{1}}\eta_{r}}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(a,q_{1},T)\Big(|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}r(1-r)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle\leq|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+C(a,q_{1},T),

which implies claim (
4.15
).

Now, for each
q_{1}\in(0,1)
, there exists a fixed
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
depending only on
q_{1}
such that

0<2^{\ell-1}q_{1}<1<2^{\ell}q_{1}<2.

We can iteratively use (
4.15
) and then obtain from (
4.14
) that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.16)

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2^{\ell-1}q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}+C(a,q_{1},T)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2^{\ell}q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}+C(a,q_{1},T)\leq C(a,q_{1},T).

This completes the proof of
\eqref{fff}_{1}
.

Finally, we show
\eqref{fff}_{2}
. For any
q_{2}\in(0,1]
and
a\in(0,1)
, it follows from
\eqref{fff}_{1}
, Lemmas
4.3
–
4.4
, and
A.3
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}}\varrho|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}}\varrho)_{r}|_{1}\leq C_{0}\big|\big((\zeta_{a})_{r}\eta^{q_{2}}\varrho,q_{2}\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho,2\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}}\sqrt{\varrho}(\sqrt{\varrho})_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}\!\!\eta^{q_{2}}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r+C(q_{2})|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{2}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+C_{0}\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}\big|_{2}\big|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2q_{2}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(a,T)\big|\zeta_{\frac{1+3a}{4}}\eta^{q_{2}}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}+C(a,q_{2},T)\leq C(a,q_{2},T).

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.5
when
n=2

(m=1)
.

2. Case
n=3
(
m=2
).

First, we can obtain from integration by parts, Lemmas
4.2
–
4.4
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}\eta=-\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta_{a})_{r}\eta\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r-2\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{a}\eta\sqrt{\varrho}(\sqrt{\varrho})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}\eta\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r+2\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}\big|_{2}|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(a,T)\big(|\eta\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq C(a,T)\big(1+|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),

which, along with the Young inequality, yields

|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho(t)|_{1}\leq C(a,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

This, together with (
2.1
) and the Hölder inequality, yields that, for all
q_{1}\in[1,3]
,

|\zeta_{a}\eta^{q_{1}-1}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}\leq|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{3-q_{1}}{2}}|\zeta_{a}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{q_{1}-1}{2}}\leq|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{3-q_{1}}{2}}|r^{2}\rho_{0}|_{1}^{\frac{q_{1}-1}{2}}\leq C(a,T),

which implies
\eqref{fff}_{1}
.

Next, it follows from
\eqref{fff}_{1}
, Lemmas
4.2
–
4.4
, and
A.3
that

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\eta\varrho|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|(\zeta_{a}\eta\varrho)_{r}|_{1}\leq C_{0}\big|((\zeta_{a})_{r}\eta\varrho,\,\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho,\,\zeta_{a}\eta\sqrt{\varrho}(\sqrt{\varrho})_{r})\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\int_{a}^{\frac{1+3a}{4}}\eta\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+C_{0}\big|(\zeta_{a}\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\sqrt{\varrho}\big|_{2}|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(a,T)\big(|\eta\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+|\zeta_{a}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq C(a,T),

which, along with
|\eta^{2}\varrho|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}
in Lemma
4.2
, leads to
\eqref{fff}_{2}
.

This completes proof of Lemma
4.5
when
n=3
(
m=2
).
∎

4.4.
L^{p}
-estimates for
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}(U,V)

Our goal of this subsection is to establish the
L^{p}
-energy estimates (
p\in[2,\infty)
) for
(U,V)
, which can be stated as follows:

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
, and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.17)

\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(\zeta r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T),

where the cut-off function
\zeta
is defined in
§
3.1.3
.

The proof of Lemma
4.6
will be divided into the following three cases:

§
4.4.1
:

Case when
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
.

§
4.4.2
:

Case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
.

§
4.4.3
:

Case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
.

4.4.1.
Case when
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)

We first establish the
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
U
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that

\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

Let
n=2
,
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\eta\eta_{r}|U|^{p-2}U
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(r\rho_{0}|U|^{p})_{t}+2\mu(p-1)r\rho_{0}|U|^{p-2}|D_{\eta}U|^{2}+2\mu r\rho_{0}\frac{|U|^{p}}{\eta^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{2\mu r\rho_{0}}{\eta_{r}}|U|^{p-2}UD_{\eta}U-\frac{Ar^{\gamma}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}}{\eta^{\gamma-1}\eta_{r}^{\gamma}}|U|^{p-2}U\Big)_{r}+\frac{A(r\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{p-2}\big((p-1)D_{\eta}U+\frac{U}{\eta}\big).

Then integrating the above over
I
, along with the Hölder and Young inequalities, yields

(4.18)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+2\mu\Big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\frac{A(r\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{p-2}\big((p-1)D_{\eta}U+\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big|\frac{(r\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Big|_{2}\Big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\underline{\Big|\frac{(r\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathrm{G}_{3}}+\mu\Big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Now, we estimate
\mathrm{G}_{3}
. Let
\iota_{0}
be a fixed constant such that

0<\iota_{0}<\min\{2,p\gamma-p\}.

Then it follows from (
2.1
), the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, Lemmas
4.3
and
4.5
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(r\rho_{0})^{2\gamma-1}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{2\gamma-2}}|U|^{p-2}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p-2}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(r\rho_{0})^{p\gamma-p+1}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{p\gamma-p}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle=\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p-2}\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{p\gamma-p+1}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{1}\frac{(r\rho_{0})^{p\gamma-p+1}}{(\eta\eta_{r})^{p\gamma-p}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|\zeta\eta^{\frac{\iota_{0}}{p\gamma-p}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{p\gamma-p}|\zeta\eta^{1-\iota_{0}}\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}+C(p,T)\leq\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,T),

where the cut-off function
\zeta
is defined in §
3.1.3
.

Substituting (
4.19
) into (
4.18
) and then applying the Grönwall inequality to the resulting inequality yield the desired result.
∎

Based on Lemma
4.7
, we obtain the following interior
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
V
:

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V(t)\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p,T).

Proof.

Let
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
. We first restrict parameter
p
such that

(4.20)

\max\big\{2,\frac{2}{\gamma-1}\big\}\leq p<\infty.

Multiplying (
4.2
) by
\zeta r\rho_{0}|V|^{p-2}V
, together with (
2.1
), gives

\frac{1}{p}(\zeta r\rho_{0}|V|^{p})_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p-2}VU.

Then, integrating the above over
I
, we obtain from the fact that

\zeta^{k}(r)\leq\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}(r)\qquad\text{for all $r\in\bar{I}$ and $k>0$},

Lemma
4.5
, and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p-2}VU\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(\zeta\eta\eta_{r}\varrho^{p\gamma-p+1})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}\eta^{\frac{2}{p\gamma-p}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{p\gamma-p}\Big|\big(\frac{r\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\Big|\big(\frac{r\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p},

which, along with Lemma
4.7
and the Grönwall inequality, yields that, for any
p
satisfying (
4.20
),

(4.21)

\big|(\zeta r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Finally, we obtain from Lemma
4.4
and interpolation that (
4.21
) holds for all
p\in[2,\infty)
.
∎

Now we can prove Lemma
4.6
for the case when
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Combining Lemmas
4.7
–
4.8
, we derive the desired estimates of Lemma
4.6
when
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
.
∎

4.4.2.
Case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]

First, we establish the
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
U
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Let
n=3
,
\gamma\in(1,2]
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\eta^{2}\eta_{r}|U|^{p-2}U
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(r^{2}\rho_{0}|U|^{p})_{t}+2\mu(p-1)r^{2}\rho_{0}|U|^{p-2}|D_{\eta}U|^{2}+4\mu r^{2}\rho_{0}\frac{|U|^{p}}{\eta^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\mu\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta_{r}}|U|^{p-2}UD_{\eta}U-\frac{Ar^{2\gamma}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}}{\eta^{2(\gamma-1)}\eta_{r}^{\gamma}}|U|^{p-2}U\Big)_{r}+\frac{A(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{p-2}\big((p-1)D_{\eta}U+\frac{2U}{\eta}\big).

Then integrating the above over
I
, along with the Hölder and Young inequalities, yields

(4.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+2\mu\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\frac{A(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\gamma}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{p-2}\big((p-1)D_{\eta}U+\frac{2U}{\eta}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big|\frac{(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Big|_{2}\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\underline{\Big|\frac{(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathrm{G}_{4}}+\mu\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

2.
To estimate
\mathrm{G}_{4}
, we can first obtain from (
2.1
), the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, Lemma
4.3
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{2\gamma-1}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{2\gamma-2}}|U|^{p-2}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{G}_{4,1}}+\Big(\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{1}\frac{(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{p\gamma-p+1}}{(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{p\gamma-p}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{G}_{4,1}+C(p,T)\Big(\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{1}(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{p\gamma-p+1}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{G}_{4,1}+\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,T).

For
\mathrm{G}_{4,1}
, it follows from (
2.1
), Lemma
4.5
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for any
\vartheta\in[0,1]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{4,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{2\gamma-1-\frac{p-2}{p}}}{\eta^{4\gamma-4-\frac{\vartheta(2p-4)}{p}}\eta_{r}^{2\gamma-2}}\Big(\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{p-2}{p}}|U|^{p-2}\Big)^{\vartheta}\Big((r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{p-2}{p}}|U|^{p-2}\Big)^{1-\vartheta}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{2+\vartheta(p-2)}\eta_{r}\varrho^{p\gamma-p+1}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{\vartheta(p-2)}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{(1-\vartheta)(p-2)}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\zeta\eta^{\frac{2+\vartheta(p-2)}{p\gamma-p}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}\big|\zeta\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}^{\frac{2}{p}}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{\vartheta(p-2)}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{(1-\vartheta)(p-2)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,p,T)\big|\zeta\eta^{\frac{2+\vartheta(p-2)}{p\gamma-p}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{p\gamma-p}+\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{\vartheta p}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{(1-\vartheta)p}.

In order to apply Lemma
4.5
to the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.24
), for each
\gamma\in(1,2]
, we need to choose suitable
(p,\vartheta)
, which depend only on
\gamma
, such that

(4.25)

1\leq\frac{2+\vartheta(p-2)}{p\gamma-p}\leq 2.

Setting
\vartheta=\gamma-1\in(0,1]
above, then

\eqref{dengjia0}\iff 0\leq\frac{4-2\gamma}{p\gamma-p}\leq 1,

which holds for all

(4.26)

p\in[p_{0}(\gamma),\infty)\qquad\text{with $p_{0}(\gamma):=\max\big\{2,\frac{4-2\gamma}{\gamma-1}\big\}$}.

This implies that (
4.25
) holds for
\vartheta=\gamma-1
, any
\gamma\in(1,2]
,
and any
p\in[p_{0}(\gamma),\infty)
. Consequently, based on (
4.24
), the above discussion, Lemma
4.5
, and the Young inequality, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\gamma\in(1,2]
, and
p\in[p_{0}(\gamma),\infty)
with
p_{0}(\gamma)
defined in (
4.26
), we have

(4.27)

\mathrm{G}_{4,1}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

Substituting (
4.27
) into (
4.23
) gives that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.28)

\mathrm{G}_{4}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

3.
Combining (
4.22
) and (
4.28
), then choosing a suitable small
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and applying the Grönwall inequality to the resulting inequality, we have

\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)

for all
p\in[p_{0}(\gamma),\infty)
with
p_{0}(\gamma)
defined in (
4.26
). Finally, we derive from Lemma
4.1
and the interpolation that the above inequality holds for all
p\in[2,\infty)
.
∎

Based on Lemma
4.9
, we obtain the following interior
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
V
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V(t)\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p,T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Let
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
. We first restrict the parameter
p
such that

(4.29)

\max\big\{2,\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\big\}\leq p<\infty.

Multiplying (
4.2
) by
\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0}|V|^{p-2}V
gives

\frac{1}{p}(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0}|V|^{p})_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p-2}VU.

Then integrating the above over
I
, together with the Young inequality, gives that, for all
\vartheta\in(0,1)
,

(4.30)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{1}\big(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{(\gamma-1)(\vartheta p+1-\vartheta)+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}|U|

\displaystyle\times\big((\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}|V|\big)^{\vartheta(p-1)}\big((\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}|V|\big)^{(1-\vartheta)(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\underline{\Big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}\eta^{\frac{2}{(\gamma-1)(\vartheta p+1-\vartheta)}}\varrho\Big|_{\infty}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!(\gamma-1)(\vartheta p+1-\vartheta)}_{:=\mathrm{G}_{5}}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+C(p)\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{4\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}.

2.
Now we estimate
\mathrm{G}_{5}
. In order to apply Lemma
4.5
to
\mathrm{G}_{5}
, for each
\gamma\in(1,2]
and
p
satisfying (
4.29
), we need to choose suitable
\vartheta
depending only on
(\gamma,p)
such that

1\leq\frac{2}{(\gamma-1)(\vartheta p+1-\vartheta)}\leq 2,

which is equivalent to showing that there exists
\vartheta=\vartheta(\gamma,p)
satisfying

(4.31)

p\in[p_{*}(\vartheta;\gamma),p^{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)]\quad\,\text{with }p_{*}(\vartheta;\gamma):=1+\frac{2-\gamma}{\vartheta(\gamma-1)}\text{ and }p^{*}(\vartheta;\gamma):=1+\frac{3-\gamma}{\vartheta(\gamma-1)}.

A direct calculation shows that
p_{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)
and
p^{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)
are both strictly decreasing with respect to
\vartheta
for each
\gamma\in(1,2]
, and

\lim_{\vartheta\to 0}p^{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)=\lim_{\vartheta\to 0}p_{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)=\infty,\quad\lim_{\vartheta\to 1}p_{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)=\frac{1}{\gamma-1},\quad\lim_{\vartheta\to 1}p^{*}(\vartheta;\gamma)=\frac{2}{\gamma-1}.

Since
\gamma\in(1,2]
and
p
satisfying (
4.29
), for each
(\gamma,p)
, we can fix

\vartheta=\vartheta_{0}=\vartheta_{0}(\gamma,p)\in(0,1)

such that (
4.31
) holds, and then we can apply Lemma
4.5
to
\mathrm{G}_{5}
to obtain

(4.32)

\mathrm{G}_{5}\leq C(p,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

3.
Substituting (
4.32
) into (
4.30
) and applying the Grönwall inequality to the resulting inequality, along with Lemma
4.9
, yield that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
p
satisfying (
4.29
),

(4.33)

\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,T)\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}v_{0}\big|_{p}\leq C(p,T).

Finally, it follows from Lemma
4.4
and the interpolation that (
4.33
) holds for all
p\in[2,\infty)
.
∎

Now we can prove Lemma
4.6
for the case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Combining Lemmas
4.9
–
4.10
, we obtain the desired estimates of Lemma
4.6
when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
.
∎

4.4.3.
Case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)

We first consider the
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
U
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(\varepsilon,p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.34)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\mu\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Let
n=3
,
\gamma\in(2,3)
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. First, repeating the same calculations as in Step 1 of the proof for Lemma
4.9
gives

(4.35)

\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\mu\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(p)\mathrm{G}_{4},

where
\mathrm{G}_{4}
is defined in (
4.22
) of the proof for Lemma
4.9
.

2.
Now, we estimate
\mathrm{G}_{4}
. Repeating calculations (
4.23
) and
\eqref{g2,1-xiao}_{1}
–
\eqref{g2,1-xiao}_{2}
in Step 2 of the proof for Lemma
4.9
and taking
\vartheta=1
in
\eqref{g2,1-xiao}_{2}
, we have

(4.36)

\mathrm{G}_{4}\leq\mathrm{G}_{4,1}+\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,T),

and, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{4,1}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{p}\eta_{r}\varrho^{p\gamma-p+1}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle=C(\varepsilon,p)\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\zeta\eta^{p}\eta_{r}\varrho^{p\gamma-p+1}\,\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}.

Then we choose

\displaystyle(a_{0},b_{0})

\displaystyle=(p,p\gamma-p+1),

\displaystyle(a_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=(\frac{p}{p-1}a_{0}+\frac{p-2}{p-1},\frac{p}{p-1}b_{0}-\frac{\gamma}{p-1}),

and denote

\mathrm{I}_{(k,\ell)}:=\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{k}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Since
2b_{0}\geq a_{0}+1
, it follows from (
2.1
), the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, Lemma
4.3
, integration by parts, and the Young inequality that, for all
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
,

(4.38)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{(a_{0},b_{0})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{a_{0}+1}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{r}\eta^{a_{0}+1}\varrho^{b_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{b_{0}p}{\gamma(a_{0}+1)}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{a_{0}+1}\varrho^{b_{0}-\frac{\gamma}{p}}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(a_{0})\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{5}{8}}\frac{r^{2b_{0}}\rho_{0}^{b_{0}}}{\eta^{2b_{0}-a_{0}-1}\eta_{r}^{b_{0}}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon_{0}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,a_{0},b_{0})\varepsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{I}_{(a_{1},b_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C(a_{0},b_{0},T)+\varepsilon_{0}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,a_{0},b_{0})\varepsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{I}_{(a_{1},b_{1})}.

Next, define two sequences
(\{a_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}},\{b_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}})
as follows:

(4.39)

\displaystyle a_{j+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{p-1}a_{j}+\frac{p-2}{p-1}\qquad\text{with }a_{0}=p,

\displaystyle b_{j+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{p-1}b_{j}-\frac{\gamma}{p-1}\qquad\text{with }b_{0}=p\gamma-p+1.

Clearly, we can solve for
(a_{j},b_{j})
from (
4.39
) that, for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.40)

a_{j}=2(p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j}-(p-2),\quad\,\,b_{j}=(\gamma-1)(p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j}+\gamma,

and check that
2b_{j}\geq a_{j}+1
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\in(2,3)
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. Following the same argument as in (
4.38
) thus implies that, for all
\varepsilon_{j}\in(0,1)
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathrm{I}_{(a_{j},b_{j})}\leq C(a_{j},b_{j},T)+\varepsilon_{j}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,a_{j},b_{j})\varepsilon_{j}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{I}_{(a_{j+1},b_{j+1})},

which, along with (
4.38
), yields

(4.41)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{(a_{0},b_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C(a_{0},b_{0},T)+\sum_{k=1}^{j}C(a_{k},b_{k},T)\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\varepsilon_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{j}\varepsilon_{k}\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+\Big(\prod_{\ell=0}^{j}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)\mathrm{I}_{(a_{j+1},b_{j+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C(a_{0},b_{0},T)+\sum_{k=1}^{j}C(a_{k},b_{k},T)\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\varepsilon_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{j}\varepsilon_{k}\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+\Big(\prod_{\ell=0}^{j}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)\big|\zeta\eta\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{b_{j+1}-1}\big|\zeta\eta^{a_{j+1}-b_{j+1}+1}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}.

Here, we need to check that Lemma
4.5
is applicable to the last term of the right-hand side of the above. To this end, for each
\gamma\in(2,3)
and
p\in[2,\infty)
, we need to show that

\exists\,j\in\mathbb{N},\text{ which depends only on $(p,\gamma)$, such that $a_{j+1}-b_{j+1}+1\in[0,2]$}.

After a direct calculation, this is equivalent to showing that, for each
\gamma\in(2,3)
and
p\in[2,\infty)
,

(4.42)

\gamma\in(2,3)\subset\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\big[f_{*}(j;p),f^{*}(j;p)\big],

where

\displaystyle f_{*}(j;p)

\displaystyle:=3-\frac{p+2}{(p-1)(\frac{p}{p-1})^{j+1}+1},

\displaystyle f^{*}(j;p)

\displaystyle:=3-\frac{p}{(p-1)(\frac{p}{p-1})^{j+1}+1}.

Clearly, both
f_{*}(j;p)
and
f^{*}(j;p)
are increasing with respect to
j
as
j\to\infty
, and

f_{*}(0;p)<2<f^{*}(0;p),\qquad\lim_{j\to\infty}f_{*}(j;p)=\lim_{j\to\infty}f^{*}(j;p)=3.

Consequently, to obtain (
4.42
), it suffices to show that, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

[f_{*}(j;p),f^{*}(j;p)]\cap[f_{*}(j+1;p),f^{*}(j+1;p)]\neq\varnothing,

or, equivalently, to show that
f_{*}(j+1;p)\leq f^{*}(j;p)
for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
i.e.
,

(4.43)

3-\frac{p+2}{(p-1)(\frac{p}{p-1})^{j+2}+1}\leq 3-\frac{p}{(p-1)(\frac{p}{p-1})^{j+1}+1}\qquad\text{for any $j\in\mathbb{N}$}.

Indeed, for
\gamma\in(2,3)
and
p\in[2,\infty)
, a direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\eqref{baohan2}

\displaystyle\iff(p+2)(p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j+1}+p+2\geq p^{2}\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j+1}+p

\displaystyle\iff(p-2)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j+1}+2\geq 0,

which thus implies claim (
4.43
). Therefore, for each
\gamma\in(2,3)
and
p\in[2,\infty)
, we can set
j=j_{0}
depending only on
(p,\gamma)
in (
4.41
) such that
a_{j_{0}+1}-b_{j_{0}+1}+1\in[0,2]
, and then obtain from Lemma
4.5
that

(4.44)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{(a_{0},b_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(C(a_{0},b_{0},T)+\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}C(a_{k},b_{k},T)\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\varepsilon_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}\varepsilon_{k}\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+\Big(\prod_{\ell=0}^{j_{0}}C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\Big)C(a_{j_{0}+1},b_{j_{0}+1},T).

Now, let
\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
, and set

\varepsilon_{0}=\tilde{\varepsilon},\qquad\,\,\,\,\varepsilon_{k}=\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{j_{0}}\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{\varepsilon_{\ell}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}{C(p,a_{\ell},b_{\ell})}\quad\,\text{for }1\leq k\leq j_{0}.

Then we obtain from (
2.1
), (
4.1
), (
4.44
),
\gamma\in(2,3)
, and Lemma
4.5
that

(4.45)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{(a_{0},b_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tilde{\varepsilon},p,T)+\Big(\tilde{\varepsilon}+\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{j_{0}}\Big)\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=C(\tilde{\varepsilon},p,T)+2\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tilde{\varepsilon},p,T)+C(p)\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{p}}\varrho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}}(U,V)\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tilde{\varepsilon},p,T)+C(p)\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|\zeta\eta^{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tilde{\varepsilon},p,T)+C(p,T)\tilde{\varepsilon}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}.

Substituting (
4.45
) into (
4.37
) yields that, for all
\varepsilon,\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
,

(4.46)

\mathrm{G}_{4,1}\leq\big(C(\varepsilon,p,T)\tilde{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\big)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p)\tilde{\varepsilon}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+C(\tilde{\varepsilon},\varepsilon,p,T).

Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, setting
\tilde{\varepsilon}
such that

0<\tilde{\varepsilon}<\min\big\{\varepsilon,\frac{\varepsilon}{C(\varepsilon,p)},\frac{\varepsilon}{C(\varepsilon,p,T)}\big\}<1,

we thus obtain from the above and (
4.46
) that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.47)

\mathrm{G}_{4,1}\leq 2\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

Collecting (
4.36
) and (
4.47
) leads to the estimate of
\mathrm{G}_{4}
:

(4.48)

\mathrm{G}_{4}\leq\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+2\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

3.
Collecting (
4.35
) and (
4.48
), and then setting
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we eventually obtain the desired estimates.
∎

Based on Lemma
4.11
, we obtain the following interior
L^{p}
-energy estimates for
V
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.49)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{pA\gamma}{4\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p,T)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}.

Proof.

Let
n=3
,
\gamma\in(2,3)
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. Multiplying (
4.2
) by
\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0}|V|^{p-2}V
leads to

\frac{1}{p}(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0}|V|^{p})_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p-2}VU.

Then, integrating the above over
I
, we obtain from Lemma
4.5
and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}\leq\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|V|^{p-1}|U|\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{4\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}\eta^{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}}\varrho\big|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{4\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{4\mu}\big|(\zeta\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p},

which yields the desired result.
∎

Now, we prove Lemma
4.6
for the case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Let
n=3
,
\gamma\in(2,3)
, and
p\in[2,\infty)
. Multiplying (
4.49
) in Lemma
4.12
by
\frac{8\mu\varepsilon}{pA\gamma}
, combined with (
4.34
) in Lemma
4.11
, gives that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.50)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{8\mu\varepsilon}{pA\gamma}\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}V\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+\mu\Big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,T)\varepsilon\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+C(\varepsilon,p,T).

Thus, we can choose
\varepsilon
in (
4.50
) sufficiently small such that

0<\varepsilon<\min\big\{1,\frac{\mu}{2C(p,T)}\big\},

and obtain from the Grönwall inequality that (
4.17
) in Lemma
4.6
holds.

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.6
for the case when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
.
∎

4.5.
Global uniform upper bound of the density

With the help of Lemmas
4.5
–
4.6
, we now ready to establish the global uniform upper bound for
\varrho
in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
, and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|\varrho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[0,T].

Proof.

It follows from (
4.1
), Lemmas
4.3
,
4.5
–
4.6
, and
A.3
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(4.51)

\displaystyle|\zeta\varrho|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=|\zeta(\varrho^{\frac{1}{5}})^{5}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\big|(\zeta_{r}\varrho,\zeta\varrho^{\frac{4}{5}}(\varrho^{\frac{1}{5}})_{r})\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|\zeta_{r}\eta_{r}^{-1}|_{\infty}\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}+\big|\zeta\eta^{-\frac{m}{4}}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}^{\frac{4}{5}}\big|(\zeta r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{5}}(V,U)\big|_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+\underline{\big|\zeta\eta^{-\frac{m}{4}}\eta_{r}\varrho\big|_{1}}_{:=\mathrm{G}_{*}}.

To estimate
\mathrm{G}_{*}
, if
n=2
(
m=1
),

-\frac{m}{4}=-\frac{1}{4}\in(-1,0),

we can apply Lemma
4.5
to obtain

(4.52)

\mathrm{G}_{*}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$};

if
n=3
(
m=2
), it follows from integration by parts, (
2.1
), (
4.1
), Lemmas
4.3
and
4.5
–
4.6
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.53)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{*}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{r}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r=-2\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta_{r}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r+\frac{1}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{r}\varrho(V-U)\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{5}{8}}\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{\frac{3}{2}}\eta_{r}}\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\eta^{\frac{1}{8}}\eta_{r}\varrho\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{4}{5}}\big|(\zeta r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{5}}(V,U)\big|_{5}\leq C(T).

Thus, collecting (
4.51
)–(
4.53
) gives that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\zeta\varrho|_{\infty}\leq C(T)+\mathrm{G}_{*}\leq C(T),

which together with Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\varrho|_{\infty}\leq|\zeta\varrho|_{\infty}+|\zeta^{\sharp}\varrho|_{\infty}\leq C(T)+|\zeta^{\sharp}\eta^{-m}|_{\infty}|\eta^{m}\varrho|_{\infty}\leq C(T),

where the cut-off function
\zeta^{\sharp}
is defined in §
3.1.3
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.13
.
∎

5.
Global-In-Time Uniform Lower Bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})

The purpose of this section is to establish the global-in-time uniform lower bounds for
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
.
The conclusion of this section can be stated as follows:

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>1
such that

\frac{\eta(t,r)}{r}\geq C(T)^{-1},\quad\eta_{r}(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Uniform lower bound for
\frac{\eta}{r}
.
It follows from Lemma
4.13
and (
2.1
) that

\Big|\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{m}\eta_{r}}(t)\Big|_{\infty}=|\varrho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$},

which implies

(5.1)

(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t,r)\geq\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}(r)}{C(T)}\qquad\text{for all }(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}.

Now, we claim that, for any
T>0
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

(5.2)

(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t,r)\geq\frac{r^{m}}{C(T)}\qquad\text{for all }(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}.

Otherwise, there exist both
T>0
and a sequence of
\{(t_{k},r_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]\times\bar{I}
such that

(5.3)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t_{k},r_{k})}{r^{m}_{k}}=0.

This, together with (
5.1
), yields

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\rho_{0}(r_{k})}{C(T)}\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t_{k},r_{k})}{r_{k}^{m}}=0,

which, along with the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim(1-r)
, implies
r_{k}\to 1
as
k\to\infty
. Moreover, since
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]
, we can extract a subsequence
\{t_{k_{\ell}}\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]
such that
t_{k_{\ell}}\to t_{0}
for some
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, and hence

(5.4)

(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})\to(t_{0},1)\qquad\text{as }\ell\to\infty.

On the other hand, thanks to Lemma
4.3
, we have

\eta(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1}r^{m+1}\qquad\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$},

and we can obtain

0\leq\eta_{r}(t,r)=\frac{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t,r)}{\eta^{m}(t,r)}\leq C(T)\frac{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t,r)}{r^{m^{2}+m}}.

This, along with (
5.3
)–(
5.4
), yields

(5.5)

0\leq\eta_{r}(t_{0},1)=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\eta_{r}(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})\leq\lim_{\ell\to\infty}C(T)\frac{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})}{r_{k_{\ell}}^{m^{2}+m}}=0.

However, (
5.5
) contradicts to the fact that
\eta_{r}|_{r=1}=1
for all
t\in[0,T]
since
U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
. Therefore, claim (
5.2
) holds.

Finally, it follows from the fact that
\eta|_{r=0}=0
and (
5.2
) that

(5.6)

\frac{\eta^{m+1}(t,r)}{r^{m+1}}=\frac{m+1}{r^{m+1}}\int_{0}^{r}\eta^{m}\eta_{r}(t,\tilde{r})\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\geq C(T)^{-1}\frac{m+1}{r^{m+1}}\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{r}^{m}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\geq C(T)^{-1}.

2. Uniform lower bound for
\eta_{r}
.
First, letting
r\to 0
in (
5.6
) gives

(5.7)

\eta_{r}(t,0)\geq C(T)^{-1}\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Next, assume contrarily that there exist both
T>0
and a sequence of
\{(t_{k},r_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]\times\bar{I}
such that

(5.8)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\eta_{r}(t_{k},r_{k})=0.

Then it follows from Lemma
4.3
that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{r_{k}^{m}}{C(T)}\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\eta_{r}(t_{k},r_{k})=0,

which yields that
r_{k}\to 0
. This implies that there exists a subsequence
\{(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
such that
(t_{k_{\ell}},r_{k_{\ell}})\to(t_{0},0)
for some
t_{0}\in[0,T]
as
\ell\to\infty
, and hence we obtain
\eta_{r}(t_{0},0)=0
. This contradicts to (
5.7
), and thus yields that
\eta_{r}
admits a uniform lower bound in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
.
∎

6.
Global-In-Time Uniform Estimates of the Effective Velocity

The purpose of this section is to establish the global-in-time uniform estimates of the effective velocity.

6.1.
Boundedness of the effective velocity away from the origin

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\iota\in(-\frac{1}{p},\infty)
, and
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a positive constant
C(p,\iota,a,T)
such that

\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}U(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,\iota,a,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$},

where
\chi_{a}^{\sharp}
denotes the characteristic function on
(a,1]
for
a\in(0,1)

(
see
§
3.1.3
)
.

Proof.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\iota\in(-\frac{1}{p},\infty)
, and
a\in(0,1)
. Let
\varepsilon>0
be a fixed constant such that

\max\Big\{0,(\iota p+1)\beta-1,(\iota p+1)\beta-\frac{p}{2},\frac{(\iota p+1)\beta^{2}}{\beta+1}\Big\}<\varepsilon<(\iota p+1)\beta.

Note that
\varepsilon
is well-defined under the above constraint and satisfies

0<(\iota p+1)\beta-\varepsilon<1,\qquad\frac{p}{(\iota p+1)\beta-\varepsilon}>2,\qquad\text{and}\ \ \frac{-\beta+\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon-(\iota p+1)\beta}>-\beta.

Hence, it follows from Lemma
4.6
, the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}U|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{1}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta p}|U|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}r=\int_{a}^{1}\rho_{0}^{-\beta+\varepsilon}(\rho_{0}^{(\iota p+1)\beta-\varepsilon}|U|^{p})\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{a}^{1}\rho_{0}^{\frac{-\beta+\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon-(\iota p+1)\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1+\varepsilon-(\iota p+1)\beta}\Big(\int_{a}^{1}\rho_{0}|U|^{\frac{p}{(\iota p+1)\beta-\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{(\iota p+1)\beta-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\iota,a)\Big(\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|U|^{\frac{p}{(\iota p+1)\beta-\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{(\iota p+1)\beta-\varepsilon}\leq C(p,\iota,a,T).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
p\in[2,\infty)
,
\iota\in(\frac{p-1}{p},\infty)
, and
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a positive constant
C(p,\iota,a,T)
such that

\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}V(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,\iota,a,T)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[0,T].

Proof.

Let
p\in[2,\infty)
,
\iota\in(\frac{p-1}{p},\infty)
and
a\in(0,1)
. First, multiplying (
4.3
) by
\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}
and taking the
L^{p}
-norm of the resulting equality, we obtain from (
2.1
) and the Minkowski integral inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}V|_{p}\leq|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}v_{0}|_{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\frac{r^{(\gamma-1)m}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta+\gamma-1}U}{(\eta^{m}\eta_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\Big|_{p}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with Lemmas
5.1
and
6.1
,
implies

|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}V|_{p}\leq|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}v_{0}|_{p}+C(a,p,T)\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta+\gamma-1}U\big|_{p}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,\iota,a,T).

Here, for the initial data, since
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
and
\iota-1>-\frac{1}{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}v_{0}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota\beta}u_{0}\big|_{p}+\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\iota-1)\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}\big|_{p}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(|\rho_{0}^{\beta}|_{\infty}^{\iota}|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|(1-r)^{\iota-1}|_{p}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty}\big)\leq C(\iota,p).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

For any
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(a,T)>0
such that

\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}V(t)\big|_{\infty}\leq C(a,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into the following two steps.

1.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
be a fixed constant such that

k\geq\max\big\{2,\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\big\}.

It follows from (
4.1
) and Lemma
A.3
that

(6.1)

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\varrho^{\gamma-1}U\big|_{\infty}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}(\rho_{0}^{k\beta}\varrho^{(\gamma-1)k}|U|^{k})_{r}\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(k-1)\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}\varrho^{(\gamma-1)k}U^{k}\big|_{1}+C(a)\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{k\beta}\varrho^{(\gamma-1)k}\eta_{r}U^{k}(V,U)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle+C(a)\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{k\beta}\varrho^{(\gamma-1)k}\eta_{r}U^{k-1}D_{\eta}U\big|_{1}:=\sum_{i=6}^{8}\mathrm{G}_{i}.

For
\mathrm{G}_{6}
–
\mathrm{G}_{8}
, it follows from (
2.1
), Lemmas
4.13
and
6.1
–
6.2
, and the Hölder inequality that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{6}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{(\gamma-1)k}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty}\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(1-\frac{1}{k})\beta}U\big|_{k}^{k}\leq C(a,T),

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{7}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big|\frac{r^{m}}{\eta^{m}}\Big|_{\infty}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{(\gamma-1)k-1}\big(|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}V|_{2}\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{(k-1)\beta+1}{k}}U\big|_{2k}^{k}+\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{k\beta+1}{k+1}}U\big|_{k+1}^{k+1}\big)\leq C(a,T),

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{8}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\Big|\frac{r^{m}}{\eta^{m}}\Big|_{\infty}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{(\gamma-1)k-1}\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{k\beta+\frac{1}{2}}U^{k-1}\big|_{2}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(a,T)\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{2k\beta+1}{2k-2}}U\big|_{k-1}^{2k-2}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}\leq C(a,T)\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}.

Substituting (
6.2
) into (
6.1
) leads to

(6.3)

\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\varrho^{\gamma-1}U\big|_{\infty}^{k}\leq C(a,T)\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}+1\big).

2.
Next, multiplying (
4.3
) by
\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}
and taking the
L^{\infty}
-norm of the resulting equality, we obtain from (
6.3
), Lemma
4.1
, and the Young inequality that

(6.4)

\displaystyle|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}V|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}v_{0}|_{\infty}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\varrho^{\gamma-1}U\big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}v_{0}|_{\infty}+C(a,T)\int_{0}^{t}\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{k}}+1\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}v_{0}|_{\infty}+C(a,T)\int_{0}^{t}\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{2}+1\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(a,T),

where the initial data can be controlled by

|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}v_{0}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\big|(\rho_{0}^{\beta}u_{0},(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r})\big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}(|\rho_{0}^{\beta}|_{\infty}|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty})\leq C_{0}.

∎

6.2.
Boundedness of the effective velocity near the symmetric center

Lemma 6.4
.

For all
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}+1\big).

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}|U|^{p-2}U
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho|U|^{p})_{t}+2\mu(p-1)\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho|U|^{p-2}\Big(|D_{\eta}U|^{2}+\frac{m}{p}\frac{U^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=A(p-1)\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho^{\gamma}|U|^{p-2}D_{\eta}U-\frac{m}{p}\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho V|U|^{p}}{\eta}

\displaystyle-2\zeta\zeta_{r}\Big(2\mu\varrho|U|^{p-2}UD_{\eta}U+\frac{2\mu m}{p}\frac{\varrho|U|^{p}}{\eta}-A\varrho^{\gamma}|U|^{p-2}U\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(2\mu\zeta^{2}\varrho|U|^{p-2}UD_{\eta}U+\frac{2\mu m}{p}\frac{\zeta^{2}\varrho|U|^{p}}{\eta}-A\zeta^{2}\varrho^{\gamma}|U|^{p-2}U\Big)_{r}.

Then integrating the above over
I
leads to

(6.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\mu\Big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq A(p-1)\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\varrho^{\gamma}|U|^{p-2}U_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{m}{p}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho V|U|^{p}}{\eta}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}\zeta\zeta_{r}\Big(2\mu\varrho|U|^{p-2}UD_{\eta}U+\frac{2\mu m}{p}\frac{\varrho|U|^{p}}{\eta}-A\varrho^{\gamma}|U|^{p-2}U\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=9}^{11}\mathrm{G}_{i}.

For
\mathrm{G}_{9}
, it follows from Lemmas
4.5
and
4.13
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{9}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\varrho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|\zeta\eta_{r}\varrho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)+\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\mu}{20}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathrm{G}_{10}
, it follows from (
2.1
), and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{if $n=2$: }\quad\mathrm{G}_{10}&=-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho V|U|^{p}}{\eta}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p)|\zeta V|_{\infty}\Big|\big(\frac{r\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}\\
&\leq C(p)(|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p};\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{if $n=3$: }\quad\mathrm{G}_{10}&=-\frac{2}{p}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho V|U|^{p}}{\eta}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p)|\zeta V|_{\infty}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big|\big(\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\\
&\leq C(p)|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}\Big|\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\mu}{20}\Big|\big(\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}.\end{aligned}

To sum up, in both cases, we have

(6.7)

\mathrm{G}_{10}\leq C(p)(|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\mu}{20}\Big|\big(\frac{\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}.

For
\mathrm{G}_{11}
, it follows from (
2.1
), Lemmas
4.6
,
4.13
, and
5.1
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{11}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{5}{8}}\Big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}|U|^{p-1}|D_{\eta}U|}{\eta^{m}\eta_{r}}+\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}|U|^{p}}{\eta^{m+1}\eta_{r}}+\varrho^{\gamma-1}\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{m}\eta_{r}}\big)|U|^{p-1}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{p-1}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+|\varrho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{p-1}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{2}.

Consequently, collecting (
6.5
)–(
6.8
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\mu}{2}\Big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}^{p}+(|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\Big|\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\eta^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}U\Big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{p-1}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p,T),

which, along with Lemma
4.6
and the Grönwall inequality, leads to the desired estimates.
∎

Next, we can derive the
L^{1}([0,T];L^{\infty})
-estimate of
\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(\varepsilon,T)>0
such that

\int_{0}^{t}|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\varepsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
be a fixed number such that

k>\max\big\{3,\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\big\}.

First, it follows from Lemma
A.3
that

(6.9)

\displaystyle|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}^{k}\leq C_{0}\big|(\zeta^{k}\varrho^{k\gamma-k}|U|^{k})_{r}\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\big|\zeta^{k-1}\zeta_{r}\varrho^{k\gamma-k}U^{k}\big|_{1}+\big|\zeta^{k}\varrho^{k\gamma-k}\eta_{r}U^{k}(V,U)\big|_{1}+\big|\zeta^{k}\varrho^{k\gamma-k}\eta_{r}U^{k-1}D_{\eta}U\big|_{1}\big):=\sum_{i=12}^{14}\mathrm{G}_{i}.

Then, using (
4.1
), Lemma
4.13
, and the Hölder inequality, we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{12}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\eta_{r}^{-1}|_{\infty}|\zeta|_{\infty}^{k-3}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{k(\gamma-1)-1}\big|(\zeta^{2}\varrho\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{k}\leq C(T)\big|(\zeta^{2}\varrho\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{k},

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{13}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{k(\gamma-1)-1}|\zeta|_{\infty}^{k-3}\big(|\zeta V|_{\infty}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{k}+|\zeta|_{\infty}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}U\big|_{k+1}^{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|\zeta V|_{\infty}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{k}+\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}U\big|_{k+1}^{k+1}\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{14}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\varrho|_{\infty}^{k(\gamma-1)-1}|\zeta|_{\infty}^{k-2}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{\frac{k}{2}}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{\frac{k}{2}}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}.

Combining (
6.9
)–(
6.10
) gives

\displaystyle|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(1+|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{k}})\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}+C(T)\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}U\big|_{k+1}^{\frac{k+1}{k}}

\displaystyle+C(T)\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{k}}U\big|_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{k}},

which, along with Lemma
6.4
and the Young inequality, leads to

|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\Big(1+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{k}}+\big(1+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{k}}\big)\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{k}}\Big).

Finally, integrating the above over
[0,t]
, then we obtain from the fact that
k>3
, Lemma
6.4
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{k}}+\big(1+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{k}}\big)\int_{0}^{t}\big|(\zeta^{2}\eta_{r}\varrho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U|^{\frac{k-2}{2}}D_{\eta}U\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{k}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{k}}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{k}}\Big)\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\varepsilon\!\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

For any
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(a,T)>0
such that

|\zeta_{a}V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(a,T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

First, it follows from (
4.3
) and Lemma
6.5
that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(6.11)

\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}\leq|\zeta v_{0}|_{\infty}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\int_{0}^{t}\big|\zeta\varrho^{\gamma-1}U\big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\frac{A\gamma\varepsilon}{2\mu}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\zeta V|_{\infty}.

Here, the bound of
|\zeta v_{0}|_{\infty}
follows from (
4.1
) and the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
,
i.e.
,

(6.12)

|\zeta v_{0}|_{\infty}\leq|\zeta u_{0}|_{\infty}+2\mu|\zeta(\log\rho_{0})_{r}|_{\infty}\leq|\zeta u_{0}|_{\infty}+C_{0}|\zeta\rho_{0}^{-\beta}|_{\infty}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}.

Then setting
\varepsilon
in (
6.11
) such that

\varepsilon=\min\big\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\mu}{A\gamma}\big\},

we obtain that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\zeta V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\implies|\zeta_{a}V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any }a\in\big(0,\frac{1}{2}\big].

Finally, for
a\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, it follows from the above, the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, and Lemma
6.3
that

|\zeta_{a}V(t)|_{\infty}\leq|\zeta V(t)|_{\infty}+\|V(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2},a)}\leq C(T)+C(a)|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}V(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(a,T).

This completes the proof.
∎

7.
Global-In-Time Uniform Upper Bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})

The purpose of this section is to establish the global-in-time uniform upper bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
.

7.1.
Uniform upper bounds of
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})
in the exterior domain

7.1.1.
Some auxiliary estimates

We first give some auxiliary estimates in Lemmas
7.1
–
7.3
, which will be used in §
7.1.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

For any
L>0
and
a\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(a,L,T)>0
such that
