), and then establish global well-posedness of classical solutions of the linearized problem
via a modified Galerkin scheme.
Specifically, we initiate to study the following linearized problem in
[0,T]\times I
(multiply
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
by
\eta^{m}\eta_{r}
and then replace
\eta\mapsto\bar{\eta}
):

(11.4)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t}+A\Big(\frac{r^{\gamma m}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\Big)_{r}-Am\frac{r^{\gamma m}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}}{\bar{\eta}(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}=2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}-2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}^{2}},\\[9.0pt]
U|_{t=0}=u_{0}\qquad\text{in }I,\end{cases}

where
\bar{\eta}
stands for the flow map corresponding to
\bar{U}
:

(11.5)

\bar{\eta}(t,r)=r+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{U}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\bar{\eta}(0,r)=r,

and
\bar{U}
is a given function satisfying
\bar{U}(0,r)=u_{0}(r)
for
r\in I

and, for any
T>0
,

(11.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,\bar{U})+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})\in L^{\infty}(0,T),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})\in L^{1}(0,T),

\displaystyle\big(\bar{U},\bar{U}_{r},\frac{\bar{U}}{r}\big)\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I})),\qquad\big(\bar{U}_{rr},(\frac{\bar{U}}{r})_{r},\bar{U}_{t}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I})),

\displaystyle\bar{U}|_{r=0}=\bar{U}_{r}|_{r=1}=0\qquad\,\text{for $t\in(0,T]$},

where
(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\bar{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
are defined in the same way as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})(t,f)
in (
2.4
) and (
2.7
), except with
\eta
in place of
\bar{\eta}
. Besides,
we define
(\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}},\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}})(t,f)
in the similar manner to
(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\bar{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
.

Clearly, (
11.5
), together with (
11.6
), also implies the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
, that is,

(11.7)

\displaystyle\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\bar{\eta},\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r},\bar{\eta}_{rr},(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r},\bar{\eta}_{rrr},(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{rr},\frac{1}{r}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r},\bar{\eta}_{rrrr},(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{rrr},(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r})_{r}\big)\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bar{\eta},\bar{\eta}_{r})\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\qquad\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(\bar{\eta}_{rr},\bar{\eta}_{rrr},\bar{\eta}_{rrrr})\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\big(\bar{\eta},\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r}\big)\in C^{1}([0,T];C(\bar{I})),\qquad\big(\bar{\eta}_{rr},(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}\big)\in C^{1}((0,T];C(\bar{I})).

Moreover, we assume here that

(11.8)

(\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})(t,r)\in\big[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\big]\qquad\text{for all }(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}.

This requirement will be fulfilled in §
11.2
for the corresponding linearization procedure.

Now we define the classical solution of the linearized problem (
11.4
), which slightly differs from Definition
2.1
.

Definition 11.1
.

We say that
U(t,r)
is a classical solution of the linearized problem (
11.4
) in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
if
U(t,r)
satisfies equation
\eqref{lp}_{1}
pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
, takes the initial data
\eqref{lp}_{2}
continuously, and

(11.9)

\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I})),\qquad\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r},U_{t}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I})).

Then the main conclusion in §
11.1
can be stated in the following lemma:

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
n=2
or
3
and
\gamma\in(\frac{4}{3},\infty)
. Assume that
\rho_{0}(r)
satisfies (
1.17
) for some
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\gamma-1]
and
u_{0}(r)
satisfies

\mathcal{E}(0,U)<\infty.

Then, for any
T>0
, the linearized problem (
11.4
) admits a unique classical solution
U
in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
satisfying

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\,\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)\in L^{1}(0,T),

\displaystyle U|_{r=0}=U_{r}|_{r=1}=0\qquad\qquad\text{for $t\in(0,T]$},

\displaystyle|U_{r}(t,r)|\leq C(T)(1-r)\qquad\text{for $(t,r)\in(0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

11.1.1.
The modified Galerkin method: weak and strong solutions of some general problems

In order to establish the well-posedness of (
11.4
), we initiate to study a general initial boundary value problem in
[0,T]\times I
:

(11.10)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{w_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{w}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}=m(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}{\bar{\eta}}-\big((r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)_{r},\\[8.0pt]
w|_{t=0}=w_{0}\qquad\text{on }I,\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
are given functions,
and
w_{0}\in L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
.

First, we study the weak solutions of problem (
11.10
).
This existence result will be frequently used in §
11.1.2
for proving Lemma
11.1
.

Definition 11.2
.

We say that a function
w(t,r)
is a weak solution in
[0,T]\times I
of
problem (
11.10
) if the following three properties hold
:

(i)

w\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
and
r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}});

(ii)

for all
\varphi
satisfying
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
and a.e. time
t\in(0,T)
,

(11.11)

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t},\varphi\right>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle;

(iii)

w(0,r)=w_{0}(r)
for a.e.
r\in I
.

Now we can establish the following existence of weak solutions and their related estimates.

Proposition 11.2
.

For all
T>0
, problem (
11.10
) admits a unique weak solution
w
in
[0,T]\times I
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|w|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\big\|r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(|w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1. Introduction of the Galerkin scheme.
First, according to Lemma
A.1
, for given
w_{0}\in L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, there exists a smooth sequence
\{w_{0}^{\vartheta}\}_{\vartheta>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
satisfying

(11.12)

\lim_{\vartheta\to 0}|w^{\vartheta}_{0}-w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}=0.

Next, we construct a sequence of Galerkin bases. In order to match the spherical symmetry structure of (
11.10
) and fulfill the Neumann boundary condition, it is reasonable to consider the following eigenvalue problem, which is the so-called Sturm-Liouville problem:

(11.13)

-(r^{m}\xi_{r})_{r}+mr^{m-2}\xi=\lambda r^{m}\xi\quad\text{on $I$},\qquad\,\,\xi|_{r=0}=\xi_{r}|_{r=1}=0.

Then we can expect to construct a Hilbert basis
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
of
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
, which is orthonormal in
L^{2}_{r^{m}}
and orthogonal in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
. Actually, we have the following well-known Sturm-Liouville theorem:

Lemma 11.2

(
[
67
]
)
.

Consider the Sturm-Liouville problem (
11.13
).

(i)

All eigenvalues
\lambda
of problem (
11.13
) are nonzero,
real, and have multiplicity one.
Moreover, there are infinite but countable eigenvalues

\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
, which are bounded below, strictly increasing and
\lambda_{j}\to\infty
as
j\to\infty
.

(ii)

There exists a sequence of eigenfunctions
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
, corresponding to the eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
. Such a sequence of eigenfunctions
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is orthonormal and complete in
L^{2}_{r^{m}}
, namely,
\langle r^{m}\xi_{j},\xi_{k}\rangle=\delta_{kj}
for
k,j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and

\lim_{N\to\infty}|f_{N}-f|_{2,r^{m}}=0\qquad\text{for any $f\in L^{2}_{r^{m}}$},

where
f_{N}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\langle r^{m}f,\xi_{j}\rangle\xi_{j}
.

(iii)

Such a sequence of eigenfunctions
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is also orthogonal and complete in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
, namely,
\langle r^{m}(\xi_{j})_{r},(\xi_{k})_{r}\rangle+m\big<r^{m-2}\xi_{j},\xi_{k}\big>=\lambda_{j}\delta_{jk}
for
k,j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and

\lim_{N\to\infty}\|f_{N}-f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}}=0\qquad\text{for any $f\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}$}.

Proof.

We only prove the completeness in (iii). Define the bilinear form
\mathscr{B}[\cdot,\cdot]
by

\mathscr{B}[f,g]:=\langle r^{m}f_{r},g_{r}\rangle+m\langle r^{m-2}f,g\rangle.

First, a direct calculation, combined with Lemma
A.4
and the Hölder inequality, implies

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}[f,g]\leq|f_{r}|_{2,r^{m}}|g_{r}|_{2,r^{m}}+m|f|_{2,r^{m-2}}|g|_{2,r^{m-2}}\leq C\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}},

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}[f,f]=|f_{r}|_{2,r^{m}}^{2}+m|f|_{2,r^{m-2}}^{2}\geq C^{-1}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}}^{2},

which yields that
\mathscr{B}[f,g]
is a bounded coercive bilinear form in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
. Next, suppose that

(11.14)

\mathscr{B}[\xi_{j},f]=0\qquad\text{for all $j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

To show that
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is complete in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
, it suffices to prove
f=0
.

Note that, since
\xi_{j}
is the eigenfunction corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda_{j}
, we have

\mathscr{B}[\xi_{j},f]=\lambda_{j}\langle r^{m}\xi_{j},f\rangle\qquad\,\,\text{for all $f\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}$}.

This, combined with (
11.14
), yields

(11.15)

\langle r^{m}\xi_{j},f\rangle=0\qquad\text{for all $j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$},

which, along with the completeness of
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
in
L^{2}_{r^{m}}
, implies
f=0
.
∎

Consequently, in view of the above Galerkin basis
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
, we set

(11.16)

w^{N,\vartheta}(t,r):=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mu_{k}^{N,\vartheta}(t)\xi_{k}(r)\qquad\text{for }\vartheta\in(0,1)\text{ and }N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}.

Here,
\mu_{k}^{N,\vartheta}(t)
are selected by solving the following ODE problem in
[0,T]
:

(11.17)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t},\xi_{j}\big>+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w^{N,\vartheta},\xi_{j}\big>\\[8.0pt]
\qquad\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\xi_{j}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\rangle,\\[8.0pt]
\mu_{j}^{n,\vartheta}(0)=\langle r^{m}w_{0}^{\vartheta},\xi_{j}\rangle,\ \ j=1,2,\cdots\!,N,\end{cases}

which can also be rewritten as

(11.18)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mu^{N,\vartheta}(t)+\mathfrak{B}(t)\cdot\mu^{N,\vartheta}(t)=\mathfrak{c}(t)\qquad\text{in $(0,T]$},\\[10.0pt]
\mu_{j}^{N,\vartheta}(0)=\langle r^{m}w_{0}^{\vartheta},\xi_{j}\rangle\ \qquad\text{for $j=1,2,\cdots\!,N$},\end{cases}

where
\mu^{N,\vartheta}(t):=(\mu_{1}^{N,\vartheta},\cdots\!,\mu_{N}^{N,\vartheta})^{\top}(t)
and

(11.19)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{A}_{kj})_{1\leq k,j\leq N},\qquad\mathfrak{B}(t)=(\mathfrak{B}_{kj}(t))_{1\leq k,j\leq N},\qquad\mathfrak{c}(t)=(\mathfrak{c}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathfrak{c}_{N})^{\top}(t),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}_{kj}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\xi_{k}\xi_{j}\,\mathrm{d}r,\qquad\ \mathfrak{B}_{kj}(t):=2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{k}D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}+m\frac{\xi_{k}\xi_{j}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{j}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\xi_{j}}{\bar{\eta}}+\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r.

To solve (
11.18
), we first note that, due to the facts that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
and the linear independency of
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
,
\{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are linearly independent, and hence its Gram matrix
\mathfrak{A}
is non-singular. Next, from (
11.5
) and Lemma
11.2
, it is direct to check that
\mathfrak{B}(t)\in C^{1}[0,T]
and
\mathfrak{c}(t)\in L^{2}(0,T)
. Therefore, we can obtain the following existence result for (
11.18
) from the classical ODE theory.

Lemma 11.3

(
[
15
]
)
.

Problem (
11.18
) admits a unique solution
\mu_{j}^{N,\vartheta}\in AC[0,T]
, for each
j=1,2,\cdots\!,N
,
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and
\vartheta\in(0,1)
, where
AC
denotes the space of absolutely continuous functions. As a consequence,
w^{N,\vartheta}(t,r)\in AC([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}})
and
w^{N,\vartheta}
is differentiable a.e. in
t
, for each
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
\vartheta\in(0,1)
.

2. Uniform estimates of
w^{N,\vartheta}
.
First, multiplying (
11.17
) by
\mu^{N,\vartheta}_{j}(t)
and summing the resulting equality with respect to
j
from
1
to
N
imply

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}+m\frac{|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{mw^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}\,\mathrm{d}r,

which, along with the Young inequality, yields

(11.20)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\!+\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}+m\frac{|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
, together with (
11.8
), leads to

(11.21)

|w^{N,\vartheta}(t)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|w^{N,\vartheta}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\!\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T)\Big(|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where

w^{N,\vartheta}(0,r)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mu_{j}^{N,\vartheta}(0)e_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\langle w_{0}^{\vartheta},\xi_{j}\rangle\xi_{j}
.

Next, we derive the
L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
-boundedness of
w^{N,\vartheta}(0,r)
. It follows from (
11.12
) that, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\vartheta_{0}=\vartheta_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
such that

(11.22)

|w^{\vartheta}_{0}-w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\qquad\text{ for any $0<\vartheta\leq\vartheta_{0}$}.

Then, for such
\varepsilon,\vartheta_{0}>0
and fixed
\vartheta\in(0,\vartheta_{0}]
, by
w_{0}^{\vartheta}\in L^{2}\subset L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
and Lemma
11.2
, we can find a large
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,\vartheta_{0})\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
so that

|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)-w^{\vartheta}_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}=\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\langle w_{0}^{\vartheta},\xi_{j}\rangle\xi_{j}-w_{0}^{\vartheta}\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\qquad\text{for any $N\geq N_{0}$},

which, combined with (
11.22
), yields

|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)-w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}\leq|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)-w^{\vartheta}_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}+|w^{\vartheta}_{0}-w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}<\varepsilon.

Hence, setting
\varepsilon:=|w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, the above statement implies that there exist
\vartheta_{0}=\vartheta_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,\vartheta_{0})\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that, for any
\vartheta\in(0,\vartheta_{0}]
and
N\geq N_{0}
,

(11.23)

|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}\leq 2|w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}.

Finally, substituting (
11.23
) into (
11.21
) yields

(11.24)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|w^{N,\vartheta}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|w^{N,\vartheta}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T)\Big(|w_{0}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

3. Taking the limit as
N,\vartheta^{-1}\to\infty
.

Based on (
11.24
), the fact that
L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
is a separable reflexive Banach space, owing to Lemma
A.1
and the weak convergence arguments, we can extract a subsequence (still denoted by)
w^{N,\vartheta}
and some limits
w
and
(\mathfrak{w}_{1},\mathfrak{w}_{2})
such that

(11.25)

\displaystyle w^{N,\vartheta}\rightharpoonup w

\displaystyle\text{weakly* in }L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

\displaystyle\big(w^{N,\vartheta}_{r},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{r}\big)\rightharpoonup(\mathfrak{w}_{1},\mathfrak{w}_{2})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

Then the definition of weak derivatives and the uniqueness of limits imply that
(\mathfrak{w}_{1},\mathfrak{w}_{2})=(w_{r},\frac{w}{r})
.
In addition, the lower semi-continuity of weak convergence (
11.25
) also implies that (
11.24
) holds for
w
.

Now we take the limit as
N,\vartheta^{-1}\to\infty
in (
11.17
). Let
\mathfrak{g}^{M}(t,r)=\sum_{j=1}^{M}g_{j}(t)\xi_{j}(r)
with
g_{j}\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(0,T)
for
j=1,\cdots\!,M
and
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
Then it follows from (
11.17
) that, for any
N\geq M
,

(11.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\!,\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t\!+\!2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}\!,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t\!+\!2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w^{N,\vartheta}\!,\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}^{M}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t.

Since both
w^{N,\vartheta}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{M}
are differentiable with respect to
t
,
\partial_{t}
in (
11.26
) can be transferred from
w^{N,\vartheta}
to
\mathfrak{g}^{M}
via integration by parts. Then, based on (
11.25
), we let
N,\vartheta^{-1}\to\infty
in the resulting equality to obtain

(11.27)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w,\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}^{M}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t.

Next, since
r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}\in H^{-1}([0,T];L^{2})
due to
w\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
, by (
11.8
), (
11.27
), and the definition of distributional derivatives, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t},\mathfrak{g}^{M}\rangle_{H_{t}^{-1}((H^{1})^{*})\times H^{1}_{0,t}(H^{1})}\big|=\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w,\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\big|\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\big|\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\Big|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}^{M}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\Big|\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\big|\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\big|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|w\|_{L^{2}_{t}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})}+\|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{t}(L^{2})}\big)\|\mathfrak{g}^{M}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})}.

We now claim that

(11.28)

\left\{\mathfrak{g}^{M}\right\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\text{ is dense in }L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

Once this is proved, it follows from the Hahn-Banach theorem (see
[
17
]
) that
r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}
, which is a functional initially defined on
H^{1}_{0}([0,T];L^{2})
, can be uniquely extended to a functional defined on
L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
and

(11.29)

\|r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})}\leq C\big(\|w\|_{L^{2}_{t}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})}+\|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{t}(L^{2})}\big)\leq C.

To derive (
11.28
), it suffices to prove the density of
\mathrm{span}\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
. Indeed, thanks to Proposition
A.1
, for any given
f\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, there exists a sequence
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
such that

f^{\varepsilon}\to f\ \ \text{in $\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}$}\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

On the other hand, for any
f\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
, we obtain from Lemma
11.2
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
that

\sum_{j=1}^{M}\langle f,\xi_{j}\rangle\xi_{j}\to f\,\,\,\,\text{in $\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}$}\qquad\,\,\text{as $M\to\infty$}.

Consequently, if setting
f^{M,\varepsilon}:=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\langle f^{\varepsilon},\xi_{j}\rangle\xi_{j}
, we derive a sequence of
\{f^{M,\varepsilon}\}\subset\mathrm{span}\{\xi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
converging to
f
in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
as
M,\varepsilon^{-1}\to\infty
, which shows claim (
11.28
).

Finally, for any
\mathfrak{g}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
,
there exists a sequence
\{\mathfrak{g}^{M}\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}

with each
\mathfrak{g}^{M}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
such that
\mathfrak{g}^{M}\to\mathfrak{g}
in
L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
as
M\to\infty
. Taking the limit as
M\to\infty
in (
11.27
), along with (
11.29
), implies that, for all
\mathfrak{g}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
,

(11.30)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t},\mathfrak{g}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\!\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t.

Hence, the weak formulation (
11.11
) follows simply by setting
\mathfrak{g}(t,r)=\varphi(r)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
in (
11.30
) and applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of the resulting equality.

4. Uniqueness and time continuity.

First, it follows from
w\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
, (
11.29
), and Lemma
A.9
(let
s=1
in Lemma
A.9
) that
w\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
.

It still remains to show
w(0,r)=w_{0}(r)
for
a.e.

r\in I
. On one hand, thanks to (
11.11
) and
w\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
, for any
\mathfrak{g}\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{1}([0,T);\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
,

(11.31)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w,\mathfrak{g}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w(0),\mathfrak{g}(0)\rangle+\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t.

On the other hand, choose
\mathfrak{g}^{M}(t,x)=\sum_{j=1}^{M}g_{j}(t)\xi_{j}
in (
11.26
) with
g_{j}(t)\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}([0,T))
(
j=1,\cdots\!,M
) such that
\mathfrak{g}^{M}\to\mathfrak{g}
in
C^{1}_{\mathrm{c}}([0,T);\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
as
M\to\infty
. Then (
11.26
) becomes

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{N,\vartheta}\!\!,\mathfrak{g}^{M}_{t}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu\!\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}\!\!,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\!\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w^{N,\vartheta}\!\!,\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{N,\vartheta}(0),\mathfrak{g}^{M}(0)\big>+\!\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}^{M}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t\!+\!\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}^{M}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

for all
M\leq N
. Taking the limit as
M,N\to\infty
in the above equality gives that, for all
\mathfrak{g}\in C^{1}_{\mathrm{c}}([0,T);\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
,

(11.32)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w,\mathfrak{g}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu\int_{0}^{T}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+2\mu m\int_{0}^{T}\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\mathfrak{g}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{0},\mathfrak{g}(0)\rangle+\int_{0}^{T}\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\mathfrak{g}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{g}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t.

Comparing with (
11.31
) and (
11.32
) yields that
w(0,r)=w_{0}
for
a.e.

r\in I
. Finally, setting
w_{0}=0
,
\varphi=w
, and
\mathfrak{q}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}_{2}=0
in (
11.11
) yields
w=0
, which implies the uniqueness.

This completes the proof of Proposition
11.2

∎

Next, we show that, if
w_{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, the regularity of weak solutions obtained in Proposition
11.2
can be improved.

Proposition 11.3
.

Assume that
w
is the weak solution of (
11.17
) obtained in
Proposition
11.2
. If
w_{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
and

(11.33)

(\mathfrak{q_{1}},\mathfrak{q}_{2})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),\qquad((\mathfrak{q_{1}})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

then

(11.34)

\displaystyle w\in L^{\infty}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\quad w_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}w_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\quad\sqrt{t}(r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{tt})\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

and
w
satisfies the following formulation
:
for any a.e.
t>0
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
,

(11.35)

\displaystyle\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{tt},\varphi\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w_{t},\varphi\big>

\displaystyle-4\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle-4\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\bar{U}w,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big),D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle.

In particular, the following energy equality holds for a.e.
t>0
:

(11.36)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|w_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t}|^{2}+m\frac{|w_{t}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=4\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}wD_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r+4\mu m\int_{0}^{1}\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\bar{U}ww_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+m\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\frac{w_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
First, from Proposition
11.1
, for given
w_{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, it follows that there exists a smooth sequence
\{w_{0}^{\vartheta}\}_{\vartheta>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
satisfying

(11.37)

\lim_{\vartheta\to 0}\|w^{\vartheta}_{0}-w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}=0.

2.
Based on the mollified initial data
w_{0}^{\vartheta}
and the Galerkin scheme shown in Step 1 of the proof of Proposition
11.2
, we can construct a Galerkin approximate sequence
w^{N,\vartheta}\in AC([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
and an ODE problem, which are the same as those given in (
11.16
)–(
11.18
).

Now, by multiplying
\eqref{galerkin-n}_{1}
by
(\mu_{j}^{N,\vartheta})_{t}(t)
and summing the
resulting equality with respect to
j
from
1
to
N
, we have

(11.38)

\displaystyle\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}+m\frac{|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\underline{-2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}+m\frac{\bar{U}|w^{N,\vartheta}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r}

\displaystyle\underline{+\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}-(\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}\big)\frac{mw^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}+(\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}-(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\underline{\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{mw^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}+\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{*}(t)}.

Here, for
\mathrm{I}_{1}
, it follows from the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(11.39)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}+|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}\Big)\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+C(T)|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2},(\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2},

and
\mathrm{I}_{*}(t)
can be controlled by

(11.40)

|\mathrm{I}_{*}(t)|\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}\qquad\text{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$}.

Thus, substituting (
11.39
)–(
11.40
) into (
11.38
) with
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we deduce from the Grönwall inequality that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|w^{N,\vartheta}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

For the initial data, based on (iii) of Lemma
11.2
, (
11.37
), and an argument similar to (
11.22
)–(
11.23
), we obtain that there exist
\vartheta_{0}>0
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(\vartheta_{0})\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that, for any
\vartheta\in(0,\vartheta_{0}]
and
N\geq N_{0}
,

\|w^{N,\vartheta}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}\leq 2\|w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}.

Therefore, we arrive at the uniform estimate:

(11.41)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|w^{N,\vartheta}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

3.
Note that, due to (
11.33
), we have

\mathfrak{B}(t)\in C^{1}[0,T],\qquad\mathfrak{c}(t)\in H^{1}(0,T),

where
(\mathfrak{B}(t),\mathfrak{c}(t))
are defined in (
11.19
). Then the classical theory of ODEs (
cf.

[
15
]
) implies that
(\mu^{N,\vartheta})_{t}(t)\in AC([0,T])
, so that
w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}
is differentiable
a.e.
in
t
. As a consequence,
we apply
\partial_{t}
to
\eqref{galerkin-n}_{1}
and obtain

\displaystyle\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{N,\vartheta}_{tt},\xi_{j}\big>+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t},\xi_{j}\big>

\displaystyle-4\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\rangle-4\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\bar{U}w^{N,\vartheta},\xi_{j}\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\frac{m\xi_{j}}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big),D_{\bar{\eta}}\xi_{j}\rangle.

Next, multiplying the above by
(\mu_{j}^{N,\vartheta})_{t}(t)
and summing the
resulting equality with respect to
j
from
1
to
N
, it follows from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|^{2}+m\frac{|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=4\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r+4\mu m\int_{0}^{1}\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\bar{U}w^{N,\vartheta}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+m\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}

\displaystyle+C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}+|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}\Big)\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t}^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w_{t}^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}},

which thus, along with the Young inequality, leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\mu\|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{N,\vartheta},\frac{w^{N,\vartheta}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2},(\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2}+|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}\Big).

Multiplying the above by
t
and integrating the resulting inequality over
[\tau,t]
, together with (
11.41
), we have

(11.42)

\displaystyle t|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}(t)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}s\|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\tau|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}(\tau)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+C(T)\Big(\|w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

Finally, by Lemma
A.6
and the fact that
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
, there exists a sequence
\{\tau_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset[0,T]
such that

\tau_{k}\to 0,\quad\tau_{k}|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}(\tau_{k})|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}\to 0\qquad\,\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

Hence, setting
\tau=\tau_{k}
in (
11.42
) and then letting
\tau_{k}\to 0
, we arrive at

\displaystyle t|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}(t)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}s\|w^{N,\vartheta}_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

4.
(
11.34
) can be derived similarly from the weak convergence argument shown in Step 3 of the proof of Proposition
11.2
. For brevity, we omit the details here.

Next, we show that
\sqrt{t}\rho_{0}w_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
. According to (
11.11
), since
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{0}w_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
, we see that,
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t},\varphi\right>+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle.

Differentiating the above with respect to
t
gives that, for
a.e.

t>0
,

(11.43)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t},\varphi\right>

\displaystyle=\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}((\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})+2\mu r^{m}\rho_{0}(2D_{\bar{\eta}}wD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}-D_{\bar{\eta}}w_{t}),D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle+\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+2\mu r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(\frac{2w\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{w_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(|((\mathfrak{q}_{1})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2})_{t})|_{2}+\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})|_{2}\Big)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|w_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}\Big)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}},

Hence, we multiply the above by
t
to derive

\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big<t(r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}),\varphi\big>\Big|\leq F(t)\|\sqrt{t}\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}\qquad\text{for some $F(t)\in L^{2}(0,T)$},

which, along with Lemma 1.1 on
[
60
]
*page 250, leads to
\sqrt{t}(r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{tt})\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
. This, together with Lemma
A.9
, gives

(11.44)

\big<\sqrt{t}(r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{tt}),\sqrt{t}w_{t}\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(t\|w_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}).

Finally, (
11.35
)–(
11.36
) follow from (
11.43
)–(
11.44
).

This completes the proof of Proposition
11.3
.
∎

11.1.2.
Proof for Lemma
11.1

We now prove Lemma
11.1
. We divide the proof into eight steps.

1. Tangential estimate
U\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
.
First, we let

w^{(0)}|_{t=0}=w_{0}^{(0)}:=u_{0},\qquad\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)}=\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)}:=A\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}},

in (
11.4
). Then we can check that
(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
and
w_{0}^{(0)}\in L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
, thereby using Proposition
11.2
to obtain a unique weak solution
w^{(0)}=U
of (
11.4
) such that

(11.45)

U\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

2. Tangential estimates
U\in C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
and
U_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
.

First, if formally applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of the equation in (
11.4
), we would obtain

(11.46)

r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{tt}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{tr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}=m\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}-\big(\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}\big)_{r},

where

(11.47)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=4\mu(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\ \underline{-A\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\big(\frac{((\gamma-1)m+1)\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}+(\gamma-1)D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)}_{:=\mathfrak{q}_{1*}^{(1)}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=4\mu(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\ \underline{-A\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\big(\frac{(\gamma-1)m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}+\gamma D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)}_{:=\mathfrak{q}_{2*}^{(1)}}.

As a consequence, we regard (
11.46
) as the equation of
w^{(1)}:=U_{t}
and study the problem:

(11.48)

\!\!\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}w_{t}^{(1)}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{w_{r}^{(1)}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{w^{(1)}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}=m\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\bar{\eta}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}-\big(\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}\big)_{r},\\[10.0pt]
w^{(1)}|_{t=0}=w^{(1)}_{0}=U_{t}\qquad\text{on }I.\end{cases}

Clearly,
w^{(1)}_{0}\in L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
and
(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
due to (
11.6
)–(
11.8
) and (
11.45
). Hence, by Proposition
11.2
, (
11.48
) admits a unique weak solution
w^{(1)}
satisfying

(11.49)

w^{(1)}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{(1)}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

Now, we show that
w^{(1)}=U_{t}
for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times I
. Define

(11.50)

\widetilde{U}(t,r):=\int_{0}^{t}w^{(1)}(\tau,r)\,\mathrm{d}\tau+u_{0}(r),\qquad\ Y:=\widetilde{U}-U.

It suffices to show that
Y=0
for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times I
.

Since
U
and
w^{(1)}
are weak solutions of (
11.4
) and (
11.48
), respectively, it follows from (
11.11
) that, for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}}
and
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
,

(11.51)

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t},\varphi\right>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}U,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle,

and

(11.52)

\displaystyle\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}w^{(1)}_{t},\varphi\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}w^{(1)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}w^{(1)},\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle.

Next, due to (
11.50
), we replace
w^{(1)}
in (
11.52
) by
\widetilde{U}_{t}
and integrate the resulting equality over
[0,t]
for
t\in(0,T]
. Using the compatibility condition
\eqref{116}_{1}
in Appendix
C
:

r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t}(0,r)=2\mu(r^{m}\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}-2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{u_{0}}{r^{2}}+Amr^{m-1}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}-A(r^{m}\rho_{0}^{\gamma})_{r},

together with the relations:

\big(\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)_{t}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1*}^{(1)}}{\bar{\eta}},\qquad\big(\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)_{t}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2*}^{(1)}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}},

we arrive at the following equality:

\displaystyle\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}\widetilde{U}_{t},\varphi\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\widetilde{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\widetilde{U},\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle-4\mu\int_{0}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}Y,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>\,\mathrm{d}s-4\mu m\int_{0}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\bar{U}Y}{\bar{\eta}^{3}},\varphi\big>\,\mathrm{d}s.

Subtracting (
11.51
) from the above equality leads to

(11.53)

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}Y_{t},\varphi\right>_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}+2\mu\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}Y,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}Y,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=-4\mu\int_{0}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}Y,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\big>\,\mathrm{d}s-4\mu m\int_{0}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\bar{U}Y}{\bar{\eta}^{3}},\varphi\big>\,\mathrm{d}s.

Thanks to
Y\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
, we set
\varphi=Y
in (
11.53
) and obtain from Lemma
A.4
, the Young inequality, and the similar calculations in (
11.20
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|Y|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{2}+\|Y\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\leq C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+1\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
, together with the strong continuity of
Y
at
t=0
and
Y|_{t=0}=0
, yields that

\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+1\Big)t\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Then it follows from the Grönwall inequality that
\|Y\|_{L^{2}_{t}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})}=0
, and hence
Y=0
.

Consequently, (
11.49
) holds for
U_{t}
,
i.e.
,

(11.54)

U_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

which, together with (
11.45
) and Lemma
A.3
, yields

(11.55)

U\in C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

3. Boundary condition of
U
.
Thanks to (
11.4
) and (
11.11
), we can show that
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times(0,1)
, and
U
satisfies the boundary condition
\rho_{0}U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
. These are crucial to our further analysis.

Lemma 11.4
.

For any
a\in(0,1)
and a.e.
t\in(0,T)
,

(11.56)

r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\in L^{2}(0,a),\qquad\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}\in L^{2}(a,1).

Furthermore, equation
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds for a.e.
(t,r)\in(0,T)\times(0,1)
, and
U
satisfies

(11.57)

U\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}},\qquad\ \rho_{0}U_{r}|_{r=1}=0\quad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Proof.

First, it follows from (
11.11
) in Definition
11.2
, (
11.54
), and the Hölder inequality that, for all
\varphi\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,1])
,

\displaystyle\Big|\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}},\varphi_{r}\big>\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}U,\varphi\big>\Big|+C\big|\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t},\varphi\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+C\Big|\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}},\varphi\big>\Big|+C\big|\big<\big((r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)_{r},\varphi\big>\big|\leq C_{\varphi}|\varphi|_{2},

where
C_{\varphi}>0
is a constant that depends on the support of
\varphi
. This yields that
r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}
admits the weak derivative

\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}\in L^{2}(a,1)\qquad\text{for $a\in(0,1)$ and {a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$},

which, along with (
11.54
) and the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
in (
11.7
), yields that each term in
\eqref{lp}_{1}
belongs to
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, and hence
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times(0,1)
. Moreover, rewrite
\eqref{lp}_{1}
as

(\mathfrak{A}_{1}U_{r})_{r}+\mathfrak{A}_{2}U=\mathfrak{A}_{3},

where

\mathfrak{A}_{1}=2\mu\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}},\quad\mathfrak{A}_{2}=-2\mu m\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}},\quad\mathfrak{A}_{3}=r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t}+m(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}}-\big((r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)_{r}.

As can be checked,
(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\mathfrak{A}_{2})\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathfrak{A}_{3}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
. Hence, it follows from the classical regularity theory of elliptic equations (
cf.

[
22
]
) that
U\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
.

Next, we show
\eqref{div-Uxx}_{1}
. Since
U\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
U_{t}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, and
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times(0,1)
, we multiply
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{-1}
to obtain

(11.58)

D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)=\frac{1}{2\mu}U_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-2}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}-\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}}{\bar{\varrho}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\qquad\bar{\varrho}:=\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}}.

Then we can directly check that, for any
a\in(0,1)
,

\Big|\zeta_{a}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{m}}\leq C\big(|\zeta_{a}U_{t}|_{2,r^{m}}+|\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-2}|_{\infty}|\zeta_{a}\bar{\varrho}_{r}|_{2,r^{m}}+|\zeta_{a}\bar{\varrho}_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{2,r^{m}}\big)\leq C(a,T),

due to (
11.54
)–(
11.55
), the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
in (
11.7
), and the fact that
\varrho^{\beta}\sim 1-r
. Hence, by Lemma
9.1
in §
9
, we derive
\eqref{div-Uxx}_{1}
.

Finally, we give the proof for (
11.57
). Due to the weak formulation (
11.11
), we obtain that, for all
\varphi\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,1])
,

(11.59)

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t},\varphi\right>+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}U,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle.

Moreover, since Lemma
A.3
and (
11.56
) lead
to
\rho_{0}U_{r}\in C([0,T]\times[a,1])
for any
a\in(0,1)
, multiplying
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by such
\varphi
and integrating over
I
yield

(11.60)

\displaystyle\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{t},\varphi\right>+2\mu\langle r^{m}\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle+2\mu m\big<\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}U,\varphi\big>

\displaystyle=2\mu\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\varphi\Big|_{r=1}+\big<(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(0)},\frac{m\varphi}{\bar{\eta}}\big>+\langle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(0)},D_{\bar{\eta}}\varphi\rangle.

Comparing (
11.59
)–(
11.60
), together with
(\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
for
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}
, we have

r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{r}\varphi\big|_{r=1}=0\qquad\text{for all $\varphi\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,1])$},

which implies that
\rho_{0}U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
. This completes the proof of Lemma
11.4
.
∎

4. Tangential estimates
U_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})
and time-weighted estimates.

We continue to improve the regularity of
U
. First, we claim that

(11.61)

\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\infty}(a,1))\qquad\text{for $a\in(0,1)$}.

Indeed, since
\rho_{0}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\in C[a,1]
for
a\in(0,1)
due to Lemmas
11.4
and
A.3
, by integrating (
11.58
) over
[r,1]
(
r\in(0,1)
), it follows from a similar argument in Lemma
9.2
, the fact that

1-r\sim\rho_{0}^{\beta}\in H^{3}(a,1),\qquad\beta\leq\gamma-1,

(
11.54
), (
11.57
), Lemma
A.4
, and the Hölder inequality that

(11.62)

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}U(t,r)=\frac{A}{2\mu}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}+\frac{m}{\bar{\varrho}}\int_{r}^{1}\bar{\varrho}\big(\frac{U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{U\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}-\frac{1}{2\mu\bar{\varrho}}\int_{r}^{1}\bar{\varrho}\bar{\eta}_{r}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

\displaystyle\implies\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{\infty}\leq C(a)\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\leq C(a,T).

This implies claim (
11.61
).

Next, by direct calculation,
w^{(1)}(0,r)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}
and
(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
due to (
11.6
)–(
11.8
), and (
11.45
), it still remains to show that

(11.63)

((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}).

To obtain this, we first have

(11.64)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t}&=4\mu(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{U\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}+\frac{U_{t}\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{2U\bar{U}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\big)\\
&\quad-A\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\Big(((\gamma-1)m+1)\big(\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{\bar{U}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)+(\gamma-1)(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}-|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|^{2})\Big)\\
&\quad+A(\gamma-1)\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\big(\frac{((\gamma-1)m+1)\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}+(\gamma-1)D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\end{aligned}

(11.65)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t}&=4\mu(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}+D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}-2D_{\bar{\eta}}U|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|^{2}\big)\\
&\quad-A\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\Big((\gamma-1)m\big(\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{\bar{U}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\big)+\gamma(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}-|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|^{2})\Big)\\
&\quad+A(\gamma-1)\frac{(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}}{(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{\gamma-1}}\big(\frac{(\gamma-1)m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}+\gamma D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big).\end{aligned}

Then, with the help of (
11.61
), we can obtain (
11.63
) by using the regularities of
(\bar{\eta},\bar{U})
, the facts that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
and
\beta>\frac{1}{3}
, (
11.54
)–(
11.55
), and Lemmas
11.4
and
A.4
, for example,

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{4}\big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{4}+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(\big|\chi r^{\frac{m+3}{4}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U)\big|_{2}\big|\chi r^{\frac{m+3}{4}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{3\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t})\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(1+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}),

which implies that
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}UD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
due to
\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})\in L^{1}(0,T)
. The remaining calculation is straightforward.

Therefore, from Proposition
11.3
, it follows that the weak solution
U_{t}
of (
11.48
) satisfies (
11.35
)–(
11.36
) with
(w,\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})
replaced by
(U_{t},\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})
and

(11.66)

\displaystyle U_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad U_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}U_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\quad\sqrt{t}(r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{ttt})\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

Moreover, using a similar argument in Lemma
11.4
, we can deduce from (
11.35
) that (
11.46
) holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T)\times(0,1)
, and for any
a\in(0,1)
and
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
,

(11.67)

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\in L^{2}(0,a),\qquad\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U_{tr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)_{r}\in L^{2}(a,1),

\displaystyle U_{t}\in H^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}},\qquad U\in H^{4}_{\mathrm{loc}},\qquad\rho_{0}U_{tr}|_{r=1}=0.

In summary, collecting (
11.45
), (
11.54
)–(
11.55
), and (
11.66
), we arrive at all the tangential estimates for
U
:

(11.68)

\displaystyle U\in C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad U_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{\infty}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

\displaystyle U_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad\sqrt{t}U_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),

\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}),\qquad\sqrt{t}(r^{m}\rho_{0}U_{ttt})\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}).

5. Total energy and dissipation estimates for
U
.
With the help of (
11.68
), we now can show that

(11.69)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)\in L^{1}(0,T),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)\in L^{1}(0,T).

In fact, the regularity properties that
U\in H^{4}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
U_{t}\in H^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, which follow from (
11.67
), allow us to freely apply
D_{\bar{\eta}}
,
D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}
, or
\partial_{t}
to (
11.58
). This process can then be used to rigorously obtain higher spatial regularity for
U
. However, the specific calculation above is rather tedious; we will provide the details in Lemmas
11.9
–
11.10
of §
11.2.2
, where the method can be applied here in a similar manner.

In fact, the major difference here, compared with the calculations in Lemmas
11.9
–
11.10
, is on establishing

(11.70)

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\qquad\sqrt{t}\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Note that the derivations of
\eqref{eequ3.66}_{1}
and
\eqref{eequ3.66}_{2}
are identical, we only sketch the proof of
\eqref{eequ3.66}_{1}
. Thus, assume that
\eqref{bbbb}_{1}
holds, and the following estimates for
U
in the
exterior domain have been derived:

(11.71)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)\in L^{\infty}(0,T),\qquad(\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt},\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}).

First, we can multiply (
11.58
) by
\varrho^{\beta}
and then apply
\varrho^{-\beta}\bar{\eta}_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}
to the resulting equation to obtain

(11.72)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}=\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}(t,r):=(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)_{r}+\big(\frac{1}{\beta}+2\big)\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\rho_{0}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U=\sum_{i=12}^{15}\mathrm{I}_{i},

where
\mathrm{I}_{i}
(
i=12,13,14,15
) is defined in (
11.144
) of §
11.2.2
. As can be checked,
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{i}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
for
i=12,13,14,15
(see Lemma
11.10
for details), and hence

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}).

Consequently, once Proposition
D.1
in Appendix
D
can be utilized, we obtain the desired conclusion immediately.
However, Proposition
D.1
is not applicable in this case, since it requires one
a priori
assumption:

(11.73)

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

which can not be implied by (
11.71
) directly, unless
\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{2\beta-1}{2\beta}
.

Therefore, to derive
\eqref{eequ3.66}_{1}
, we need to first obtain (
11.73
).
To achieve this, we claim that

(11.74)

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Indeed, based on
\eqref{3...136}_{1}
, using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
9.2
, we can obtain that, for all
\iota\in(-\frac{\beta}{2},1+\frac{\beta}{2})
and
\sigma>0
,

(11.75)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota-\beta+\sigma}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\leq C(\sigma,\iota,T)\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Hence, choosing

\iota=\frac{1}{2}-\sigma\qquad\text{with some fixed $\sigma<\min\big\{\frac{1+\beta}{2},\beta\big\}$},

then we derive from (
11.75
) and Lemma
A.4
that

(11.76)

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma+2\beta}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C(T)\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma+\beta}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}\leq C(T)(1+\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}),

which, along with (
11.71
), leads to (
11.74
).

We continue to show (
11.73
). Multiplying (
11.58
) by
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and applying the
L^{2}
-norm to the resulting equality, we can deduce from
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, (
11.71
), (
11.74
), and the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
in (
11.7
) that

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{t},U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U)\big|_{2}+(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}+|\bar{\varrho}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1-\beta})|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}|_{\infty}\big)\leq C(T),

that is,
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. Similarly, based on this and (
11.74
), applying
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}
to (
11.58
), we can further obtain

(11.77)

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Next, multiplying (
11.58
) by
\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}
and then applying
\bar{\varrho}^{-\beta}\bar{\eta}_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}
to the resulting equality, we arrive at the following type of equation:

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}:=(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U)_{r}+\big(\frac{1}{\beta}+1\big)\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\rho_{0}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U=\mathrm{I}_{2}+\mathrm{I}_{3},

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\mu}\eta_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}+\frac{1}{2\mu}\frac{(\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}U_{t}-m\eta_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)-m\frac{(\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}U-(1+\beta)\frac{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}}{r^{m}}\big(\frac{r^{m}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})_{r}+\frac{A\gamma(\gamma-1)}{2\mu\beta^{2}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}(\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}.

After a direct calculation by using
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
,
\beta\leq\gamma-1
, (
11.71
), Lemma
A.4
, and the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
in (
11.7
), we can check that

\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}(\mathrm{I}_{2},\mathrm{I}_{3})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})\implies\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

which, along with (
11.77
) and the fact that
\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
and Proposition
D.1
, leads to
\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
, so that

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|(\zeta-\chi)\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}.

This, together with
\eqref{bbbb}_{1}
, yields the desired estimate (
11.73
), and thus yields
\eqref{eequ3.66}_{1}
.

6. Regularity of
U
given in (
11.9
).
Now, we can show that

(11.78)

\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I})),\qquad\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r},U_{t}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I})).

6.1. Regularity of
U
near the origin.
In this step, we aim to show that

(11.79)

\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\flat})),\qquad\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\flat})),

where
I_{\flat}:=[0,\frac{1}{2})
, and the regularity of
U_{t}
can be derived similarly.

To obtain this, define the M-D representative of
U
as

\bm{U}(t,\bm{y})=U(t,r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}.

First, using
\eqref{bbbb}_{1}
, Lemma
B.1
, and Lemma
C.1
in Appendix
C
, we have

\zeta\bm{U}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}_{0}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}_{0}(\Omega)),\qquad\zeta\bm{U}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{2}_{0}(\Omega)).

Here,
\zeta
is read as
\zeta(\bm{y})=\zeta(r)
defined on
\Omega
. Then this, together with Lemma
A.8
, implies

\zeta\bm{U}\in C([0,T];H^{3}_{0}(\Omega)).

Using Lemma
B.1
again, we obtain from the above that

r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r},U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r},U_{rrr},(\frac{U}{r})_{rr}\big)\in C([0,T];L^{2}(I_{\flat})),

which, along with Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
,
implies

\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r},U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r}\big)\in C([0,T];L^{2}(I_{\flat}))\implies\big(U,U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\flat})).

Next, similarly, it follows from
\eqref{bbbb}_{1}
and Lemmas
B.1
and
C.1
that

t\zeta\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}_{0}(\Omega)),\qquad(t\zeta\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega),

which, along with Lemma
A.8
, leads to

t\zeta\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U}\in C([0,T];W^{1,4}(\Omega)).

This, together with Lemma
B.1
, implies

(11.80)

r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r},U_{rrr},(\frac{U}{r})_{rr}\big)\in C((0,T];L^{4}(I_{\flat})).

On the other hand, since, for any function
f=f(r)
,

|f|_{1}\leq|r^{-\frac{m}{4}}r^{\frac{m}{4}}f|_{1}\leq|r^{-\frac{m}{4}}|_{\frac{4}{3}}|r^{\frac{m}{4}}f|_{4}\leq C|r^{\frac{m}{4}}f|_{4},

we can derive from the above, (
11.80
), and Lemma
A.3
that

\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r}\big)\in C((0,T];W^{1,1}(I_{\flat}))\implies\big(U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\flat})),

which completes the proof of (
11.79
).

6.2. Regularity of
U
away from the origin.
Define
I_{\sharp}:=(\frac{1}{2},1)
. To obtain (
11.78
), it still remains to show

(11.81)

U\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\bar{I}_{\sharp})),\qquad(U_{rr},U_{t})\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\sharp})).

First, it follows from (
11.68
) that

(11.82)

\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U,U_{r},U_{t})\in C([0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Next, note that, for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, due to the fact that

\big(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta>-\frac{\beta}{2},

we obtain from (
11.69
) and Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
and
C.1
that

\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U_{rr},U_{rrr})\in H^{1}(I_{\sharp})\implies\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U_{rr},U_{rrr})\in C(\bar{I}_{\sharp}),

which, by using (
11.69
) again, leads to

\displaystyle\zeta_{\frac{1}{3}}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(U_{rr},U_{rrr})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{0}),

\displaystyle\zeta_{\frac{1}{3}}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(U_{trr},U_{trrr})\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}).

Then it follows from the above and Lemma
A.8
that

(11.83)

\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(U_{rr},U_{rrr})\in C([0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Hence,
\eqref{classical-ex}_{1}
follows from (
11.82
)–(
11.83
) and Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
:

U\in C([0,T];W^{2,1}(I_{\sharp}))\implies U\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\bar{I}_{\sharp})).

It remains to prove
\eqref{classical-ex}_{2}
. To this end, we first obtain from (
11.68
) that

\displaystyle tU_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\cap L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{1}),\qquad r^{m}\rho_{0}(tU_{tt})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}}^{-1}),

which, along with (
11.69
) and Lemmas
A.3
and
A.9
, gives

(11.84)

t(U_{tr},U_{tt})\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}})\implies\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{tr},U_{tt})\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Now, following an argument similar to the proof of
\sqrt{t}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp}))

(this proof is omitted in Step 5 above; see Steps 2.1 and 3 in the proof of Lemma
11.10
below for details),
with the help of time-continuities (
11.82
)–(
11.84
) and the regularities of
(\bar{U},\bar{\eta})
in (
11.6
)–(
11.7
), we can also arrive at

\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})),

and then additionally utilize the chain rules to obtain

(11.85)

\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}U_{trr}\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Finally, recalling (
11.72
), we similarly define

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{*}=\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}^{*}(t,r):=U_{rrrr}+\big(\frac{1}{\beta}+2\big)\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\rho_{0}^{\beta}}U_{rrr}.

Clearly, based on (
11.83
)–(
11.85
), the chain rules and the regularity of
\bar{\eta}
in (
11.7
), we can follow a similar argument in Steps 2.2 and 3 of Lemma
11.10
(this argument is actually contained in Step 5 above, however, we omit it) and obtain the time-continuity for
\bar{\mathcal{T}}^{*}_{\mathrm{cross}}
,
i.e.
,

\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}\bar{\mathcal{T}}^{*}_{\mathrm{cross}}\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Then, by Proposition
D.1
, for any
0<t,t_{0}\leq T
,

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(U_{rrrr}(t)-U_{rrrr}(t_{0}))\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}(t)-\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}(t_{0}))\big|_{2}

\displaystyle+C(T)\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}(U_{rrr}(t)-U_{rrr}(t_{0}))\big|_{2}.

Taking the limit as
t\to t_{0}
, together with (
11.83
), yields

(11.86)

\rho_{0}^{\frac{(3-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}{2}}U_{rrrr}\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I_{\sharp})).

Therefore,
\eqref{classical-ex}_{2}
follows from (
11.83
), (
11.84
)–(
11.86
), and Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
:

(U_{rr},U_{t})\in C((0,T];W^{1,1}(I_{\sharp}))\implies(U_{rr},U_{t})\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}_{\sharp})).

This completes the proof of (
11.81
), and hence the proof of (
11.78
).

7. Derivation of the boundary condition.
The Neumann boundary condition of
U
can be proved by basically follow the argument used in Remark
2.4
of §
2
. First, since
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times(0,1)
, we can divide
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}
to obtain

(11.87)

\bar{\varrho}U_{t}+AD_{\bar{\eta}}(\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma})=2\mu\bar{\varrho}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U+2\mu(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho})(D_{\bar{\eta}}U)+2\mu m\bar{\varrho}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),\qquad\bar{\varrho}=\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}}.

Then multiplying the above by
\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{-1}
gives

(11.88)

\displaystyle\frac{2\mu}{\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}U_{r}

\displaystyle=\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}\rho_{0}^{\beta}U_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{\beta}\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}\Big((\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}-\beta\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big(m\frac{r}{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}+\frac{\bar{\eta}_{rr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-2m\mu\bar{\eta}_{r}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big((\frac{U}{r})_{r}\frac{r}{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{U}{r}\frac{r^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}\big)-2\mu\bar{\eta}_{r}\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big(\frac{U_{rr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}-\frac{\bar{\eta}_{rr}U_{r}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+2\mu\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big(m\frac{r}{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}+\frac{\bar{\eta}_{rr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)U_{r}.

Due to the facts that

(11.89)

\displaystyle\big(U_{r},\frac{U}{r},U_{rr},(\frac{U}{r})_{r},\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r},\bar{\eta}_{rr},(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}\big)\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I})),

\displaystyle\big(\bar{\eta}_{r},\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r}\big)\in\big[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\big],\qquad\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r,\qquad(\rho_{0},(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r})\in C(\bar{I}),

we see that the right-hand side of (
11.88
) belongs to
C((0,T]\times\bar{I})
and vanishes at the boundary
\partial I
. As a consequence, taking the limit as
r\to 1
in (
11.88
), we obtain from
(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{r=1}\neq 0
that

(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}U_{r}|_{r=1}=\lim_{r\to 1}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}U_{r}=0\implies U_{r}|_{r=1}=0.

Next,
U|_{r=0}=0
follows directly from
\frac{U}{r}\in C([0,T];C(\bar{I}))
. Finally, for any
(t,r)\in(0,T]\times\bar{I}
, by
U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
and
U_{rr}\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}))
,

(11.90)

|U_{r}(t,r)|=\Big|\int_{r}^{1}U_{rr}(t,\tilde{r})\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\Big|\leq|U_{rr}|_{\infty}(1-r)\leq C(T)(1-r).

8. Equation
\eqref{lp}_{1}
holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
.
By Definition
11.1
, it remains to show that
U(t,r)
satisfies equation
\eqref{lp}_{1}
pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
. However, to make the method in this step applicable to the well-posedness of the nonlinear problem (
2.2
), we consider a more general case here. More precisely, we show that
\eqref{lp}_{1}\times(\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r})^{-1}
, that is, (
11.87
) holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
. Furthermore, based on the structure of (
11.87
), it suffices to show that the “most singular” term

\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}(t,r):=2\mu(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\varrho})(D_{\bar{\eta}}U)

holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
.

A direct calculation gives

(11.91)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle=-2\mu\big(m\frac{r}{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\eta}}{r})_{r}+\frac{\bar{\eta}_{rr}}{\bar{\eta}_{r}}\big)\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}^{3}}U_{r}+\frac{2\mu}{\beta}\frac{r^{m}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}^{3}}\underline{\rho_{0}^{1-\beta}U_{r}}_{:=\mathbb{S}_{*}(t,r)}.

From (
11.89
), it follows that all the terms in (
11.91
) belong to
C((0,T]\times\bar{I})
, hence holding pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
, except for the term
\mathbb{S}_{*}
.

To prove that
\mathbb{S}_{*}
holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times\bar{I}
, we only need to check

(11.92)

\mathbb{S}_{*}|_{r=1}<\infty\qquad\text{on }(0,T].

Indeed, since
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
and
U_{r}|_{r=1}=0
, by (
11.90
), we obtain that, for all
(t,r)\in(0,T]\times I
,

|\mathbb{S}_{*}(t,r)|\leq C(T)|(1-r)^{\frac{1}{\beta}-1}U_{r}|\leq C(T)(1-r)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}.

Letting
r\to 1
leads to
|\mathbb{S}_{*}(t,r)|\to 0
for each
t\in(0,T]
. Hence, we derive (
11.92
).

This completes the proof of Lemma
11.1
.

11.2.
Uniform estimates to the linearized problem

Based on Lemma
11.1
, we can establish the uniform estimates
for the classical solution
U
of problem (
11.4
).

In §
11.2
–§
11.3
,
C\in(1,\infty)
denotes a generic constant depending only on
(n,\mu,A,\gamma,\beta,\varepsilon_{0})
, and
C(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k})\in(1,\infty)
a generic constant depending on
C

and the additional parameters
(l_{1},\cdots\!,l_{k})
, which may be different at each occurrence. Moreover, for simplicity, we denote
\mathfrak{p}(\cdot)
a generic polynomial function, taking the form:

\mathfrak{p}(s)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}s^{j}\qquad\text{with some $k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

Now, let
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
be given initial data satisfying the hypothesis of Lemma
11.1
.
Assume that
there exists a constant
c_{0}>1
such that

(11.93)

1+\mathcal{K}_{1}+\mathcal{K}_{2}+\|\rho_{0}^{\beta}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{E}(0,U)\leq c_{0}.

Then fix
T>0
, and assume that there exist some constants
T^{*}\in(0,T]
and
c_{1}
such that
1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}
and, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

(11.94)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,\bar{U})+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,\bar{U})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\qquad|\bar{\eta}_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\bar{\eta}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Here,
(c_{1},T^{*})
will be determined later, which depend only on
(c_{0},\varepsilon_{0},\beta,\mu,n,A,\gamma,T)
.

11.2.1.
Some basic estimates

First, we have the following estimates associated with
\bar{U}
.

Lemma 11.5
.

For any
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

\Big|\big(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\qquad\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}),\bar{U}_{t}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}),

and, for any
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\Big|\chi_{a}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\qquad\Big|\chi_{a}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t},\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\leq C(a)(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{\beta}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},\bar{U}_{t})\big|_{\infty}\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\qquad\big|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{\infty}\leq C(a)(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}).

Proof.

The proof can be directly derived by (
11.94
) and Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
. For example, it follows that, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
and
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\ \begin{aligned} \Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{\infty}&\leq C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}),D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{1}\\
