&\leq C\sum_{j=2}^{4}\Big(\big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}\bar{U}\big|_{2}+\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j-1}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\Big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}\bar{U}\big|_{2}\Big)\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Big|\chi_{a}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t},\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}&\leq C(a)\Big|\chi_{a}r^{\frac{m+3}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t},\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\\
&\leq C(a)(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ \big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C(a)\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{3\beta}{2}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}_{t})\big|_{2}\leq C(a)(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}).

The rest of this lemma can be proved analogously, we omit the details here for brevity.
∎

Next, to further simplify the calculations, we define the quantities:

(11.95)

\bar{\Lambda}:=D_{\bar{\eta}}(\bar{\varrho}^{\beta})=D_{\bar{\eta}}(\rho_{0}^{\beta}\bar{\mathscr{J}}^{-\beta}),\qquad\bar{\mathscr{J}}:=\frac{\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}}{r^{m}}.

Then we obtain some useful estimates for
(\bar{\Lambda},\bar{\mathscr{J}})
.

Lemma 11.6
.

For any
0\leq t\leq T_{1}=\min\{T^{*},\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})^{-1}\}
,
a\in(0,1)
, and
\sigma>0
,

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta_{a}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda},\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}+\Big|\zeta_{a}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda},\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}|_{\infty}+|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{a}\rho_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2}+\sigma}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}\leq C(a,\sigma)\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad|\zeta_{a}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{4}+|\chi_{a}^{\sharp}\Lambda_{t}|_{\infty}\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Proof.

By (
11.5
) and (
11.93
)–(
11.94
), we see that, for all
(t,r)\in[0,T^{*}]\times\bar{I}
,

(11.96)

\bar{\mathscr{J}}_{t}=\bar{\mathscr{J}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\qquad C^{-1}\leq\bar{\mathscr{J}}(t,r)\leq C.

Then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}})_{t}&=D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}+\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\\
(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\mathscr{J}})_{t}&=D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\mathscr{J}}\big(\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}-D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)+D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{2m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{\mathscr{J}})_{t}&=D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{\mathscr{J}}\big(\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}-2D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)+D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{3m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\\
&\quad+D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(2D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{3m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\bar{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big).\end{aligned}

Hence, these, together with (
11.96
) and Lemma
11.5
, yield

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}|&\leq Ce^{Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\mathscr{J}}|&\leq Ce^{2Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+e^{Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s,\end{aligned}

(11.97)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{\mathscr{J}}|&\leq Ce^{3Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{3}\\
&\quad+Ce^{2Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
&\quad+Ce^{Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{aligned}

On the other hand, by the chain rules, we have

(11.98)

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}^{k}\bar{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}C_{k,j}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{k+1-j}\rho_{0}^{\beta})D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}(\bar{\mathscr{J}}^{-\beta})\qquad\text{for $k=0,1,2$},

\displaystyle\bar{\Lambda}_{t}

\displaystyle=\beta\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\beta\bar{\Lambda}\big((\beta-1)D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big),

where
C_{k,j}
are some constants depend only on
(k,j)
.

Therefore, for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}:=\min\{T^{*},\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})^{-1}\}
, combining (
11.97
)–(
11.98
), together with (
11.93
)–(
11.94
), (
11.96
), Lemmas
11.5
and
A.3
–
A.4
, and the Hölder and Minkowski inequalities, we can recursively obtain the desired estimates of this lemma. The calculation is tedious, but rather direct.
∎

11.2.2.
Uniform estimates of
U

The proof will be divided into the following several lemmas.

Lemma 11.7
.

For all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}=\min\{T_{1},\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})^{-1}\}
.

\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}},U_{t}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
Multiplying
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
U
and integrating the resulting equation over
I
,
then we obtain from (
11.93
)–(
11.94
) and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu m\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=A\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})+\frac{\mu}{8}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
, along with (
11.93
), implies that, for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

(11.99)

\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\bar{\mathcal{E}}(0,U)+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})t)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.

Multiplying
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
U_{t}
and integrating the resulting equation over
I
, we obtain from (
11.93
)–(
11.94
), Lemma
11.5
, and the Young inequality that

(11.100)

\displaystyle\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}^{2}+m\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big)+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|D_{\bar{\eta}}U|^{2}+m\frac{\bar{U}|U|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r+A\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\eta}^{m}\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}+\frac{mU_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})+\frac{\mu}{8}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\Big(\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+1\Big)+\frac{\mu}{8}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Next, recalling
(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})
in (
11.47
), we derive from (
11.93
)–(
11.94
) and Lemma
11.5
that

(11.101)

\displaystyle|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+|\bar{\varrho}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big)\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}.

Then multiplying (
11.46
) by
U_{t}
and integrating the resulting equation over
I
, combined with (
11.101
) and the Young inequality, imply

(11.102)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu m\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}\frac{mU_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}+\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\mu}{8}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2},

which, along with (
11.93
), (
11.99
)–(
11.100
), and the Grönwall inequality, implies that, for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}=\min\{T_{1},\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})^{-1}\}
,

(11.103)

\displaystyle\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}(\bar{\mathcal{E}}(0,U)+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.8
.

For all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}},\sqrt{t}U_{tt}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(U_{tt},sD_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},s\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
First, it follows from (
11.58
) and (
11.95
) that

(11.104)

D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)=\frac{1}{2\mu}U_{t}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\bar{\Lambda}.

Then, due to the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, (
11.93
), and Lemmas
9.1
and
11.6
–
11.7
, we see that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.105)

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{t}|_{2}+(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{2}+|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}|_{2})|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),

which, along with Lemma
A.4
, also leads to

(11.106)

\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m+3}{4}}\big(\zeta D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\zeta\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}},\zeta_{r}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\zeta_{r}\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}},\zeta D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,\zeta D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Next, using (
11.99
), (
11.103
), and the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
9.2
, we obtain that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.107)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Of course, based on (
11.103
), we also derive from (
11.101
) that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

(11.108)

|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Finally, recall
((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})
in (
11.64
)–(
11.65
). It then follows from (
11.93
)–(
11.94
), (
11.106
), Lemmas
11.5
and
11.7
, and the Hölder inequality that

(11.109)

\displaystyle\ \begin{aligned} \big|\chi((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})\big|_{2}&\leq\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t},\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\\
&\quad+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\\
&\quad+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}^{2}\\
&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big),\end{aligned}

(11.110)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})\big|_{2}&\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})+|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U|_{2}(|\bar{U}_{t}|_{\infty}+|\bar{U}|_{\infty}^{2})+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}|(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}\\
&\quad+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}_{t}\big|_{2}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{\infty}\\
&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big).\end{aligned}

2.
Now, multiplying (
11.46
) by
U_{tt}
and integrating over
I
, we have

(11.111)

\displaystyle\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\underline{\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}+m\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\Big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathcal{F}(t)}\Big)+\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{-2\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|^{2}+m\frac{\bar{U}|U_{t}|^{2}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r}

\displaystyle\underline{+\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}-(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t}\big)\frac{mU_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}+(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}-(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\underline{\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}\frac{mU_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}+\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{**}(t)}.

For
\mathrm{I}_{4}
, it follows from (
11.108
)–(
11.110
), Lemma
11.5
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

(11.112)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\mathcal{F}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\!+\!\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})|_{2}\!+|((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})|_{2}\Big)\mathcal{F}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})(1+\mathcal{F}(t))+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and
\mathrm{I}_{**}(t)
can be handled by

(11.113)

|\mathrm{I}_{**}(t)|\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})+\varepsilon\mathcal{F}(t)\qquad\text{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$}.

Thus, substituting (
11.112
)–(
11.113
) into (
11.111
) with
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we can deduce from the Grönwall inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\mathcal{F}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}(s)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big).

To further simplified the above inequality, define

\mathcal{Y}(t)=C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}(s)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big).

Then
\mathcal{F}(t)\leq\mathcal{Y}(t)
and

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(t)=C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{Y}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Clearly, this, together with (
11.94
), implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\mathcal{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\leq\mathcal{Y}^{\frac{1}{2}}(0)+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,\bar{U})^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})(c_{1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),

which yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.114)

\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

3.
Since we have shown that
U
satisfies (
11.36
) with
(w,\mathfrak{q}_{1},\mathfrak{q}_{2})
replaced by
(U_{t},\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})
in Step 4 of §
11.1.2
, we have

(11.115)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\mu m\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{4\mu\int_{0}^{1}r^{m}\rho_{0}\Big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt}+m\frac{\bar{U}U_{t}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}^{3}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{5}}

\displaystyle+\underline{\int_{0}^{1}(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\big((\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt}+m\big((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t}-\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)}\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathrm{I}_{6}},

where, by using (
11.108
)–(
11.110
), (
11.114
), Lemma
11.5
, the Hölder and Young inequalities,

(11.116)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathrm{I}_{5}&\leq C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\\
&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\frac{\mu}{100}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2},\end{aligned}

(11.117)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathrm{I}_{6}&\leq C\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})|_{2}\!+|((\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{(1)})_{t},(\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{(1)})_{t})|_{2}\Big)\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U}))+\frac{\mu}{100}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.\end{aligned}

Thus, plugging (
11.116
)–(
11.117
) into (
11.115
), we obtain from (
11.94
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(t\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)+\mu t\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{tt},\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ct(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,\bar{U}))\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),

which, along with the same argument as for (
9.98
)–(
9.99
)
in §
9
, yields the desired results.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.118)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+\Big|(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)(t)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Boundedness of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)
.
It only remains to establish the third-order elliptic estimate for
U
near the origin. First, it follows from (
11.58
) that

(11.119)

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mu}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\varrho}^{2\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U

\displaystyle+\frac{A\gamma(\gamma-1-\beta)}{2\mu\beta^{2}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}\bar{\Lambda}^{2}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}.

Then it follows from the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, (
11.93
), and Lemmas
9.1
and
11.6
–
11.7
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.120)

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}+\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},\frac{\bar{U}_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}+\Big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda},\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{4}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(|\Lambda|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-2\beta})\big|_{2}+\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda},\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}|\bar{\varrho}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2. Boundedness of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)
.
It only remains to establish the second- and third-order elliptic estimates for
U
away from the origin.

2.1.
First, rewrite (
11.104
) as

(11.121)

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U=\frac{1}{2\mu}U_{t}-mD_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\bar{\Lambda}.

Then it follows from the above, (
11.93
), and Lemmas
11.6
–
11.7
that, for all
\varepsilon>0
,

(11.122)

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}(U_{t},U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U)\big|_{2}+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2}+\varepsilon}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}-\beta+\varepsilon}\big|_{2}|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\leq C(\varepsilon)\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Clearly, since
(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta>\frac{1}{2}
, the above also leads to

(11.123)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.2.

Next, we can obtain similarly from (
11.75
) that, for all
\iota\in(-\frac{\beta}{2},1+\frac{\beta}{2})
and
\sigma>0
,

(11.124)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota-\beta+\sigma}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\leq C(\sigma,\iota)\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\iota}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T_{2}]$}.

Then, due to the facts that

\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{2},\qquad\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta>\frac{1}{2},

we can choose fixed
(\iota,\sigma)
in (
11.75
) such that

\iota+\sigma=\big(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta,\qquad\iota\in\big(-\frac{\beta}{2},1+\frac{\beta}{2}\big),\qquad 0<\sigma<\min\big\{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\beta,\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta-\frac{1}{2}\big\}.

Hence, it follows from
(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta-\sigma>\frac{1}{2}
, (
11.123
), and Lemmas
11.7
–
11.8
and
A.4
that

(11.125)

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{-(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta-\sigma}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta-\sigma}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Finally, based on (
11.121
), using an argument similar to (
9.76
)–(
9.77
) in Lemma
9.13
with
\eta
replaced by
\bar{\eta}
, together with (
11.125
) and Lemmas
11.6
–
11.8
, yields that, for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

(11.126)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.3.
Based on (
11.119
), using a similar argument in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
9.15
with
\eta
replaced by
\bar{\eta}
, we obtain from (
11.123
)–(
11.126
) and Lemmas
11.6
–
11.8
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.127)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, leads to the desired result of this lemma.

3.
Finally, it follows from (
11.105
), (
11.120
), (
11.123
), (
11.127
), and Lemmas
11.7
–
11.8
and
A.3
–
A.4
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\Big|(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\Big|(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{3}\big(\big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}U\big|_{2}\big)+C\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{j}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\Big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.10
.

For all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
L^{1}(0,T)
-boundedness of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)
.

1.1.

First, it follows from (
11.104
) by applying
\partial_{t}
that

(11.128)

D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}+\frac{mU_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)=\sum_{i=7}^{9}\mathrm{I}_{i},

where

(11.129)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{7}

\displaystyle:=D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}+2D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U+m\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{8}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\mu}U_{tt}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{t}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U-\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}\Big(\big(\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{1}{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{9}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{A\gamma(\gamma-1-\beta)}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\bar{\Lambda}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}.

Then, by the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, (
11.93
), Lemmas
11.5
–
11.6
, and the Hölder inequality, we have

(11.130)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathrm{I}_{7}|_{2}&\leq C\Big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{4}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{4}\\
&\quad+C\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\end{aligned}

(11.131)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathrm{I}_{8}|_{2}&\leq C|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{tt}|_{2}+C|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{4}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{4}\\
&\quad+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\Big(\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|_{2}\Big)\leq C(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{tt}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})),\end{aligned}

(11.132)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathrm{I}_{9}|_{2}\leq C|\bar{\varrho}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1-\beta}\Big(|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U},\frac{\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{4}\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).\end{aligned}

Hence, collecting (
11.128
) and (
11.130
)–(
11.132
), together with Lemmas
9.1
and
11.8
, gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.133)

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

1.2.
It follows from (
11.119
) by applying
D_{\bar{\eta}}
that

(11.134)

D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)=\sum_{i=10}^{11}\mathrm{I}_{i},

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{10}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\mu}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{3}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{2\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U-\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{3}}{\bar{\varrho}^{3\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U+\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\varrho}^{2\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{11}

\displaystyle:=\frac{A\gamma(\gamma-1-\beta)(\gamma-1-2\beta)}{2\mu\beta^{3}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-3\beta}\bar{\Lambda}^{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{3A\gamma(\gamma-1-\beta)}{2\mu\beta^{2}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}.

Then, due to the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, (
11.93
), (
11.106
), Lemmas
11.6
and
11.9
, and the Hölder inequality, we obtain that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.135)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathrm{I}_{10}|_{2}&\leq C|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+C\big(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{3}\big)|D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}\\
&\quad\,+C\big(\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{4}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{2}\big)|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{4}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U|_{4}+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U|_{2}\\
&\leq C(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})),\end{aligned}

(11.136)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathrm{I}_{11}|_{2}&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{3}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}+|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}\big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).\end{aligned}

Hence, collecting (
11.134
)–(
11.136
) gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.137)

\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})).

Next, we multiply (
11.104
) by
\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}
and apply
D_{\bar{\eta}}
to the resulting equality to obtain

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mu}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})-\frac{1}{\beta}\Big(D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}})\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}U}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}-\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}\bar{\varrho}^{2\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{A\gamma(\gamma-1-\beta)}{2\mu\beta^{2}}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}).

Then, following the calculations (
11.134
)–(
11.137
), we can similarly obtain

(11.138)

\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{mU}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\Big(\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\Big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big).

Finally, combining (
11.137
)–(
11.138
), along with Lemma
9.1
and (
11.133
), implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.139)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.
L^{1}(0,T)
-boundedness of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)
.

2.1.
First, applying
\partial_{t}
to
\eqref{3...136}_{1}
yields

\displaystyle D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{A(\gamma-1)}{2\mu}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\frac{m}{\bar{\varrho}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\int_{r}^{1}\tilde{r}^{m}\rho_{0}\big(\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}U}{\bar{\eta}^{m+1}}-\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}^{m+2}}\big)\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{\bar{\varrho}}\int_{r}^{1}\tilde{r}^{m}\rho_{0}\big(\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}-D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}U}{\bar{\eta}^{m+1}}-\frac{(m+1)\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}U+U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}^{m+2}}+\frac{(m+2)\bar{U}U}{\bar{\eta}^{m+3}}\big)\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\mu\bar{\varrho}}\Big(\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{m\bar{U}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\tilde{r}^{m}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}}\rho_{0}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}+\int_{r}^{1}\tilde{r}^{m}\rho_{0}\big(\frac{U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}^{m}}-\frac{m\bar{U}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}^{m+1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\Big)+D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}D_{\bar{\eta}}U.

Note that the above equality enjoys a similar structure of (
9.84
), and hence we can follow analogous calculations (
9.85
)–(
9.87
) to derive

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\beta-\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\big)+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2},

and, by Lemma
A.4
,

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\beta-\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\leq C(T)\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{3\beta}{2}}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Therefore, combining the above two inequalities gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.140)

\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big).

As a consequence, recalling (
11.128
)–(
11.129
), we obtain from (
2.3
), (
11.93
), (
11.125
)–(
11.126
), (
11.140
), and Lemmas
11.5
–
11.6
and
A.4
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{7}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C|(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{8}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}U_{tt}\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}\big)\\
&\quad+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big(|(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big)\\
&\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{9}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1+(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\big|_{2}|(1,\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}|\chi^{\sharp}(\bar{\Lambda},\bar{\Lambda}_{t})|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\end{aligned}

which, along with (
11.128
) and Lemmas
11.7
–
11.8
, leads to

(11.141)

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}+C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big).

Finally, integrating the above over
[0,t]
, together with Lemma
11.8
, implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.142)

\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.2.

First, recall the derivation of (
11.72
):

(11.143)

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}:=(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)_{r}+\big(\frac{1}{\beta}+2\big)\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\rho_{0}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U=\sum_{i=12}^{15}\mathrm{I}_{i},

where

(11.144)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{12}

\displaystyle:=(1+2\beta)\bar{\eta}_{r}\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}}{\bar{\mathscr{J}}^{\beta+1}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U-\big(1+\frac{2}{\beta}\big)\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}U,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{13}

\displaystyle:=-m\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}\Big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+2\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{14}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\mu}\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}U_{t}+2\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}+\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{15}

\displaystyle:=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu\beta}\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1-3\beta}\Big(\bar{\varrho}^{2\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}+\frac{3(\gamma-1)}{\beta}\bar{\varrho}^{\beta}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}+\frac{(\gamma-1)(\gamma-1-\beta)}{\beta^{2}}\bar{\Lambda}^{3}\Big).

For the right-hand side of the above, it follows from the facts that

\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r,\qquad\big(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta+\gamma-1>0,\qquad\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta+\gamma-1-3\beta>-\frac{\beta}{2},

(
2.3
), (
11.93
), (
11.126
), and Lemmas
11.5
–
11.9
and
A.4
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{12}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big(|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\mathscr{J}}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{13}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}|\chi^{\sharp}(U,D_{\eta}U)|_{\infty}\\
&\quad+C(1+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\end{aligned}

(11.145)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{14}\big|_{2}&\leq C|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C(1+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t})\big|_{2}\\
&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\mathrm{I}_{15}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big(|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}\big)\\
&\quad+C\underline{(\gamma-1-\beta)\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta+\gamma-1-3\beta}\big|_{2}|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{3}}_{\,(=0,\text{ if $\beta=\gamma-1$})}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).\end{aligned}

Therefore, substituting (
11.145
) into (
11.143
) gives

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big),

which, along with Proposition
D.1
and Lemma
11.9
, yields

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|(\zeta-\chi)\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big).

Finally, it follows from the
L^{1}(0,T)
-estimate of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)
and (
11.142
) that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(t+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

3.
L^{\infty}(0,T)
-boundedness for
t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)
.
Following a similar argument in Step 1–Step 2, we can obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}(|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{tt}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\Big(\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\big),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\right)\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\big).

Therefore, this, combined with Lemma
11.8
, leads to the desired result of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 11.11
.

For any
0\leq t\leq T_{3}=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\}
,

(11.146)

\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Proof.

Collecting the estimates established in Lemmas
11.7
–
11.10
, we see that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(11.147)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

First, (
11.5
), together with (
11.94
) and Lemma
11.9
, yields that, for any
0\leq t\leq T_{3}:=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\}
,

(11.148)

\displaystyle|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})T_{3}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Next, let
(\mathring{\mathcal{E}},\mathring{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
be defined in the same way as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})(t,f)
in (
2.4
) and (
2.7
), respectively, except for letting
\eta(r)=r
. Then, based on (
11.147
)–(
11.148
), we obtain from Lemma
C.1
that,
for all
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

which thus leads to
\eqref{39''-0}_{1}
.
∎

Hence, defining constants
(c_{1},T^{*})
as

(11.149)

c_{1}:=C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad T^{*}:=T_{3}=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\},

then we obtain from Lemma
11.11
that, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

(11.150)

\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\qquad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

11.3.
Local-in-time well-posedness of the nonlinear problem

In this section, we prove the local well-posedness of classical solutions of (
2.2
) stated in Theorem
3.1
.

For convenience, in the rest of §
11.3
, we let
(\mathring{\mathcal{E}},\mathring{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
and
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{k}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}})(t,f)
be defined in the same way as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})(t,f)
in (
2.4
) and (
2.7
), except with
\eta(r)
in place of
r
and
\eta^{\mathrm{k}}
, respectively, where
\eta^{\mathrm{k}}
denotes the
\mathrm{k}
-th generation of the iterative sequence that will be given later.

Proof.

We divide the proof into the following four steps.

1. Construction of the iterative sequence.
Let
c_{0}
be given as in (
11.93
) and

U^{0}(t,r):=u_{0},\qquad\eta^{0}(t,r)=r+tu_{0}.

Then there exists a small positive time
T^{\prime}\leq T^{*}
, with
T^{*}
defined in (
11.149
),
such that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(11.151)

\mathcal{E}_{0}(t,U^{0})+t\mathcal{D}_{0}(t,U^{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{0}(s,U^{0})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{0}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{0}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Next, set
\bar{\eta}=\eta^{0}
in (
11.4
). By Lemma
11.1
and (
11.5
), problem (
11.4
) admits a unique classical solution
(U^{1},\eta^{1})
in
[0,T^{\prime}]\times\bar{I}
. Certainly, it follows from (
11.150
) that
(U^{1},\eta^{1})
also satisfies the following uniform estimates on
[0,T^{\prime}]
:

(11.152)

\mathcal{E}_{1}(t,U^{1})+t\mathcal{D}_{1}(t,U^{1})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{1}(s,U^{1})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{1}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{1}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

As a consequence, the approximate sequence
(U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1})
(
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
) can be constructed iteratively as follows: Given
(U^{\mathrm{k}},\eta^{\mathrm{k}})
, define
(U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1})
by solving the following problem in
(0,T^{\prime}]\times I
:

(11.153)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}U^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{r}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}+1}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}\\[8.0pt]
\qquad\displaystyle=-A((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{m}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma})_{r}+Am(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{m-1}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}=U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\\[4.0pt]
(U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1})(r)=(u_{0}(r),r)\qquad\text{for $r\in I$},\end{cases}

where
\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}
is given by

\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}=\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{m}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}.

By (
11.150
), we obtain an iterative solution sequence
(U^{\mathrm{k}},\eta^{\mathrm{k}})
satisfying (
11.151
), that is, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}
,

(11.154)

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}}(s,U^{\mathrm{k}})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Of course, by Lemma
C.1
, (
11.154
) also implies

(11.155)

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U^{\mathrm{k}})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\quad|\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

2. Convergence of
(U^{\mathrm{k}},\eta^{\mathrm{k}})
.

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}

\displaystyle:=U^{\mathrm{k}+1}-U^{\mathrm{k}},\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1}:=\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1}-\eta^{\mathrm{k}}=\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\Phi^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma-1},\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k+1}}\!:=\Phi^{\mathrm{k+1}}-\Phi^{\mathrm{k}},

and introduce the following energy function:

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k}},\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Then, based on (
11.153
), the problem of
(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1})
can be written as

(11.156)

\!\!\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{r}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}=\big((r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}\big)_{r}+(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}},\\[8.0pt]
\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1})(r)=(0,0)\qquad\text{for }r\in I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(2\mu\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}-1}_{r})^{2}}\big)-A\big(\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k}}+\Phi^{\mathrm{k-1}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r}})\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(-2\mu m\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}-1})^{2}}\big)+Am\big(\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k}}+\Phi^{\mathrm{k-1}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}})\big)\Big).

2.1. Estimates for
(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\mathrm{k}},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{\mathrm{k}})
.
Since

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\ \big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\big)_{t}=\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-\frac{U^{\mathrm{k-1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\big)\\
&\implies\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}=1+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Big(-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k-1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\,\mathrm{d}s,\end{aligned}

it follows from (
11.154
) that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(11.157)

\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)^{2}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Similarly, we can also obtain

(11.158)

\Big|\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)^{2}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}|\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Moreover, since

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k}}_{t}

\displaystyle=-(\gamma-1)\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k}}\big(\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{mU^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)-(\gamma-1)\Phi^{\mathrm{k-1}}\Big(\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-m(\gamma-1)\Phi^{\mathrm{k-1}}\Big(\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{U^{\mathrm{k-1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)\Big),

it follows from (
11.154
) and (
11.157
)–(
11.158
) that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(11.159)

|\widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{k}}|\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(|\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}|+\Big|\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\Big|\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, it follows from (
11.154
), (
11.157
)–(
11.159
), and the Hölder and Minkowski inequalities that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

(11.160)

\displaystyle|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\mathrm{k}},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{\mathrm{k}})(t)|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k}},\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

2.2.
Now, multiplying
\eqref{398}_{1}
by
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, we obtain from (
11.154
), (
11.160
), and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}\big|_{2}^{2}+\mu\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k+1}},\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C|(\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{\mathrm{k}},\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{\mathrm{k}})|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k}},\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
leads to

\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}+1}(t)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t^{2}e^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T^{\prime}]$ and $\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

Choosing
t=T_{*}
in the above inequality such that

C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})T_{*}^{2}e^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})T_{*}}\leq\frac{1}{2},\qquad T_{*}\leq T^{\prime},

leads to

(11.161)

\sum_{\mathrm{k}=1}^{\infty}\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(T_{*})\leq\Big(\sum_{\mathrm{k}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\mathrm{k}}\Big)\widehat{E}_{1}(T_{*})\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Then (
11.161
) implies that
\{U^{\mathrm{k}}\}_{\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence that converges to some limit
U
as
\mathrm{k}\to\infty
in the following sense:

\displaystyle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U^{\mathrm{k}}\to(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U

\displaystyle\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

\displaystyle(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(U^{\mathrm{k}}_{r},\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\big)\to(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(U_{r},\frac{U}{r}\big)

\displaystyle\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

which also leads to

(11.162)

U^{\mathrm{k}}\to U\qquad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}})\qquad\text{as $\mathrm{k}\to\infty$}.

On the other hand, from (
11.155
) and
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
, it follows that, for any
t\in[0,T_{*}]
and
a\in(0,1)
,

(11.163)

t\|U^{\mathrm{k}}\|_{H^{4}(a,1-a)}^{2}\leq C(a)(\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U))\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Hence, it follows (
11.162
)–(
11.163
) and Lemma
A.2
that, for any
a\in(0,1)
,

(11.164)

U^{\mathrm{k}}\to U\qquad\text{in }L^{1}([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}})\ \ \text{as $\mathrm{k}\to\infty$}.

Now, based on (
11.164
) and
\eqref{396}_{2}
, we have

(11.165)

\eta^{\mathrm{k}}=r+\int_{0}^{t}U^{\mathrm{k}}\,\mathrm{d}s\to r+\int_{0}^{t}U\,\mathrm{d}s\qquad\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}).

This also implies that
\{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}})
which converges to some limit
\eta
so that

\eta=r+\int_{0}^{t}U\,\mathrm{d}s\qquad\text{for {a.e.} $(t,r)\in(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)$}.

To recover equation
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
, we first divide
\eqref{396}_{1}
by
(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{m}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}
to derive

U^{\mathrm{k+1}}_{t}=\frac{1}{\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big(2\mu\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+2\mu m\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}-A(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma}\Big)_{r}-2\mu m\frac{(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})_{r}U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}.

Then, due to Lemma
A.3
,
\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r
,
\rho^{\beta}_{0}\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, and (
11.164
)–(
11.165
), we take the limit as
k\to\infty
in the above and obtain

U^{\mathrm{k}}_{t}\to\frac{1}{\varrho}D_{\eta}\Big(2\mu\Big(\varrho\big(D_{\eta}U+\frac{mU}{\eta}\big)\Big)-A\varrho^{\gamma}\Big)-2\mu m\frac{D_{\eta}\varrho U}{\varrho\eta}\qquad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}),

which, along with the uniqueness of limits, implies that
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)
. Moreover, by the lower semi-continuity of weak convergence and (
11.155
), we have

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\quad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

This completes the proof of the existence.

3. Uniqueness.
Let
(U^{\mathsf{a}},\eta^{\mathsf{a}})
and
(U^{\mathsf{b}},\eta^{\mathsf{b}})
be two solutions of (
2.2
) in
(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)
. Define

(\widehat{\eta},\widehat{U}):=(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}-\eta^{\mathsf{a}},U^{\mathsf{b}}-U^{\mathsf{a}}),\quad\,\,\widehat{E}(t):=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r},\frac{\widehat{U}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

It follows from (
2.2
) that
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})
solves the following problem in
(0,T_{*}]\times I
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}\widehat{U}_{t}-2\mu\big(r^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r})^{2}}\big)_{r}+2\mu mr^{m}\rho_{0}\frac{\widehat{U}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}}=\big((r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big)_{r}+(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}},\\[8.0pt]
\widehat{\eta}_{t}=\widehat{U},\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})(r)=(0,0)\qquad\text{for }r\in I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(2\mu\frac{U_{r}^{\mathsf{b}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{2}}\big)-A\big(\widehat{\Phi}+\Phi^{\mathsf{a}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}})\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}

\displaystyle:=(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(-2\mu m\frac{U^{\mathsf{b}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{2}}\big)+Am\big(\widehat{\Phi}+\Phi^{\mathsf{a}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}})\Big),

\displaystyle\Phi^{\mathsf{a}}

\displaystyle:=\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{m}\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}}\big)^{\gamma-1},\quad\Phi^{\mathsf{b}}:=\big(\frac{r^{m}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{m}\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big)^{\gamma-1},\quad\widehat{\Phi}:=\Phi^{\mathsf{b}}-\Phi^{\mathsf{a}}.

Similarly, we can show that
(\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{2})
satisfy (
11.160
) with
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}
replaced by
\widehat{U}
. Hence, by the same arguments as in Step 2.2, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}+\mu\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r},\frac{\widehat{U}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\widehat{U}_{r},\frac{\widehat{U}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, together with the Grönwall inequality, yields that
\widehat{E}(t)\equiv 0
,
i.e.
,
U^{\mathsf{a}}\equiv U^{\mathsf{b}}
.

4.
(U,\eta)
is classical satisfying (
3.2
).
First, the regularity of
U
and (
3.2
) can be proved by the same argument as in Steps 6–7 in §
11.1.2
. Then the regularity of
\eta
follows directly from the formula:
\eta_{t}=U
. Finally, following a similar argument in Step 8 in §
11.1.2
, we can show that
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
holds pointwise in
(0,T_{*}]\times\bar{I}
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
∎

Appendix A
Some Basic Lemmas

For the convenience of readers, we list some basic facts that have been used frequently in this paper. Throughout the following Appendices
A
–
D
, let
\rho_{0}(\bm{y})=\rho_{0}(r)
, with
\rho_{0}(r)
satisfy (
1.17
), that is, for some constants
\mathcal{K}_{2}>\mathcal{K}_{1}>0
and
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\gamma-1]
,

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\beta},(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r},(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{rr},\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{rrr},(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r})_{r}\big)\in L^{2}(I),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}(1-r)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq\rho_{0}(r)\leq\mathcal{K}_{2}(1-r)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $r\in I$}.

The first lemma concerns the separability and density of the weighted Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.1

(
[
39
]
)
.

Let
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}\in(-1,\infty)
, and let
d=d(r)=r^{\vartheta_{1}}(1-r)^{\vartheta_{2}}
be a function defined on
I
. Then, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
H^{k}_{d}
is a reflexive separable Banach space. Moreover, if
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
is dense in
H^{k}_{d}
with respect to the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{k,d}
.

The second lemma concerns the well-known interpolation theory for the
H^{k}
spaces.

Lemma A.2

(
[
40
]
)
.

Let
J\subset\mathbb{R}
be some open interval and
F\in H^{p}(J)\cap H^{q}(J)

(p,q\geq 0)
. Then
F\in H^{l}(J)
for
l=p\vartheta+q(1-\vartheta)
and
0\leq\vartheta\leq 1
, and the following inequality holds
:

\left\|F\right\|_{l}\leq C\left\|F\right\|_{p}^{\vartheta}\left\|F\right\|_{q}^{1-\vartheta},

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
(p,q,\vartheta)
.

The third lemma is on the classical Sobolev embedding theorem.

Lemma A.3

(
[
40
]
)
.

Let
J\subset\mathbb{R}
be some open interval and
f=f(r)
be some function on
J
. Then there exist two positive constants
(s_{0},C)
, depending only on the Lebesgue measure of
J
, such that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{1}(J)}+C\|f_{r}\|_{L^{1}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in W^{1,1}(J),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}(J)}+C\|f_{r}\|_{L^{2}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in H^{1}(J),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}(J)}+C\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(J)}\|f_{r}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in H^{1}(J).

In particular,
W^{1,1}(J)\hookrightarrow C(\bar{J})
and
H^{1}(J)\hookrightarrow C(\bar{J})
continuously
;
moreover,
if
f(r_{0})=0
for some
r_{0}\in\bar{J}
, then
s_{0}=0
can be chosen in the above.

The next two lemmas are on the Hardy inequality and some weighted interpolation inequality. In Lemmas
A.4
–
A.5
, we let
0\leq a<b<\infty
and
J=(a,b)
, and let
d=d(r)
be some function on
J
, taking one of the following two forms:

d(r)=r-a,\qquad\text{or}\ \ d(r)=b-r.

Lemma A.4

(
[
9
,
55
]
)
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{p}

(\vartheta>0\text{ if }p=\infty)
. Then, for any
f
such that
d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{2}(J)
,

(i)

If
p\in[1,2)
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
, depending only on
(\varepsilon,p,a,b,\vartheta)
, such that

\|d^{\vartheta+\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C_{1}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{2}(J)};

(ii)

If
p\in[2,\infty]
, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
, which depends only on
(p,a,b,\vartheta)
if
p\neq\infty
and depends only on
(a,b,\vartheta)
if
p=\infty
, such that

\|d^{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C_{2}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}.

Moreover, if
p=\infty
,
d^{\vartheta}f\in C(\bar{J})
.

Lemma A.5

(
[
63
]
)
.

Let
p\in[2,\infty]
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{p}

(\vartheta>0\text{ if }p=\infty)
. Then, for any
f
satisfying
d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{2}(J)
, there exists a constant
C>0
, which depends only on
(p,a,b,\vartheta)
if
p\neq\infty
and depends only on
(a,b,\vartheta)
if
p=\infty
, such that

(A.1)

\|d^{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C\big(\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}+\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f_{r}\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Proof.

It suffices to prove (
A.1
) with
J=I
and
d(r)=r
. The proof for (
A.1
)
with the other cases can be obtained similarly.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
. First, if
p=2
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{2}
, a direct calculation, combined with integration by parts, yields

(A.2)

\displaystyle|r^{\vartheta}f|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\vartheta+1}f^{2}(1)-\frac{2}{2\vartheta+1}\int_{0}^{1}r^{2\vartheta+1}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}}f|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\vartheta+2}f^{2}(1)-\frac{2}{2\vartheta+2}\int_{0}^{1}r^{2\vartheta+2}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r,

which, along with the Hölder inequality, implies

(A.3)

\displaystyle|r^{\vartheta}f|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\vartheta+2}{2\vartheta+1}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2}{2\vartheta+1}\int_{0}^{1}(r-1)r^{2\vartheta+1}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big).

Next, if
p=\infty
and
\vartheta>0
, it follows from the above, Lemma
A.3
, and the Hölder inequality that

(A.4)

\displaystyle|r^{\vartheta}f|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}|(r^{2\vartheta}f^{2})_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r=C\int_{0}^{1}r^{2\vartheta-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+C\int_{0}^{1}r^{2\vartheta}|f||f_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\big|r^{\vartheta-\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}^{2}+C|r^{\vartheta}f|_{2}|r^{\vartheta}f_{r}|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|r^{\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\vartheta}f|_{2}|r^{\vartheta}f_{r}|_{2}\big).

Finally, if
p\in(2,\infty)
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{p}
, repeating the similar calculations in (
A.2
)–(
A.3
), combined with (
A.4
), leads to

(A.5)

\displaystyle|r^{\vartheta}f|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{p\vartheta+2}{p\vartheta+1}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{p}{p\vartheta+1}\int_{0}^{1}(r-1)r^{p\vartheta+1}|f|^{p-2}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|r^{(\vartheta+\frac{1}{p})(p-1)}|f|^{p-1}\big|_{2}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big(\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{2}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{2}^{p}+\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big|_{2}^{\frac{p}{2}}\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{p}{2}}\big).

Therefore, we complete the proof for (
A.1
) when
f\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
.

For
f\in H^{1}_{d^{2\vartheta+\frac{2}{p}}}
, there exists a sequence
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
due to Lemma
A.1

such that

\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)\big|_{2}+\big|r^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}(f^{\varepsilon}_{r}-f_{r})\big|_{2}\to 0\qquad\text{as }\varepsilon\to 0.

Then we can show that (
A.1
) holds for all
f\in H^{1}_{d^{2\vartheta+\frac{2}{p}}}
via the density argument.

This completes the proof of Lemma
A.5
.
∎

The sixth lemma is used to obtain the time-weighted estimates of the velocity.

Lemma A.6

(
[
2
]
)
.

Let
f\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
. Then there exists a sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{k}\rightarrow 0,\quad\,\,t_{k}|f(t_{k})|^{2}_{2}\rightarrow 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\rightarrow\infty$}.

Proof.

Let
F(t)=|f(t)|^{2}_{2}
. Clearly,
0\leq F(t)\in L^{1}(0,T)
. Then it suffices to show that, for any
k\geq 1
, there exists
t_{k}\in(0,\frac{T}{1+k})
such that

t_{k}F(t_{k})<\frac{1}{k}\to 0\qquad\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

Assume by contradiction that there exists some
k_{0}\geq 1
such that

tF(t)\geq\frac{1}{k_{0}}\qquad\text{for any $t\in\big(0,\frac{T}{1+k_{0}}\big)$}.

Then

\int_{0}^{T}F(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\geq\frac{1}{k_{0}}\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{1+k_{0}}}\frac{1}{s}\,\mathrm{d}s=\infty.

This contradicts with the fact that
F(t)\in L^{1}(0,T)
. Thus, the claim holds.
∎

The seventh lemma is an equivalent statement for spherical symmetric vector functions.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\bm{f}=\bm{f}(\bm{y})
be a spherically symmetric continuous vector function on
B_{R}=\{\bm{y}:\,|\bm{y}|<R\}
for some
R>0
. Then
\bm{f}
takes the form
:

\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(|\bm{y}|)\frac{\bm{y}}{|\bm{y}|}
if and only if

(A.6)

\mathcal{O}\bm{f}(\bm{y})=\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})\qquad\text{for all $\bm{y}\in B_{R}$ and $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

In particular, any spherically symmetric vector function
\bm{f}
satisfies
\bm{f}(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
.

Proof.

Indeed, since
\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(|\bm{y}|)\frac{\bm{y}}{|\bm{y}|}
is spherically symmetric, it follows that

\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})=\mathcal{O}\bm{f}(\bm{y})
for all
\bm{y}\in B_{R}
and

\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
.

Conversely, if (
A.6
) holds, we take the 3-D case as an example. Let
\bm{y}_{0}\in B_{R}
be any fixed displacement vector and
\bm{e}_{1}=\frac{\bm{y}_{0}}{|\bm{y}_{0}|}
. Assume that
\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
is a rotation by 180 degrees about an axis parallel to
\bm{y}_{0}
, that is,
\mathcal{O}_{1}\bm{y}_{0}=\bm{y}_{0}
. Then we can obtain from (
A.6
) that

(A.7)

\mathcal{O}_{1}\bm{f}(\bm{y}_{0})=\bm{f}(\bm{y}_{0}).

Next, let
\{\bm{e}_{2},\bm{e}_{3}\}
be two unit vectors such that
\{\bm{e}_{1},\bm{e}_{2},\bm{e}_{3}\}
becomes an orthonormal basis in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then there exist constants
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i}(\bm{y}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}

(i=1,2,3)
, depending only on
\bm{y}_{0}
, such that

\bm{f}(\bm{y}_{0})=\alpha_{1}\bm{e}_{1}+\alpha_{2}\bm{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\bm{e}_{3}.

This, together with (
A.7
), gives

\alpha_{1}\bm{e}_{1}+\alpha_{2}\bm{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\bm{e}_{3}=\alpha_{1}\bm{e}_{1}-\alpha_{2}\bm{e}_{2}-\alpha_{3}\bm{e}_{3}\implies\alpha_{2}\bm{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\bm{e}_{3}=\bm{0},

which, along with the linear independence of
\{\bm{e}_{2},\bm{e}_{3}\}
, leads to
\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=0
. Hence, for any fixed
\bm{y}_{0}\in B_{R}
,

\bm{f}(\bm{y}_{0})=\alpha_{1}\bm{e}_{1}=\alpha_{1}(\bm{y}_{0})\frac{\bm{y}_{0}}{|\bm{y}_{0}|}.

By (
A.6
), we see that
\alpha_{1}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y}_{0})=\alpha_{1}(\bm{y}_{0})
for all
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
, that is,
\alpha_{1}(\bm{y}_{0})=\alpha_{1}(|\bm{y}_{0}|)
.
Then defining
f(r):=\alpha_{1}(r)
implies that
\bm{f}
takes the form:
\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(|\bm{y}|)\frac{\bm{y}}{|\bm{y}|}
.

Finally, from (
A.6
), we can take
\bm{y}=\bm{0}
to obtain

(A.8)

\bm{f}(\bm{0})=\mathcal{O}\bm{f}(\bm{0})\qquad\text{for all $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

Then, choosing
\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}_{2}
by 180 degrees about an axis perpendicular to
\bm{f}(\bm{0})
, that is,
\mathcal{O}_{2}\bm{f}(\bm{0})=-\bm{f}(\bm{0})
, we obtain from (
A.8
) that
\bm{f}(\bm{0})=-\bm{f}(\bm{0})
, so that
\bm{f}(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
.
∎

Finally, for giving the time continuity of the velocity in our proof, the following two types of evolution triple embedding are required.

Lemma A.8

(
[
22
]
)
.

Let
T>0
and
J\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}

(k=1,2,3)
be some open subset. Assume that
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{0}(J))
and
f_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}(J))
.
Then
f\in C([0,T];L^{2}(J))
, and the map
:

t\mapsto\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{2}
is absolutely continuous
with

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{2}=2\left<f_{t},f\right>_{H^{-1}(J)\times H_{0}^{1}(J)}\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Moreover, if additionally
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H_{0}^{1}(J))
, then
f\in C([0,T];L^{4}(J))
.

Lemma A.9

(
[
63
]
)
.

Let
T>0
,
s\geq 0
, and
\mathcal{H}\subset L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{*}
, where
\mathcal{H}
is a Banach space. Assume that
f\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H})
and
r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{*})
. Then
f\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}})
, and the map
:

t\mapsto|f(t)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}^{2}
is absolutely continuous with

(A.9)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|f(t)|_{2,r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}^{2}=2\left<r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t},f\right>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}.

Proof.

This lemma can be obtained by basically following the proof of Theorem 3 on page 303
in
[
22
]
*Chapter 5, and we only sketch it here. Let
\omega_{\varepsilon}
be the standard mollifiers and

f^{\epsilon}(t,r):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f(t-\tau,r)\omega_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Thus, after the extension and regularization, for any
\varepsilon,\sigma>0
, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=2\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma},f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}.

Integrating the above over
[0,T]
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\varepsilon}(0)-f^{\sigma}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}+\big\|r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Since
L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}
,
\mathcal{H}
, and
\mathcal{H}^{*}
are all Banach spaces due
to Lemma
A.1
, by Theorem 8.20 in
[
40
]
*Chapter 8, for all
g_{1}(0)\in L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}
,
g_{2}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H})
, and
g_{3}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{*})
, we have

\begin{gathered}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\|g_{1}^{\varepsilon}(0)-g_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|g_{2}^{\varepsilon}-g_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\|g_{3}^{\varepsilon}-g_{3}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t=0.\end{gathered}

Hence, letting
(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)
, together with the fact that
(r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f)*\omega_{\varepsilon}=r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f^{\varepsilon}
, yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\|f^{\varepsilon}(0)-f^{\sigma}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\lim_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}+\big\|r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t=0,

which shows that
f^{\varepsilon}
converges to
f
in
C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}})
.
Similarly, we have

\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=\|f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $\tau,t\in[0,T]$}.

Taking the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
implies

(A.10)
