\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=\|f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\big<r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t},f\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime},

which implies the absolute continuity of
\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{m}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}
. Applying
\partial_{t}
to (
A.10
) yields (
A.9
).
∎

Appendix B
Coordinate Transformations

This appendix is devoted to showing the conversion of some Sobolev spaces between the M-D coordinates
\bm{y}
and the spherical coordinate
r=|\bm{y}|
for spherically symmetric functions. Let
n
be the number of spatial dimension and
m=n-1
.

B.1.
Transformation between multidimensional coordinates and spherical ones

Let
0\leq a<b
,
\mathcal{J}:=\{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,a\leq|\bm{y}|<b\}
, and
r\in J:=[a,b)
with
r=|\bm{y}|
. Consider a coordinate transformation
\bm{\xi}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})\in C^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{J}})
such that

\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\xi(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r},\qquad\bm{\xi}:\mathcal{J}\to\mathcal{G}:=\bm{\xi}(\mathcal{J}),\qquad\bm{y}\mapsto\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}).

Assume that
\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi}
is a non-singular matrix, and define

\begin{gathered}D_{\xi}f=\frac{f_{r}}{\xi_{r}},\qquad\mathcal{B}=(\mathcal{B}_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{B}_{ij}:=((\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{-1})_{ij}$},\\[-2.0pt]
\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f=((\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{1},\cdots\!,(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{n})^{\top}\qquad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{ki}\partial_{y_{k}}f$},\\[-3.0pt]
\bm{f}=(f_{1},\cdots\!,f_{n})^{\top},\qquad\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f}=((\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f})_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\qquad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f})_{ij}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{kj}\partial_{y_{k}}f_{i}$},\end{gathered}

where
f(\bm{y})=f(r)
and
\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
are sufficiently smooth functions.

Then we have the following coordinate transformations.

Lemma B.1
.

Assume that
(g,\bm{g})(\bm{x})
are spherically symmetric functions defined on
\mathcal{G}
and
(f,\bm{f})(\bm{y})
satisfy

f(\bm{y})=g(\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}))=g(\bm{x}),\qquad\bm{f}(\bm{y})=\bm{g}(\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}))=\bm{g}(\bm{x}).

Then, for any
q\in[1,\infty]
, the following statements hold
:

(i)

Transformations for
(g,f)
:
for
j=0,1
and
k=2,3
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}g\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{j}f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\|(\xi^{m}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}D_{\xi}^{j}f\|_{L^{q}(J)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\Big\|(\xi^{m}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}\Big(D_{\xi}^{k}f,D_{\xi}^{k-2}\big(\frac{D_{\xi}f}{\xi}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(J)};

(ii)

Transformations for
(\bm{g},\bm{f})
:
for
j=1,2
and
k=3,4
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\bm{f}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\|(\xi^{m}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}f\|_{L^{q}(J)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{j}\bm{f}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\Big\|(\xi^{m}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}\Big(D_{\xi}^{j}f,D_{\xi}^{j-1}\big(\frac{f}{\xi}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(J)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}\bm{f}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\Big\|(\xi^{m}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}\Big(D_{\xi}^{k}f,D_{\xi}^{k-1}\big(\frac{f}{\xi}\big),D_{\xi}^{k-3}\big(\frac{1}{\xi}D_{\xi}(\frac{f}{\xi})\big)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(J)}.

Here,
E\sim F
denotes
C^{-1}E\leq F\leq CE
for some constant
C\geq 1
depending only on
n
, and we emphasize that, for any function space
X
and functions
(\varphi,h_{1},\cdots\!,h_{k})
,

\|\varphi(h_{1},\cdots\!,h_{k})\|_{X}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|\varphi h_{i}\|_{X}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the transformations for
(\bm{g},\bm{f})
since
\nabla_{\bm{y}}f=f_{r}\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
can be regarded as a vector function
\bm{h}=h\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
with
h=f_{r}
.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
We first prove the case when
\xi(r)=r
. In this case,
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\bm{y}
and
\mathcal{B}
is the
n\times n
identity matrix. It follows from direct calculations that

\displaystyle\quad\ \,\begin{aligned} (f_{k})_{y_{i}}&=\frac{y_{i}y_{k}}{r^{2}}f_{r}+\frac{\delta_{ik}r^{2}-y_{i}y_{k}}{r^{3}}f,\\
(f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}}&=\frac{y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}}{r^{3}}f_{rr}+\Big(\frac{\delta_{ij}y_{k}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}}{r}-\frac{3y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}}{r^{3}}\Big)\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\ \ \,\begin{aligned} (f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}}&=\frac{y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}}{r^{4}}f_{rrr}\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}}{r^{2}}-\frac{6y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}}{r^{4}}\Big)\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr}\\
&\quad+\Big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{k\ell}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{j\ell}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{i\ell}-\frac{\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}}{r^{2}}+\frac{3y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}}{r^{4}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}}&=\frac{y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}f_{rrrr}\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{\delta_{ip}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jp}y_{i}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{kp}y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}y_{p}}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{3}}-\frac{10y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}\Big)\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\quad\ \ \begin{aligned} &\quad+\Big(\frac{\delta_{i\ell}\delta_{jp}y_{k}+\delta_{i\ell}\delta_{kp}y_{j}+\delta_{j\ell}\delta_{ip}y_{k}+\delta_{j\ell}\delta_{kp}y_{i}+\delta_{k\ell}\delta_{ip}y_{j}+\delta_{k\ell}\delta_{jp}y_{i}}{r}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}\delta_{kp}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ij}\delta_{\ell p}y_{k}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jp}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{\ell p}y_{j}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{ip}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}}{r}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}\delta_{k\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{j\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{i\ell}y_{p}}{r}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{ip}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jp}y_{i}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{kp}y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}y_{j}y_{k})}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}y_{p})}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}y_{p})}{r^{3}}+\frac{15y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}.\end{aligned}

Then the above expressions yield

\displaystyle|\bm{f}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k}^{n}|f_{k}|^{2}=|f|^{2},\quad|\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|^{2}=\sum_{i,k=1}^{n}|(f_{k})_{y_{i}}|^{2}=|f_{r}|^{2}+m\Big|\frac{f}{r}\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{f}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}|(f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}}|^{2}=|f_{rr}|^{2}+3m\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{3}\bm{f}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\ell=1}^{n}\big|(f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}}\big|^{2}=|f_{rrr}|^{2}+6m\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr}\Big|^{2}+(3m^{2}+6m)\Big|\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{4}\bm{f}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\ell,p=1}^{n}\big|(f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}}\big|^{2}=|f_{rrrr}|^{2}\!+10m\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr}\Big|^{2}\!+(15m^{2}\!+\!30m)\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big|^{2},

which implies

(B.1)

\begin{gathered}|\bm{f}|\sim|f|,\quad|\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|\sim|f_{r}|+\Big|\frac{f}{r}\Big|,\quad|\nabla^{2}_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|\sim|f_{rr}|+\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|,\\
|\nabla^{3}_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|\sim|f_{rrr}|+\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr}\Big|+\Big|\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|,\quad|\nabla^{4}_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|\sim|f_{rrrr}|+\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr}\Big|+\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big|.\end{gathered}

Finally, thanks to the integral identity:

\int_{\mathcal{J}}f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{y}=\omega_{n}\int_{J}f(r)r^{m}\,\mathrm{d}r,

where
\omega_{n}
denotes the surface area of the
n
-sphere, we thus obtain the desired conclusions of this lemma when
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\bm{y}
from (
B.1
).

2.
For general
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
, we can first repeat the calculations in Step 1 with the coordinate
\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
and the function
\bm{g}(\bm{x})
. Specifically, if we let
x:=|\bm{x}|
, then

x=\xi(r)\in G,\qquad G:=[\xi(a),\xi(b)),

and we can obtain from (
B.1
) that

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|x^{\frac{m}{q}}g\|_{L^{q}(G)},\qquad\|\nabla\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}\sim\Big\|x^{\frac{m}{q}}\big(g_{x},\frac{g}{x}\big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(G)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\Big\|x^{\frac{m}{q}}\Big(g_{xx},\big(\frac{g}{x}\big)_{x}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(G)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\Big\|x^{\frac{m}{q}}\Big(g_{xxx},\big(\frac{g}{x}\big)_{xx},\frac{1}{x}\big(\frac{g}{x}\big)_{x}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(G)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{4}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\Big\|x^{\frac{m}{q}}\Big(g_{xxxx},\big(\frac{g}{x}\big)_{xxx},\big(\frac{1}{x}(\frac{g}{x})_{x}\big)_{x}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{q}(G)}.

Next, using the coordinate transformations
\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
and
x=\xi(y)
, we have

(B.3)

\nabla^{k}\bm{g}=\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}\bm{f},\quad\,\,\partial_{x}^{k}g=D_{\xi}^{k}f,\qquad\,\,k=0,1,2,3,4.

Therefore, (
B.2
)–(
B.3
), together with the following integral identities

\int_{\mathcal{G}}g(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{x}=\int_{\mathcal{J}}f(\bm{y})(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{y},\qquad\int_{G}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{J}f(r)\xi_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r,

lead to the desired results of this lemma.
∎

B.2.
Transformation from the M-D Lagrangian coordinates to the spherically symmetric coordinates

Generally, it is desirable to consider the Lagrangian formulation, so that we can pullback (
1.1
) on the moving domain
\Omega(t)
to a problem on a fixed domain
\Omega
. To this end, denote by
\bm{x}=\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})
the position of the fluid particle
\bm{x}\in\Omega(t)
at time
t\geq 0
so that

(B.4)

\bm{\eta}_{t}(t,\bm{y})=\bm{u}(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}))\ \ \text{for $t>0$},\qquad\text{with $\bm{\eta}(0,\bm{y})=\bm{y}$},

and
(t,\bm{y})
are the M-D Lagrangian coordinates. Then, by introducing the Lagrangian density and velocity:

(B.5)

\varrho(t,\bm{y})=\rho(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})),\qquad\bm{U}(t,\bm{y})=\bm{u}(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})),

we can rewrite (
1.1
) as

(B.6)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varrho_{t}+\varrho\dive_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}=0&\text{in }(0,T]\times\Omega,\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle\varrho\bm{U}_{t}+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}P=\mu\dive_{\mathcal{A}}\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}+(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})^{\top})\big)&\text{in }(0,T]\times\Omega,\\[6.0pt]
\bm{\eta}_{t}=\bm{U}&\text{in }(0,T]\times\Omega,\\[6.0pt]
\varrho>0&\text{in }(0,T]\times\Omega,\\[6.0pt]
\varrho|_{\partial\Omega}=0&\text{on }(0,T],\\[6.0pt]
(\varrho,\bm{U},\bm{\eta})(0,\bm{y})=(\rho_{0}(\bm{y}),\bm{u}_{0}(\bm{y}),\bm{y})&\text{for $\bm{y}\in\Omega$}.\end{cases}

Here
\bm{y}=(y_{1},\cdots\!,y_{n})^{\top}
,
\bm{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\cdots\!,\eta_{n})^{\top}
,
\bm{U}=(U_{1},\cdots\!,U_{n})^{\top}
, and

(B.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=(\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta})^{-1},\qquad\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta}\quad\text{with $(\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta})_{ij}=\frac{\partial\eta_{i}}{\partial y_{j}}$},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}P=\mathcal{A}^{\top}(\nabla P)\quad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}P)_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{ki}\frac{\partial P}{\partial y_{k}}$},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}=(\nabla\bm{U})\cdot\mathcal{A}\quad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})_{ij}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{kj}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial y_{k}}$},\qquad\dive_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})_{ii}.

The initial density
\rho_{0}
under consideration satisfies the following condition:

(B.8)

\rho_{0}^{\beta}(\bm{y})\in H^{3}(\Omega),\quad\,\,\mathcal{K}_{1}(1-|\bm{y}|)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq\rho_{0}(\bm{y})\leq\mathcal{K}_{2}(1-|\bm{y}|)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $\bm{y}\in\overline{\Omega}$},

for some constants
\mathcal{K}_{2}>\mathcal{K}_{1}>0
and
\beta>0
.

Now, we show that problem (
B.6
) also implies (
1.16
) in §
1
.

Lemma B.2
.

Under the spherical symmetry assumption of
(\rho,\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})
in (
1.8
), if
(\rho,\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})
is the classical solution of
VFBP
(
1.1
), then

(B.9)

(\varrho,\bm{U},\bm{\eta})(t,\bm{y})=(\varrho(t,r),U(t,r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r},\eta(t,r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}),

where
r=|\bm{y}|
, and
\eta(t,r)
and
(\varrho,U)(t,r)
are given by (
1.14
) and (
1.15
), respectively. Moreover,
(\varrho,U,\eta)(t,r)
satisfies
IBVP
(
1.16
).

Proof.

First, we show that, under (
1.8
),
\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})
is spherically symmetric, taking the form:

(B.10)

\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})=\eta(t,r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r},

where
r=|\bm{y}|
, and
\eta(t,r)=x=|\bm{x}|
is defined in (
1.14
). By Lemma
A.7
in Appendix
A
, it suffices to prove

(B.11)

(\mathcal{O}\bm{\eta})(t,\bm{y})=\bm{\eta}(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{y})\qquad\text{for any $\bm{y}\in\Omega$ and $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

Indeed, define

(B.12)

\tilde{\bm{\eta}}(t,\bm{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{-1}\bm{\eta})(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{y}).

Then it follows from (
B.4
) and the spherical symmetry of
\bm{u}(t,\bm{x})
(
1.8
) that

\tilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t}(t,\bm{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{-1}\bm{u})(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{y}))=(\mathcal{O}^{-1}\bm{u})(t,(\mathcal{O}\tilde{\bm{\eta}})(t,\bm{y}))=\bm{u}(t,\tilde{\bm{\eta}}(t,\bm{y})).

Note that
\bm{u}(t,\bm{x})
is a classical solution of problem (
1.1
),
i.e.
,
\bm{u}(t,\bm{x})\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}(t))
for each
t>0
. Comparing the above with (
B.4
), since
\tilde{\bm{\eta}}(0,\bm{y})=\bm{\eta}(0,\bm{y})=\bm{y}
, we can derive from the uniqueness of ODEs (
B.4
) that
\tilde{\bm{\eta}}\equiv\bm{\eta}
, which leads to (
B.11
).

Next, from the radial coordinate
r=|\bm{y}|
and the definition of
(\varrho,U)(t,r)
in (
1.15
), it follows that (
B.5
) becomes

(B.13)

\displaystyle\varrho(t,\bm{y})

\displaystyle=\rho(t,|\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})|)=\rho(t,\eta(t,r))=\varrho(t,r),

\displaystyle\bm{U}(t,\bm{y})

\displaystyle=u(t,|\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})|)\frac{\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})}{|\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})|}=u(t,\eta(t,r))\frac{\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})}{\eta(t,r)}=U(t,r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}.

This implies that
(\varrho,U)(t,r)
in (
1.15
) are radial projections of
(\varrho,\bm{U})(t,\bm{y})
.

Finally, from the proof of Lemma
B.1
, it follows that

(B.14)

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{r}\delta_{ij}+\big(\eta_{r}-\frac{\eta}{r}\big)\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{U})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{U}{r}\delta_{ij}+\big(U_{r}-\frac{U}{r}\big)\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}},\qquad(\nabla_{\bm{y}}P)_{i}=\frac{y_{i}}{r}P_{r},

which, along with a direct calculation, yields

(B.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{ij}

\displaystyle=((\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta})^{-1})_{ij}=\frac{r}{\eta}\delta_{ij}+\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{r}}-\frac{r}{\eta}\big)\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{kj}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial y_{k}}=\frac{U}{\eta}\delta_{ij}+\big(\frac{U_{r}}{\eta_{r}}-\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\dive_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{r}}{\eta_{r}}+\frac{mU}{\eta},\qquad(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}P)_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{ki}\frac{\partial P}{\partial y_{k}}=\frac{y_{i}}{r}\frac{P_{r}}{\eta_{r}}.

Based on the above, we can derive the equations of
(\varrho,U,\eta)(t,r)
in
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{1}
–
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{3}
.
∎

B.3.
Transformation between the Eulerian and Lagrangian coordinates

We give the transformation relations between
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
and
(\varrho(t,r),U(t,r))
.

First, define the moving domain
\Omega(t)
as

(B.16)

\Omega(t):=\big\{\bm{x}=\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}):\,\bm{y}\in\Omega\big\},

and, for every
t\in[0,T]
and
\bm{x}\in\Omega(t)
, define the inverse flow map
\bm{\eta}_{*}
by

\bm{y}=\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}):\quad\Omega(t)\to\Omega,\quad(t,\bm{x})\mapsto(t,\bm{y}),

which satisfies

(B.17)

\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\bm{x},\qquad\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}))=\bm{y}.

Then we have the following result:

Lemma B.3
.

Let
(U,\eta)
be the classical solution of
IBVP
(
2.2
) obtained in
Theorem
2.1
. Then the inverse flow map
\bm{\eta}_{*}=\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})
is well-defined on
\Omega(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
, and
\bm{\eta}_{*}
is spherically symmetric, which takes the form
:

(B.18)

\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})=\eta_{*}(t,x)\frac{\bm{x}}{x},\qquad x=|\bm{x}|.

Moreover, if we set

(B.19)

\rho(t,\bm{x}):=\varrho(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),\qquad\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}):=\bm{U}(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),

where
(\varrho,\bm{U})(t,\bm{y})
takes form (
B.9
) in
Lemma
B.2
, then
(\rho,\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})
is spherically symmetric, taking the form (
1.8
), and
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
is a solution of
VFBP
(
1.1
) described by
(i)–(ii)
of
Theorem
2.3
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1.
First, by (
B.14
)–(
B.15
),

\det(\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta})=\frac{\eta^{m}\eta_{r}}{r^{m}},\qquad|\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta}|^{2}=\eta_{r}^{2}+\frac{m\eta^{2}}{r^{2}},\qquad|\mathcal{A}|^{2}=\frac{1}{\eta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{mr^{2}}{\eta^{2}},

it thus follows from (
2.12
) that the flow map
\bm{\eta}(t,\cdot)\!:\Omega\to\Omega(t)
is injective.
Moreover,
(\eta,\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})\in C^{1}([0,T];C(\bar{I}))
ensures that

\bm{\eta}\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
. By the inverse function theorem, for each fixed
t\in[0,T]
,
\bm{\eta}(t,\cdot)
is a diffeomorphism from
\Omega
onto its image
\Omega(t)
. Thus, the inverse map
\bm{\eta}_{*}:\Omega(t)\to\Omega
is well‑defined and belongs to
C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{E}(T)})
, where
\mathbb{E}(T)
is defined by

\mathbb{E}(T)=\big\{(t,\bm{x}):\,t\in(0,T],\ \bm{x}\in\overline{\Omega}(t)\big\}.

In particular,
\bm{\eta}_{*}
satisfies the following relations:

(B.20)

\nabla\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})=\mathcal{A}(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),\qquad(\bm{\eta}_{*})_{t}(t,\bm{x})=-(\mathcal{A}\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})).

2.
We show the spherical symmetry of
\bm{\eta}_{*}
. For any
\bm{x}\in\Omega(t)
, there exists
\bm{y}\in\Omega
such that
\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})=\bm{x}
. It then follows from (
B.17
) that, for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}\bm{\eta}_{*})(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}\bm{\eta}_{*})(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}))=\mathcal{O}\bm{y},

\displaystyle\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,(\mathcal{O}\bm{\eta})(t,\bm{y}))=\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{y}))=\mathcal{O}\bm{y}.

Hence, for any
\bm{x}\in\Omega(t)
and
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
, we have

(\mathcal{O}\bm{\eta}_{*})(t,\bm{x})=\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\mathcal{O}\bm{x}),

which, along with Lemma
A.7
, yields (
B.18
).

3.
Now, set
(\rho,\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})
as in (
B.19
). Clearly, by (
B.18
), we can directly derive

(B.21)

\displaystyle\varrho(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))

\displaystyle=\varrho(t,|\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})|)=\varrho(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

\displaystyle\bm{U}(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))

\displaystyle=U(t,|\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})|)\frac{\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})}{|\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})|}=U(t,\eta_{*}(t,x))\frac{\bm{x}}{x},

and hence
(\rho,\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})
satisfies (
1.8
).

Next, we show that
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
solves problem (
1.1
). Indeed, from (
B.14
)–(
B.15
) and (
B.20
), we have

(B.22)

\displaystyle\rho_{t}(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\varrho_{t}-\bm{U}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\varrho)(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\big(\varrho_{t}-U\frac{\varrho_{r}}{\eta_{r}}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

\displaystyle\dive(\rho\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\dive_{\mathcal{A}}(\varrho\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\big(\frac{(\varrho U)_{r}}{\eta_{r}}+\frac{m\varrho U}{\eta}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

which, along with
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{1}
, leads to

(B.23)

\rho_{t}+\dive(\rho\bm{u})=0\qquad\text{in }\Omega(t).

Similarly, we can also obtain

\displaystyle\rho\bm{u}_{t}(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\varrho\bm{U}_{t}-\varrho\bm{U}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\big(\varrho U_{t}-\frac{UU_{r}}{\eta_{r}}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

\displaystyle(\rho\bm{u}\cdot\nabla\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\varrho\bm{U}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\big(\frac{UU_{r}}{\eta_{r}}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

\displaystyle\dive(\rho D(\bm{u}))(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\dive_{\mathcal{A}}\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U}+(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})^{\top})\big)(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\eta_{r}}\big(\varrho(\frac{U_{r}}{\eta_{r}}+\frac{mU}{\eta})\big)_{r}-m\frac{\varrho_{r}U}{\eta\eta_{r}}\Big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

\displaystyle\nabla\rho^{\gamma}(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\varrho^{\gamma})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\big(\frac{(\varrho^{\gamma})_{r}}{\eta_{r}}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x)),

which, along with
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La}_{2}
and (
B.23
), leads to

(B.24)

(\rho\bm{u})_{t}+\dive(\rho\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u})+A\nabla\rho^{\gamma}=2\mu\dive(\rho D(\bm{u}))\qquad\text{in }\Omega(t).

Finally, since
\partial\Omega(t)=\{\bm{x}:\,\bm{x}=\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}),\,\bm{y}\in\partial\Omega\}
, for any
\bm{y}_{0}\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a unique point
\bm{x}_{0}\in\partial\Omega(t)
. Thus, we have

(B.25)

\mathcal{V}(\partial\Omega(t))|_{\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{0}}=\bm{\eta}_{t}(t,\cdot)\cdot\bm{N}|_{\bm{y}=\bm{y}_{0}}=\bm{U}(t,\cdot)\cdot\bm{N}|_{\bm{y}=\bm{y}_{0}}=(\bm{u}\cdot\bm{n})(t,\cdot)|_{\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{0}},

where
(\bm{N},\bm{n})
denote the exterior unit normal vector to
(\partial\Omega,\partial\Omega(t))
, respectively, that is,

(B.26)

\bm{N}=\bm{N}(\bm{y})=\frac{\bm{y}}{r},\qquad\bm{n}=\bm{N}(\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\frac{\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})}{|\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})|}=\frac{\bm{x}}{x}.

4.
We show that
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
is a solution described by (ii) of Theorem
2.3
. First, we can derive from Lemma
B.1
, (
2.1
),
and the regularity of
(U,\eta)
that

(B.27)

(\varrho,\varrho_{t},\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U},\bm{U}_{t},\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{\eta})\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\Omega}^{\sharp})),

where
\Omega^{\sharp}:=\{\bm{y}:\,\frac{1}{2}\leq|\bm{y}|<1\}
.

Next, to derive the time continuity of
(\varrho,\bm{U},\bm{\eta})
in
(0,T]\times\overline{\Omega}^{\flat}
(
\Omega^{\flat}:=\Omega\backslash\Omega^{\sharp}
), we can follow the same argument as in Step 6.1 of §
11.1.2
to derive

\bm{U}\in C((0,T];H^{3}(\Omega^{\flat})),\qquad(\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U},\bm{U}_{t})\in C((0,T];W^{1,4}(\Omega^{\flat})).

Then, by the classical Sobolev embeddings:

H^{3}(\Omega^{\flat})\hookrightarrow C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}^{\flat}),\qquad W^{1,4}(\Omega^{\flat})\hookrightarrow C(\overline{\Omega}^{\flat}),

it follows from (
2.1
) and the regularity of
(U,\eta)
that

(B.28)

(\varrho,\varrho_{t},\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U},\bm{U}_{t},\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{\eta})\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\Omega}^{\flat})).

Therefore, (
B.27
)–(
B.28
) lead to

(B.29)

(\varrho,\varrho_{t},\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{U},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{U},\bm{U}_{t},\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\eta},\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{2}\bm{\eta})\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\Omega})).

Finally, (
B.29
), together with the identity

\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}_{ij}}{\partial y_{k}}=-\sum_{p,\ell=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{ip}\frac{\partial^{2}\eta_{p}}{\partial y_{k}\partial y_{\ell}}\mathcal{A}_{\ell j}\qquad\text{for $k=1,\cdots\!,n$},

ensures the following regularity of
(\bm{\eta}_{*},\mathcal{A})
:

(B.30)

\bm{\eta}_{*}\in C(\mathbb{E}(T)),\qquad(\mathcal{A},\nabla\mathcal{A})\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\Omega})).

Consequently, it follows from the following relations:

\displaystyle\dot{\rho}(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle:=(\rho_{t}+\bm{u}\cdot\nabla\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})=\varrho_{t}(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{t}(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\bm{U}_{t}-\bm{U}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),

\displaystyle(\nabla\bm{u})(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\bm{U})(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),

\displaystyle(\partial_{j}\partial_{k}u_{i})(t,\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,\ell=1}^{n}\big(\mathcal{A}_{pj}\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}_{\ell k}}{\partial y_{p}}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial y_{\ell}}+\mathcal{A}_{pj}\frac{\partial^{2}U_{i}}{\partial y_{p}\partial y_{\ell}}\mathcal{A}_{\ell k}\big)(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x})),

and (
B.29
)–(
B.30
) that

(B.31)

(\rho,\dot{\rho},\bm{u},\nabla\bm{u},\nabla^{2}\bm{u},\bm{u}_{t})\in C(\mathbb{E}(T)),\qquad\partial\Omega(t)\in C^{2}((0,T]).

5.
We show that
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
is a solution described by (i) of Theorem
2.3
. Indeed, if moreover
\beta\leq 1
, then

(B.32)

\nabla\rho(t,\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\beta}(\varrho^{1-\beta}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}(\varrho^{\beta}))(t,\bm{\eta}_{*}(t,\bm{x}))=\frac{1}{\beta}\big(\varrho^{1-\beta}\frac{(\varrho^{\beta})_{r}}{\eta_{r}}\big)(t,\eta_{*}(t,x))\frac{\bm{x}}{x}.

Note that, since
(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})\in C((0,T];C^{1}(\bar{I}))
and

(B.33)

(\varrho^{\beta})_{r}=\frac{r^{m\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{\eta^{m\beta}\eta_{r}^{\beta}}+\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big(\frac{r^{m\beta}}{\eta^{m\beta}\eta_{r}^{\beta}}\big)_{r},

it is direct to show that
(\varrho^{\beta})_{r}\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I}))
. Moreover, from (
1.17
) and Lemma
A.4
, it follows that

\Big\|\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r}\Big\|_{1,1}\leq C\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r},(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r})_{r},(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r})_{rr}\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C,

which, along with Lemma
A.3
, yields that
\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}}{r}\in C(\bar{I})
. Hence, we see that
(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{r=0}=0
,
and we can obtain from

\eta_{rr}|_{r=0}=(\frac{\eta}{r})_{r}\Big|_{r=0}=0

and (
B.33
) that

(\varrho^{\beta})_{r}\in C((0,T];C(\bar{I})),\qquad(\varrho^{\beta})_{r}|_{r=0}=0,

which, along with (
B.32
), gives

(B.34)

|\nabla\rho|(t,\bm{0})=0,\qquad\ \nabla\rho(t,\bm{x})\in C(\mathbb{E}(T)).

Finally, it follows from the above, (
B.22
), and (
B.31
) that
\rho_{t}(t,\bm{x})\in C(\mathbb{E}(T))
. Hence,
(\rho(t,\bm{x}),\bm{u}(t,\bm{x}),\partial\Omega(t))
is a classical solution of
VFBP
(
1.1
) in
\overline{\mathbb{E}(T)}
.
∎

Appendix C
Remarks on the Energy Functionals and the Initial Condition

This appendix is devoted to giving some equivalent forms of the energy functionals, and the initial condition (
2.11
) in terms of
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
themselves and their spatial derivatives. In what follows, we always let
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
be the initial data of
IBVP
(
2.2
),
\rho_{0}
satisfy (
1.17
) for some
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\gamma-1]
, and
u_{0}
satisfy (
2.11
).

C.1.
Some equivalent forms of the energy functionals

Define

(C.1)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,f)

\displaystyle=\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(f,f_{r},\frac{f}{r},f_{t},f_{tr},\frac{f_{t}}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(f_{rr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r},f_{rrr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(f,f_{r},f_{t},f_{tr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(f_{rr},f_{rrr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2},

and

(C.2)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)

\displaystyle=\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U),

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(f_{tt},f_{trr},\big(\frac{f_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},f_{rrrr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr},\big(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{f}{r})_{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(f_{trr},f_{rrrr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}.

Clearly, we have

\mathring{\mathcal{X}}(0,f)=\mathcal{X}(0,f)\qquad\text{for $\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}},\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}$}.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
T>0
, and let
\eta
be defined by
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{2}
and
(\eta,U)
satisfy

(C.3)

\begin{gathered}(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})\in[\delta_{*},\delta^{*}],\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{E}(t,U)=\mathrm{E}_{1}<\infty,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U))+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s=\mathrm{E}_{2}<\infty\end{gathered}

for some given positive constants
(\delta_{*},\delta^{*},\mathrm{E}_{1},\mathrm{E}_{2})
. Assume that
f=f(t,r)
is a function defined on
[0,T]\times I
such that

(C.4)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\mathcal{E}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}(t,f))+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty.

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
F_{1}\sim F_{2}
denotes

C^{-1}e^{-CT}F_{1}\leq F_{2}\leq Ce^{CT}F_{1}

for some positive finite constant
C
, which depends only on
(\delta_{*},\delta^{*},\mathrm{E}_{1},\mathrm{E}_{2},n,\rho_{0},\beta,\varepsilon_{0})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we only show that

(C.5)

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

and the rest of this lemma can be proved analogously. Moreover, note that

(C.6)

f_{r}=\eta_{r}D_{\eta}f,\qquad f_{tr}=\eta_{r}D_{\eta}f_{t},

we directly obtain

\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(f,f_{r},\frac{f}{r},f_{t},f_{tr},\frac{f_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(f,f_{r},f_{t},f_{tr})\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

and thus we only need to focus on the estimates for the higher spatial derivatives of
f
.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Estimates for
\eta
.
First, according to
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{2}
, we have

(C.7)

\displaystyle\eta_{trr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}U+\eta_{r}^{2}D_{\eta}^{2}U,\qquad\eta_{trrr}=\eta_{rrr}D_{\eta}U+3\eta_{r}\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}^{2}U+\eta_{r}^{3}D_{\eta}^{3}U,

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{tr}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\frac{U}{\eta}+\eta_{r}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),\qquad\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{tr}=\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\frac{U}{\eta}+\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)^{2}\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{trr}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr}\frac{U}{\eta}+2\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{rr}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{r}^{2}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big).

On the other hand, it follows from (
C.3
) and Lemmas
A.3
–
A.4
that

(C.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\,\Big|\big(D_{\eta}U,\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}U,D_{\eta}^{2}U,\frac{U}{\eta},D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{1}\\
&\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{3}\Big(\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(D_{\eta}^{j}U,D_{\eta}^{j-1}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}\Big)\leq C,\end{aligned}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\,\Big|\big(D_{\eta}^{2}U,D_{\eta}(\frac{U}{\eta})\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{2}U,D_{\eta}^{3}U,D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{1}\\
&\leq C\sum_{j=2}^{4}\Big(\Big|\chi r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(D_{\eta}^{j}U,D_{\eta}^{j-1}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}D_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}\Big)\leq C\mathcal{D}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{aligned}

As a consequence, (
C.7
), together with (
C.3
), (
C.9
), and the Hölder inequality, implies that

(C.10)

\Big|\Big(\eta_{rr},\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq Ce^{Ct}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{2}U,D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\sqrt{t}e^{Ct},

and

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\eta_{rrr},\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Ct}\Big|\Big(\eta_{rr},\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{2}U,D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+Ce^{Ct}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{3}U,D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cte^{Ct}+Ce^{Ct}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{3}U,D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with the Minkowski inequality, leads to

(C.11)

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\eta_{rrr},\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cte^{Ct}+Cte^{Ct}\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cte^{Ct},

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\eta_{rrr}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cte^{Ct}+Cte^{Ct}\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cte^{Ct}.

2. Estimates for
f
.
Now, a direct calculation gives

\displaystyle f_{rr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}f+\eta_{r}^{2}D_{\eta}^{2}f,\qquad f_{rrr}=\eta_{rrr}D_{\eta}f+3\eta_{r}\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}^{2}f+\eta_{r}^{3}D_{\eta}^{3}f,

\displaystyle\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}

\displaystyle=\eta_{r}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r},\qquad\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}=\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)^{2}\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r},

\displaystyle\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+2\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{r}^{2}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr},

which, combined with (
C.10
), yields

(C.12)

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(f_{rr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Ct}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}f,\frac{f}{\eta},D_{\eta}^{2}f,D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|,

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(f_{rrr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Ct}\Big|\Big(D_{\eta}^{2}f,D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big),D_{\eta}^{3}f,D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big),\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big(\eta_{rrr},\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|\Big|\big(D_{\eta}f,\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big|.

Finally, repeating the same calculation (
C.8
), we have

\Big|\big(D_{\eta}f,\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\mathcal{E}(t,f).

Hence, (
C.12
), together with the above and (
C.11
), implies

(C.13)

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(f_{rr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r},f_{rrr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}(f_{rr},f_{rrr})\big|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f).

This completes the proof of Lemma
C.1
.
∎

C.2.
Some equivalent forms of the initial condition

First, according to the time evolution equation of
U
in (
2.2
), all the desired initial values of time derivatives of
U
in (
2.11
) can be completely expressed by those of
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
themselves and their spatial derivatives:

(C.14)

\displaystyle U_{t}(0,r)

\displaystyle=2\mu\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}+2\mu m(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r},

\displaystyle U_{tr}(0,r)

\displaystyle=2\mu\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}+m(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}.

First, we show that the initial condition (
2.11
) implicitly contains
the Neumann boundary condition of
u_{0}
.

Lemma C.2
.

If
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(0,U)<\infty
,
u_{0}
satisfies the Neumann boundary condition
(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(0,U)<\infty
, then
U_{t}(0,r)\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Due to the facts that

\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r,\qquad\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{2},\qquad\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta>\frac{1}{2},

and Lemma
A.4
, we have

(C.15)

\rho_{0}^{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}U_{t}(0)\in C\big[\frac{1}{2},1\big]\implies\rho_{0}^{\beta}U_{t}(0)|_{r=1}=0.

Similarly, since

\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\big((u_{0})_{rr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r},(u_{0})_{rrr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{rr}\big)\in L^{2}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

we also deduce

(C.16)

\rho_{0}^{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\beta}\big((u_{0})_{rr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)\in C\big[\frac{1}{2},1\big]\implies\rho_{0}^{\beta}(u_{0})_{rr}|_{r=1}=0,\quad\rho_{0}^{\beta}(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}|_{r=1}=0.

Now, multiplying
\eqref{116}_{1}
by
\rho_{0}^{\beta}
, we have

\frac{2\mu}{\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}(u_{0})_{r}=\rho_{0}^{\beta}U_{t}(0,r)-2\mu\rho_{0}^{\beta}\big((u_{0})_{r}+\frac{mu_{0}}{r}\big)_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{\beta}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}.

Then, taking the limit as
r\to 1
, we find that, by (
C.15
)–(
C.16
), the right-hand side of the above vanishes on boundary
\partial I
, which, along with the fact that
(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{r=1}\neq 0
, yields

(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0\implies(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0.

∎

Lemma C.3
.

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(0,U)<\infty
if and only if

(C.17)

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0,\qquad(u_{0})_{r}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{1-2\beta}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),\qquad u_{0}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{rrr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\beta}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),\qquad 2\mu\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big).

Moreover, if
\beta=\gamma-1
or, additionally,
\beta<\frac{2\gamma-1}{5}
, then the above can be reduced to

(C.18)

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0,\qquad(u_{0})_{r}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{1-2\beta}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),\qquad u_{0}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{rrr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\beta}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),\qquad\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big).

Proof.

The proof of (
C.18
) is straightforward, since

(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}=\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}\frac{\gamma-1-\beta}{\beta}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|^{2}+\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1-\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{rr},

and
(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}
belongs to
L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
whenever
\beta=\gamma-1
or
\beta<\frac{2\gamma-1}{5}
.

Therefore, it suffices to prove (
C.17
). We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
We first prove the necessity. Assume that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(0,U)<\infty
. Then we have

(C.19)

(u_{0},U_{t}(0))\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),\qquad(u_{0})_{rr}\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\beta}},

and
(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0
follows from Lemma
C.2
.

Next, we show that
\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}(u_{0})_{r}\in L^{2}(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Thanks to (
C.19
) and

(u_{0})_{r}|_{r=1}=0,\qquad\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r,\qquad\rho_{0}^{\beta}\in H^{3}(\frac{1}{2},1),\qquad\beta>\frac{1}{3},

multiplying
\eqref{116}_{1}
by
\rho_{0}
and integrating over
[r,1]
with
r\in[\frac{1}{2},1]
, we obtain from Lemma
A.4
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle 2\mu\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}(u_{0})_{r}\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|-A\rho_{0}^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}-\beta}+\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\beta}\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}\big(U_{t}(0,\tilde{r})-2\mu m\frac{(u_{0})_{r}}{\tilde{r}}+2\mu m\frac{u_{0}}{\tilde{r}^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C+C\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\beta}{2}}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}(u_{0},(u_{0})_{r},U_{t}(0))\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C+C\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\beta}{2}}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{3}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}+2\beta}\partial_{r}^{j}u_{0}\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}+\beta}(U_{t},U_{tr})(0)\big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C+C\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\beta}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(0,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\beta}{2}}\in L^{2}(\frac{1}{2},1)
, we derive from the above that
\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}(u_{0})_{r}\in L^{2}(\frac{1}{2},1)
.

Consequently, based on this, we can obtain from
\eqref{116}_{1}
that

U_{t}(0)\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)\implies\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}\in L^{2}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)\implies\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{0})_{rr}\in L^{2}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

and thus obtain from
\eqref{116}_{2}
that

(C.20)

\displaystyle U_{tr}(0)\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)

\displaystyle\implies 2\mu\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}+m(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

\displaystyle\implies 2\mu\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big).

This completes the proof of necessity.

2.
We prove the sufficiency. Assume that (
C.17
) holds. It suffices to show that

(C.21)

U_{t}(0)\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big).

U_{t}(0)\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)
is obvious under (
C.17
).
Moreover, we obtain from
\eqref{116}_{2}
that

(C.22)

U_{tr}(0)\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)\iff(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{rr}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big)\iff u_{0}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big).

This completes the proof of sufficiency.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(0,U)<\infty
if and only if

(C.23)

\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{0},(u_{0})_{r},\frac{u_{0}}{r},(u_{0})_{rr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r},(u_{0})_{rrr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{rr},\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)\in L^{2}.

Proof.

In fact, we only need to show

(C.24)

\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(U_{t},U_{tr},\frac{U_{t}}{r}\big)(0)\in L^{2}.

Indeed, it follows from (
C.14
) and Lemma
B.1
that

\displaystyle|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{t}(0)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((u_{0})_{rr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0})_{r}|_{2}(|\zeta(u_{0})_{r}|_{\infty}+1)\big)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(0,U),

\displaystyle|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}U_{tr}(0)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\big(\big|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((u_{0})_{rrr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{rr}\big)\big|_{2}+|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty}|\zeta r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{0})_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{rr}|_{2}(|\zeta(u_{0})_{r}|_{\infty}+1)

\displaystyle+C|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{2}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{\infty}(|\zeta(u_{0})_{r}|_{\infty}+1)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(0,U),

\displaystyle|\zeta r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}U_{t}(0)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\zeta r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\big((u_{0})_{rr},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|_{2}(|\zeta(u_{0})_{r}|_{\infty}+1)\big)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(0,U).

This completes the proof.
∎

Finally, based on Lemmas
C.3
–
C.4
, we give one remark to show that the examples of the initial data given by (
2.17
)–(
2.18
) in Remark
2.3
satisfy the initial assumptions (
1.17
) and (
2.11
) in Theorem
2.1
.

Remark C.1
.

First, due to
Lemma
B.1
, the initial assumption (
1.17
) is nothing but (
B.8
) in
Appendix
B
, that is,

\rho_{0}^{\beta}(\bm{y})\in H^{3}(\Omega),\quad\,\,\mathcal{K}_{1}(1-|\bm{y}|)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq\rho_{0}(\bm{y})\leq\mathcal{K}_{2}(1-|\bm{y}|)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $\bm{y}\in\overline{\Omega}$},

for some constants
\mathcal{K}_{2}>\mathcal{K}_{1}>0
and
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\gamma-1]
. Hence,
\rho_{0}(\bm{y})
given in (
2.17
) takes the form
:

\rho_{0}^{\beta}(\bm{y})=1-|\bm{y}|^{2k}\qquad{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}},

thus satisfies the condition above.

Next we check that
\bm{u}_{0}
given in (
2.18
) satisfies (
2.11
). Note that, in the interior domain
\Omega^{\flat}:=\{\bm{y}:\,|\bm{y}|<\frac{1}{2}\}
, from
Lemmas
B.1
and
C.4
, we only need to ensure that
\bm{u}_{0}\in H^{3}(\Omega^{\flat})
, which is, of course, satisfied by
\bm{u}_{0}
given in (
2.18
).

While in the exterior domain
\Omega^{\sharp}:=\Omega\backslash\Omega^{\flat}
, when
\beta\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{2\gamma-1}{5})
or
\beta=\gamma-1
, we can easily to show that
\bm{u}_{0}
given in
\eqref{initial-ex2}_{1}
satisfies (
C.18
) in
Lemma
C.3
. Hence, it still remains to show that
\bm{u}_{0}
given in
\eqref{initial-ex2}_{2}
satisfies (
C.17
), or equivalently, show that

(C.25)

u_{0}(r)=-\frac{A}{2\mu}\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}+\tilde{u}_{0}(r)\qquad\text{satisfies \eqref{con-b3} when $\beta\in\big[\frac{2\gamma-1}{5},\gamma-1\big)$.}

Indeed, a direct calculation gives that

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{A}{2\mu}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}+(\tilde{u}_{0})_{r},

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{rr}

\displaystyle=\frac{A}{2\mu}\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1-\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}+(\tilde{u}_{0})_{rr},

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{rrr}

\displaystyle=\frac{A}{2\mu}\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}\big(\frac{\gamma-1-\beta}{\beta}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1-2\beta}|(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{r}|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1-\beta}(\rho_{0}^{\beta})_{rr}\big)+(\tilde{u}_{0})_{rrr},

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{A\gamma}{2\mu(\gamma-1)}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr}+\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(\tilde{u}_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}.

Then, based on the facts that

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}\big[\frac{1}{2},1\big),\quad\rho_{0}^{\beta}\sim 1-r,\quad\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)\beta+\gamma-1-2\beta>0,\quad\gamma-\frac{1}{2}-\beta>0,

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}(\rho_{0}(\tilde{u}_{0})_{r})_{r}\big)_{r}=\big(\frac{(\rho_{0})_{r}}{\rho_{0}}\big)_{r}(\tilde{u}_{0})_{r}+\frac{(\rho_{0})_{r}}{\rho_{0}}(\tilde{u}_{0})_{rr}+(\tilde{u}_{0})_{rrr}\text{ is compactly supported in $\big[\frac{1}{2},1\big)$},

we obtain that (
C.25
) holds.

Appendix D
Cross-Derivatives Embedding

The following embedding theorem is used to obtain the higher-order elliptic estimates.

Proposition D.1
.

Assume that
\varphi=\varphi(r)
is a function defined on
I
and satisfies

(D.1)

\varphi\in C^{1}(\bar{I})\cap C^{2}((0,1]),\qquad\frac{1}{K}(1-r)\leq\varphi\leq K(1-r)\ \ \text{for some $K>1$}.

Let
(b,c)
be two parameters such that

(D.2)

\frac{1}{2}<b\leq\frac{c+1}{2},

and let
f=f(r)\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
satisfy both
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2q}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
for some
q\in[b,\frac{c+1}{2}]
and

(D.3)

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}<\infty.

Then, for any
\varphi
satisfying (
D.1
) and
(b,c)
satisfying (
D.2
), there exists a constant
C>0
, which depends only on
(\varphi,b,c)
, such that, for all
f
satisfying (
D.3
),

(D.4)

|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}\big).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Case
f\in C^{\infty}([\frac{1}{2},1])
.

It follows from integration by parts, (
D.1
)–(
D.3
), Lemma
A.5
, and the Young inequality that

(D.5)

\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} |\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}^{2}&=\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}-c^{2}|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f|_{2}^{2}-2c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&=\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+c\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\quad+\underline{(2b-1-c)c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{\,\leq 0}-\underline{c(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}f^{2}\Big|_{r=0}^{r=1}}_{\,=0}\\
&\leq\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+C\big(|(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi^{-1}\varphi_{r}|_{\infty}|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}^{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi_{rr}|_{\infty}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\\
&\leq\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+C|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}^{2},\end{aligned}

which yields (
D.4
).

2. Case
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2b}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
In this case, we can repeat the calculation in (
D.5
) to derive (
D.4
), except for justifying the following integral equality:

(D.6)

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+(2b-1)\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Indeed, thanks to Lemma
A.1
, there exists a sequence
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}([\frac{1}{2},1])
such that

(D.7)

|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}(f^{\varepsilon}_{r}-f_{r})|_{2}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$},

which, along with Lemma
A.4
, yields

(D.8)

|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-1}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-\frac{1}{2}}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)\big|_{\infty}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Hence, according to (
D.7
)–(
D.8
) and integration by parts for
f^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}(f^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+(2b-1)\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}(f^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
implies that (
D.6
) holds for all
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2b}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
.

This completes the proof of (
D.4
) when
q=b
.

3. General case.

It suffices to establish (
D.4
) when (
D.3
) holds and

b<\frac{c+1}{2},\qquad q=\frac{c+1}{2},\qquad\ f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

due to the fact that
H^{1}_{\varphi^{2q}}(\frac{1}{2},1)\subset H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
if
q\leq\frac{c+1}{2}
. Note that, in this case, integration by parts in (
D.6
) fails owing to
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\notin L^{1}
.

To overcome this difficulty, set

\vartheta:=\frac{c+1}{2}-b,\qquad\varphi_{j}:=\varphi+\frac{1}{j}\quad\text{for $j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

We first show a variant of (
A.1
) in Lemma
A.5
, that is, for all
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
,

(D.9)

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big).

Based on Lemma
A.1
and the proof in Lemma
A.5
, it suffices to show that (
D.9
) holds for
f\in C^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Clearly, we can further let
(\varphi,\varphi_{j})=(d,d_{j})
with
d=d(r):=1-r
and
d_{j}:=d+\frac{1}{j}
, due to

\frac{d}{K}\leq\varphi\leq Kd,\qquad\frac{d_{j}}{K}\leq\varphi_{j}\leq Kd_{j}.

It follows from the above reductions, integration by parts, and
\mathrm{d}(d^{c+1})_{r}=(c+1)d^{c}\mathrm{d}r
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{c+1}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\vartheta}{c+1}\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{c+1}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\vartheta}{c+1}\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r,

which implies

(D.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{b}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}d^{\frac{c+1}{2}}d_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}d^{\frac{c+1}{2}}d_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}d^{\frac{c+1}{2}}d_{j}^{-\vartheta}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big).

This completes the proof of (
D.9
).

Now, we continue to prove (
D.4
). It follows from (
D.3
) and Lemma
A.4
that

(D.11)

Q_{j}:=\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}(\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}f_{r}+c\varphi^{\frac{c-1}{2}}\varphi_{r}f)\in L^{2}\qquad\text{for any $j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

Using a density argument similar to that in Step 2, we can show that the following integral equality still holds,
i.e.
, for all
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
and
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{c}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{c}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\big(c\varphi^{c-1}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta}-2\vartheta\varphi^{c}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta-1}\big)(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Hence, based on the above equality and the calculation similar to (
D.5
), we deduce from (
D.11
) and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=|Q_{j}|_{2}^{2}-c^{2}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c-1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}\varphi_{r}f\big|_{2}^{2}-2c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{c}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=|Q_{j}|_{2}^{2}+c\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{c}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\underline{-2\vartheta c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{c-1}\varphi_{j}^{-2\vartheta-1}(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{\,\leq 0}

\displaystyle\leq|Q_{j}|_{2}^{2}+C\big(|(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi^{-1}\varphi_{r}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi_{rr}|_{\infty}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}\big),

which, along with (
D.1
) and (
D.9
), gives

\displaystyle\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|Q_{j}|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}\big).

Since
C
is independent of
j
, we can extract a subsequence (still denoted by
j
) such that

(D.12)

\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\to g\qquad\text{weakly in }L^{2}\quad\text{as }j\to\infty,

for some limit function
g\in L^{2}
, and

|g|_{2}\leq\liminf_{j\to\infty}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}\big).

Note that
f_{r}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem also gives

(D.13)

\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\to\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}\qquad\text{in }L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\ \ \text{as }j\to\infty.

Hence, by (
D.12
)–(
D.13
) and the uniqueness of the limits, we have
g=\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
D.1
.
∎
